# Technical PRD — Voice AI Mock Case Interview (Feature 1, Build Spec)

**Companion to:** `prd-voice-ai-mock-case.md` (product PRD — source of *what* and *why*)
**This document:** the engineering build spec for an AI coding agent (Claude Code). It pins down stack, data model, API surface, orchestration logic, repo structure, and build order.
**Status:** Draft v1, updated through interviewer-behavior v4.3 (2026-09-29) · **Target:** MVP validation build · **Scale:** tens of pilot users (consulting clubs), bursty seasonal concurrency.

> Read this alongside the product PRD. Where the product PRD states a requirement (FR-N), this doc says how to build it. The product PRD wins on intent; this doc wins on implementation detail. If they conflict, flag it — don't silently pick one.

> **Companion normative docs** (added as the build matured; this PRD points to them rather than duplicating):
> - `docs/interviewer-behavior.md` — the authored interviewer conduct spec (Rules 1–19, incl. the conduct/wellbeing track; currently v4.3, with an implementation-status register in Part V). Governs §5.4.
> - `docs/scoring-qa.md` — judge, report-verification pipeline, and scoring-attribution requirements (implemented/pending marked). Governs §6.
> - `docs/case-authoring.md` — case-content authoring + the ledger-consistency QA gate. Governs §10.

---

## 0. The one decision that shapes the whole build

**Build the text case end-to-end before any voice infrastructure exists.** The entire case state machine, data-gating, interviewer agent, scoring engine, and feedback report MUST run and be testable over **typed input** with zero STT/TTS/WebRTC in the stack. Voice (M2) is a transport layer that wraps the proven text core — not a prerequisite.

Concretely: the orchestrator's interface to the candidate is an abstract `CandidateChannel` (sends interviewer text, receives candidate text + control signals). The text build implements it with an HTTP/WebSocket text channel. The voice build implements the *same interface* with the LiveKit voice pipeline. **Nothing in the orchestrator, agent, or scoring code may import a voice library.**

This is the single most important constraint in the document. It de-risks the expensive work (the feedback engine — "the rubric is the product") before the realism work (voice).

---

## 1. Stack

| Layer | Choice | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Framework | **Next.js (App Router)** | Server actions for mutations; route handlers for streaming/voice endpoints. |
| Hosting | **Vercel** | Note: long-lived voice sessions don't belong on Vercel serverless — see §8.4. |
| DB + Auth | **Supabase** (Postgres + Supabase Auth) | Email + **club code** gating. RLS on all candidate-owned tables. |
| ORM / DB access | Supabase client; **`drizzle`** for typed schema + migrations (agent's choice if it prefers Supabase migrations directly — flag the decision). |
| Styling | Tailwind + a component lib of the agent's choice (shadcn/ui acceptable). Keep mid-case UI minimal (§10 product PRD). |
| Live interviewer LLM | **Claude Sonnet 5.5** (`claude-sonnet-5-5`), thinking off (`between_tools`), structured JSON turn, streamed | Since 12 Oct (batch 20: Sonnet 20.7 D1–D9 faults per 100 turns vs Haiku 5.5's 32.7). Server-side refusal fallback on (`fallbacks: "default"`); request settings follow the model via `interviewerRequestOptions`. Haiku 5.5 (thinking disabled, effort medium, no fallback — it has none) stays selectable: `INTERVIEWER_PROVIDER=anthropic-haiku55-none-medium`. Evaluations name an explicit config (`INTERVIEWER_EVAL_CONFIG` / `persona-batch --interviewer=`): `sonnet55-baseline`, `haiku55-thinking-off` or `haiku55-effort-low`; a run answered by another model fails. Since 6 Oct the model returns one JSON turn — `{say, move, requests, exhibit, rescue_item, question}` — and makes no data, phase or ending decisions (§5.3). Swappable behind the `InterviewerModel` interface; ids in `lib/models.ts`. Screened and not adopted (7–8 Oct; adapters kept behind switches): Cerebras gpt-oss-120b, GPT-6 Luna and Sol, GPT-6.1 Sol, Qwen 3.8 27B, Gemini 3.8 Flash — results in §16 (15 Oct, body trim). History: Haiku 4.5 → Opus 4.8 (July 2026, missed live math errors) → Sonnet 5.5 (Oct, speed and cost) → Haiku 5.5 (11 Oct, latency) → Sonnet 5.5 (12 Oct, quality); the deterministic math backstops now carry most of the math-catching load — watch batch results for regressions. |
| Scoring/judge LLM | **Claude Opus 5.5** (`claude-opus-5-5`), adaptive thinking, effort `high` | Runs once at case end; latency-insensitive; this is the product. Three Opus passes: judge, claim verifier, dimension reconciliation (§6). Oct 2026: moved from Opus 4.8. Server-side refusal fallback on. |
| Background LLM passes | Per role in `lib/models.ts` (`BACKGROUND_MODEL_ID`): **Claude Haiku 5.5** (`claude-haiku-5-5`, thinking disabled, effort medium) for the pressure-test judges and the C5 distress check since 8 Oct; **Claude Haiku 4.5** for coverage, the data-request classifier and the hint check | **Haiku 5.5 migration (8 Oct; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-haiku-5-5-background-migration.md`):** each role switched only after a targeted regression check on fixed saved inputs. Judges: fewer false unlocks than 4.5. Distress: same fires on 271 real messages. Hint check failed (stays 4.5). Data-request: unparseable without a schema; with an enforced schema it parses but over-maps asks to ledger items (stays 4.5). Coverage: same end gate, different mid-case steer (stays 4.5). Live coverage agent and Rule 11 data-request classifier run after the response (`after()`). In parallel with every interviewer call: the C5 distress check (v4.6) — it adds latency only when it outlasts the model's first sentence (batch 11: verdict at 0.7–0.95s, never waited on). Same-turn data-request detection was removed from model turns on 6 Oct (the model declares the requests; it fed only a log-only audit). Synchronous: recommendation-ask turns classify the current message (~1s), and turns where a hint's delivery rests on a question run the hint check — count both against the M2 latency budget. |
| STT | **Deepgram Flux** (`flux-general-en`, streaming, model end-of-turn) | M0 Phase A (6–7 Oct): Flux over Nova-3 — Nova-3's default 300ms endpointing ended the turn mid-answer on 7/10 long answers, Flux on 3/41 (all at thinking pauses). Behind `STTProvider` (`lib/voice/deepgram.ts`, both adapters). STT mishears case names ("Brew & Bean" → "brewing bean") — keyterms per case are open. Settings: end-of-turn 0.7 / eager 0.5; a 7 Oct synthetic sweep found no setting both faster and safer — human recordings decide any change (§8.5). |
| TTS | **Cartesia** (Sonic 3.6 since 18 Oct — 3.5 before — voice 533b2990 by default, `CARTESIA_VOICE_ID` overrides; Lorenzo's pick by ear; one WebSocket context per interviewer turn) | M0 Phase A: first audio ~125–155ms after a segment. Behind `TTSProvider` (`lib/voice/cartesia.ts`). Free tier exhausted during the spike; the pilot needs Pro (commercial use). Deepgram Aura used for the listening demo (`scripts/voice-demo.ts`) — per-segment REST calls reset the voice between sentences; Cartesia's per-turn context avoids that. |
| Voice transport / orchestration | **LiveKit Agents** | WebRTC, VAD/endpointing, barge-in. **Caveat:** data-gating lives in OUR orchestrator, not LiveKit's loop — see §8. Not yet tested: M0 Phase B (transport, mic, barge-in) not started. |

**Supabase keys (non-negotiable):**
- Client-side: `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` (publishable key).
- Server-only: `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` — never behind `NEXT_PUBLIC_` or any client-exposed var.
- Use the new publishable/secret keys, **not** legacy anon/service_role JWTs.

**All vendor choices marked spike-pending are swappable behind interfaces.** The M0 spike validates latency on the cascade before committing. If the spike fails the ≤1.5s gate, the interface boundary is where you swap providers — no orchestrator rewrite.

---

## 2. System architecture

```
┌─────────────┐     CandidateChannel (abstract)      ┌──────────────────┐
│  Candidate  │◄────────────────────────────────────►│   Orchestrator   │
│  (browser)  │   text channel  OR  voice pipeline    │  (session state, │
└─────────────┘                                        │  data ledger,    │
                                                       │  phase logic,    │
   TEXT BUILD (M1):  WebSocket text channel            │  action exec)    │
   VOICE BUILD (M2): LiveKit ⟷ Deepgram/Cartesia       └────────┬─────────┘
                                                                │
                                                    per-turn    │  declarations
                                                    prompt      │  + words
                                                                ▼
                                                       ┌──────────────────┐
                                                       │ Interviewer LLM  │
                                                       │  (Sonnet 5.5,    │
                                                       │   JSON turn)     │
                                                       └──────────────────┘

   case end ──► Scoring service (Opus 5.5 judge/verifier/reconciliation + deterministic checks) ──► Report
```

**Core principle:** the orchestrator is the source of truth and the only thing that touches the data ledger. The LLM never sees a number it isn't allowed to reveal yet (see §5). The candidate channel is swappable; the orchestrator is not.

---

## 3. Repository structure (proposed — agent may refine, flag changes)

```
/app                      Next.js App Router
  /(marketing)            landing, club-code entry
  /case/[sessionId]       live case UI (text channel for M1, voice for M2)
  /case/[sessionId]/report  feedback report
  /api
    /session              create/advance/end session (route handlers)
    /channel              text/voice channel endpoints
/lib
  /orchestrator           session state machine, phase logic, action executor
    state-machine.ts      INTRO→…→SCORING transitions
    data-ledger.ts        reveal gating; the ONLY module that discloses numbers
    channel.ts            CandidateChannel abstract interface
    session-runner.ts     one candidate turn end to end (runTurn): reads → plan → stream → settle → commit
    plan-turn.ts          Plan stage: every pre-model decision, the turn kind (§5.2), no writes
    stream-turn.ts        Stream stage: delivers say sentences + code's data line as the model writes (vetoReason)
    speculation.ts, speculative-turn.ts   speculative turn on Flux's eager signal — DraftGate, plan fingerprint, startDraft. IMPLEMENTED, DISABLED: no live caller (§8.2)
    prompt-context.ts     the interviewer's per-turn prompt context from a plan (shared by the runner and the speculation fingerprint)
    pressure-test.ts      Rule 7 pressure-test state (not_asked/awaiting/satisfied/waived), probe detection, the Haiku answer judge, gate scope, bounded fallback questions (8 Oct); v3 reply bound, re-ask / narrowed probe / waiver, probe-as-heard for the judge (10 Oct)
    request-signal.ts     guard B's explicit-ask detector (phrasing; precision-tuned) and the sentences it keeps for request_signal events; addressedAskSentences / matchAskDetections — which classifier detections a code-written turn answers (9 Oct)
    settle-turn.ts        Settle stage: composes the turn, books reveals, derives phase, one commit
    turn-record.ts        TurnRecorder — one evaluation record per turn (exact model requests, raw replies, state before, check verdicts, timed delivery, recorder errors); filled only when runTurn gets onTurnRecord (evaluation runs), production passes none (9 Oct)
    data-decisions.ts     Rule 11 decided in code from the model's declared requests; renders every data line
    turn-data.ts          one turn's data decisions (shared by Stream and Settle)
    progress.ts           phase and stage administration from declared moves (Rule 8, §5.2)
    data-requests.ts      Rule 11 data-request classifier (Haiku) + open-request / force-release helpers
    post-turn.ts          background passes after each turn (coverage agent, data-request audit)
    start-session.ts      session creation + deterministic opening turn
    numeric-provenance.ts provenance audit + enforcement (blocked sentences withheld, Rule 6)
    recompute.ts          live math check → probe/correct hint + Rule 14 attempt counter; flags of kind step / unit / arithmetic / pricing with the claim (quantity, units, base, timeframe); `checkCandidateMath` is the one flags-and-stamps computation the live turn and scoring share (13 Oct)
    arithmetic-check.ts   stated operations verified exactly: correct / incorrect / unclear (v4.10)
    unit-check.ts         nested-percentage conversions checked numerically against the case's shareBases (v4.10; the keyword detector is gone); a conversion is checked at the base the candidate states, and one right on that base with a share of another year is a period question, not a flag (15 Oct)
    math-checker.ts       background math checker (13 Oct): a model checks each candidate message with a quantitative claim against the full case; per-claim verdicts; its probe and pointer probe vetted for FR-4 in code; corrections written by code from its own computation
    math-checker-live.ts  the checker in the live turn (13 Oct; wired, not yet run live): started in Plan, never awaited, its verdict a session event read by later turns as a Rule 14 flag (stale / dedupe / expiry, one a turn, the close hold)
    math-talk.ts          reads whether an interviewer sentence doubts the candidate's math or asks for the working (v4.10)
    conduct.ts            C1–C5 conduct classifier (Rule 17) — regex floor; ambiguous C3 stems → "confirm" (v4.10)
    conduct-model.ts      Rule 17 model layer: the C3 backstop and tiebreaker, beside the interviewer call (v4.10)
    distress.ts           C5 model layer (Haiku, parallel with the interviewer call); after a declined offer, judges only a new or worse signal (v4.10)
    candidate-exit.ts     Rule 18A: stop requests, bare sign-offs, refusals to start, the reply to "do you want to stop?" (v4.10)
    session-end.ts, session-outcome.ts   the End button and the shared "report or not" rule (v4.10)
    stage-evidence.ts     the stage record: delivered + outcome per stage; owed stages before a normal end (Rule 9 v4.10)
    probe-guard.ts        doubt on the candidate's math only with a flag; explain probes once per figure (Rule 2 v4.5 → v4.10)
    assumption-guard.ts   withholds challenges to assumptions about requested data (Rule 11 v4.5)
    timeframe-check.ts    cross-period arithmetic in interviewer speech, log-only (Rule 6 v4.6; the candidate side is unit-check's period flag)
    hint-check.ts         model check that a question-based rung was really a hint (Rule 13)
    check-log.ts          per-turn decision log for every check (Part V v4.6)
    silence.ts, spoken-close.ts, stall.ts, pacing.ts   silence/pause, one-goodbye close, stall ladder, time budgets + grace ask
  /agent
    interviewer.ts        per-turn prompt assembly, catalog id resolver, the streamed turn
    prompts/              system prompt, anti-hallucination, anti-jailbreak; system-compact.ts — compact-prompt A/B arm, not on the live path (7 Oct)
    models/               InterviewerModel interface + Anthropic impl (Sonnet 5.5; ids in lib/models.ts); turn-schema.ts, turn-stream.ts; factory.ts picks the provider (INTERVIEWER_PROVIDER), cerebras.ts kept behind it, unused (7 Oct)
    opener.ts             Haiku-written opener — experiment, replay arm only, not on the live path (7 Oct)
  /scoring
    judge.ts              Opus rubric evaluation over full transcript
    deterministic.ts      math-tolerance checks, data-leak audit (NOT the LLM)
    report.ts             assembles the 8-dimension report + model answer
    score-session.ts      the full scoring pipeline (§6), shared by the score route and scripts
    transcript-artifacts.ts, evidence-audit.ts, verifier.ts, reconcile.ts   report verification (§6)
    data-coverage.ts      requested-vs-never-requested data split for the judge (Rule 11)
    math-spans.ts         source spans for math checks, shared by live recompute and scoring (Rules 2/3)
    interviewer-errors.ts interviewer-error + wellbeing marks from the event log (Rule 3, 17-C5)
    caveat-floor.ts       caveated dimensions: meets_bar only on remaining evidence, else not assessed (Rule 9 v4.10)
    challenge-marks.ts    delivered doubts → confirmed_interviewer_error / unsupported_challenge; claims code settled, for the judge (Rule 3 v4.10)
    stage-caps.ts         the stage record for the judge; a dimension whose stage never happened is rated on evidence or not assessed; partial reports (Rule 9 v4.10)
    answer-key-pass.ts    no missing-answer-key-idea weaknesses; stage faults under not-run caveats (Rule 3 v4.5)
    strong-gate.ts        "strong" only on an evidenced strong-anchor checklist; overall capped
  models.ts               every model id + the server-side fallback setting; background classifier ids by role + Haiku 5.5 request settings (backgroundRequest)
  llm-pricing.ts          one price table by exact model id (cache, Haiku 5.5 long-prompt tier, Cerebras qwen-3.8-27b for screening); unknown ids throw — never $0 (8 Oct)
  anthropic-client.ts, llm-meter.ts, llm-budget.ts   the one Anthropic client; its fetch meter checks a run budget before each call and records usage from the response (JSON and SSE, aborted streams estimated); LLM_BUDGET_USD / LLM_BUDGET_FILE (8 Oct); optional extra cap LLM_EXTRA_BUDGET_USD + LLM_EXTRA_BUDGET_FILE, checked and recorded beside the main one — a targeted check inside a batch (10 Oct)
  number-words.ts         spoken numbers → digits for the stall signal and math spans
  /eval                   persona-batch evaluation (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-persona-batch-evaluation-design.md; dev only, never imported by app/):
                          turn-file.ts (<run>.turns.jsonl.gz: header, TurnRecords, end line), git-info.ts (commit + code fingerprint),
                          batch-manifest.ts (waves per batch, run numbering, freeze on code fingerprint + model ids + bands/severity hashes, versions, reruns; batch 18 frozen; per-run requested/returned interviewer model),
                          model-guard.ts (the declared evaluation interviewer; requested vs returned model per call; unpriced/unknown/mismatch fails the run) (v4.10),
                          wave-commands.ts, scorecard.ts (one row per interview: completion, stages, loops, code flags, opportunities, latency, cost, ratings, evidence),
                          prescreen.ts (Opus fault proposals, verbatim-quote check), review-sheet.ts + headline.ts (human confirmation, batch headline), bands.ts (reports vs persona bands)
  /voice                  STTProvider/TTSProvider (types.ts), Deepgram Flux/Nova-3 (deepgram.ts), Cartesia (cartesia.ts), segment → speech (speak.ts), instant acknowledgment (acknowledge.ts; the thinking filler was removed 7 Oct), playback scheduler (playback.ts), end-of-turn clip variants + signal scoring (endpoint.ts), PCM helpers (pcm.ts) — M0 Phase A; LiveKit glue is Phase B
  /cases                  case JSON loader + schema validation (zod)
/cases                    human-authored case content (JSON, version-controlled)
/db                       drizzle schema + migrations
/tests                    QA harness (incl. 50-case hallucination run); tests/replay/ re-runs the deterministic checks over saved persona runs (free; skips without them)
/scripts                  live-run.ts — end-to-end run with a simulated candidate against real models + DB (dev only; --finish re-scores a saved session);
                          regrade.ts — dry-run re-grade of stored sessions; eval-distress.ts — C5 model-layer eval (all cost money);
                          voice and latency experiments: voice-latency.ts, voice-demo.ts, endpoint-sweep.ts, replay-output-format.ts (cost money);
                          useful-latency.ts, playback-sim.ts (free — read saved runs);
                          evaluation: live-run --script / --seed / --seed-through / --max-turns (scripted and saved-prefix replays, no Opus),
                          guard-check.ts, review-dump.ts, request-signal-check.ts, plan-inspect.ts (free — read saved runs);
                          eval-probe-judge.ts, eval-background.ts (per-role Haiku 5.5 regression checks on fixed saved inputs; --dry estimates cost free);
                          every paid script requires LLM_BUDGET_USD (lib/llm-budget.ts);
                          persona batch (9 Oct): persona-batch.ts — one wave of ≤10 personas in parallel (cost money; --dry free; needs --env-file=.env.local);
                          batch-scorecard.ts, batch-review-sheet.ts (--write/--apply), batch-bands.ts (free); batch-prescreen.ts (Opus, cost money, resumable)
/evals                    frozen batch-evaluation inputs: severity.md (fault severity definitions), persona-bands.json (expected rating per persona × dimension)
```

The `/voice` directory must not be imported by `/lib/orchestrator`, `/lib/agent`, or `/lib/scoring`. Enforce with a lint rule (e.g. `eslint-plugin-boundaries`) so M1 stays voice-free by construction.

## 4. Data model (Supabase / Postgres)

RLS on every candidate-owned row: a candidate reads/writes only their own sessions. Cases and exhibits are readable by authenticated users; never expose `data_ledger` answer values or `*_key` fields to the client (see §7 — these stay server-side).

```
users                  (Supabase Auth)
  id, email, club_code, created_at

cases                  -- human-authored, version-controlled in /cases, synced to DB
  id (text, e.g. "prof-001"), title, firm_style, difficulty,
  prompt, content_jsonb (full case schema), version, active

sessions
  id (uuid), user_id (fk), case_id (fk),
  phase (enum), elapsed_ms, phase_started_at,
  status (active|completed|abandoned|terminated), abandon_phase,
  started_at, completed_at,
  coverage_jsonb         -- per-dimension live coverage (0-100 evidence, NOT
                         --   quality) from the background coverage agent;
                         --   steers the interviewer + gates an early close
  flags_jsonb            -- orchestrator run-state. Booleans: stalled, ran_long,
                         --   asked_repeat, off_topic_count, advanced_last_turn
                         --   (gates the no-back-to-back behavior shift),
                         --   time_warning_fired, load_shed_logged,
                         --   grace_ask_fired (Rule 12 time-up ask, v4.3).
                         -- Sub-objects: stall{} (stall-ladder state, Rule 13),
                         --   conduct{ warnings, distress_offered,
                         --   distress_offered_at_ms (C5 clock pause), category }
                         --   (Rule 17), silence{} (check-in / technical-pause
                         --   state, Rules 13/16/19), recompute_attempts{ step_id:
                         --   n } (Rule 14 attempt counter, v4.3).
                         -- (pushback_done is vestigial — the "push back once"
                         --   nag was removed; general rigor rules cover it.)

session_turns
  id, session_id (fk), turn_index, role (interviewer|candidate),
  text, timestamp_ms, latency_ms (nullable, voice),
  transcript_confidence (nullable, STT)

revealed_data
  id, session_id (fk), ledger_item_id, revealed_at_ms
  -- the data-disclosure trail; supports scoring + prevents re-ask confusion

exhibits_shown
  id, session_id (fk), exhibit_id, shown_at_ms

session_events         -- typed log; three categories share one table
  id, session_id (fk), category (intervention|conduct|data_request), subtype,
  turn_index, phase, payload_jsonb, created_at
  -- 'intervention': stall-ladder assists (restate_anchor|narrow_frame|
  --    directive_rescue), synthesis_unresolved, load_shed — SCORING inputs
  --    ("assisted ≠ covered" and time-pressure coverage caveats); also
  --    silence_check_in, technical_pause(_expired), session_resumed,
  --    recompute_flag (payload: step, candidate value, span, attempt — v4.3),
  --    grace_ask (v4.3). Only ladder rungs count as assists.
  -- 'conduct': C1–C5 conduct events (Rule 17), plus C2_excluded (quoted /
  --    reported / generic-you speech, logged not warned — v4.3). Internal
  --    only, NEVER surfaced to client/report (Rule 18, FERPA). Scoring reads
  --    'conduct' for ONE purpose (v4.3, interviewer-error marking): to EXCLUDE
  --    C2-warning and C5 exchanges from candidate evidence. It never feeds a
  --    penalty, and the judge is told not to mention either in the report.
  -- 'data_request': Rule 11 audit — one row per candidate data request
  --    (subtype release|refuse|defer|clarify|none; payload what, ledgerItemIds,
  --    revealedByNow) plus a 'classified' marker per checked exchange, so
  --    scoring can backfill exchanges the background pass missed.

scores
  id, session_id (fk),
  -- 8 rubric dimensions, each: <dim>_rating (enum; NULL when the dimension
  --   is "not assessed", Rule 9 v4.3) + <dim>_evidence_jsonb (legacy):
  --   structure, quantitative, data_exhibit, judgment, creativity,
  --   synthesis, communication, pushback
  overall_rating (enum), top_fix (text),
  rubric_jsonb           -- full structured judge output (per-dimension
                         --   wentWell/needsWork/missedOpportunities + coverageCaveat
                         --   + notAssessed);
                         --   the *_evidence columns are legacy (pre-8-dim rows)
  deterministic_jsonb    -- math step results (per-step errorClass + source span)
                         --   + data-leak audit
  model_answer_jsonb     -- structure / key math / recommendation exemplars
  scoring_runtime_ms, judge_model, created_at

session_audio          -- OPTIONAL, consent-gated; off by default
  session_id (fk), storage_path, consent_at

analytics_events       -- append-only; feeds §13 metrics
  id, session_id (nullable), user_id (nullable),
  event_type, payload_jsonb, created_at

voice_tts_usage        -- server-only (RLS on, no policies); voice deploy, 18 Oct
  month (text 'YYYY-MM', pk), used (int), updated_at
  -- the monthly Cartesia character ledger, shared by every agent job on every
  --   worker and the local voice scripts; incremented in one statement

voice_session_lines    -- server-only; voice deploy, 18 Oct
  id (identity), session_id (fk), line (jsonb), created_at
  -- a voice session's per-turn records (lib/voice/records.ts), one row per
  --   line of the old voice-live/<date>/<id>.jsonl; scripts/voice-export.ts
  --   writes the file back out
```

**Enums:** `phase` = `INTRO|CLARIFY|STRUCTURE|ANALYSIS|EXHIBIT|BRAINSTORM|RECOMMENDATION|WRAP|SCORING`. `session_status` = `active|completed|abandoned|terminated`. `rating` = `needs_work|meets_bar|strong` (displayed as needs work / adequate / strong). "Not assessed" is not an enum value: it is a `notAssessed` flag in `rubric_jsonb` with a NULL rating column, shown via `ratingLabel`.

**Terminated / abandoned scoring (Rule 18/19):** a `terminated` session (conduct C2-repeat / C3) produces **no score, no report**; a C5-`abandoned` session is excluded from scoring. The score route only proceeds for `status = completed`, so both are safe by construction.

**Candidate-ended sessions (Rule 18A, v4.10).** A stop request or the End button ends the session: `completed` with a report when something assessable happened (`flags.endReason = candidate_stop | candidate_end_button`, `flags.partial` when the case was cut short — no overall rating, the score row's `overall_rating` NULL), else `abandoned` (no report). No new `session_status` value — that would need a migration (flag it if wanted). A C5 offer accepted after the case is finished also completes with the report (`endReason = distress_stop_after_case`).

`content_jsonb` holds the full case schema (§11 of product PRD). It is **loaded server-side only**; the client receives the read-aloud prompt and exhibits-as-shown, never the ledger answers or keys.

---

## 5. The orchestrator — anti-hallucination is the core invariant

This is where FR-4 ("zero hallucinated figures") is won or lost. **The acceptance criterion is hard: 0 invented numbers across a 50-case QA run.** Build for that from line one, not as a later hardening pass.

### 5.1 Data-gating mechanism

1. The interviewer LLM **never receives un-revealed numeric values in its prompt.** The per-turn prompt includes: case prompt, current phase, conversation history, the *labels* of ledger items (so it knows what data exists and can decide whether the candidate's request warrants revealing it), exhibit titles, and the values of items **already in `revealed_data`** — nothing more. Labels, exhibit titles and code's stage lines may carry no figure the case prompt doesn't state (all-case test, 13 Oct — four new-case labels/titles did: "purchase intent at $3.99"); footnotes with figures live in exhibit `notes`, shown only with the exhibit.
2. To disclose a number, the LLM **declares** the request in its turn (`requests`: what was asked, the catalog ids that cover it, explicit ask vs passing mention, release now or later — §5.3). **Code decides and speaks it** (`data-decisions.ts`): only items the case holds and hasn't released, only explicit asks (a passing mention is offered, never released — and only when the candidate states a need for it: "I'd want…", "whether…", "worth checking the actual COGS percentage" — `mentionStatesNeed`), at most three a turn (the rest deferred out loud; a group of items the candidate asked for — a `factAnchors` group named in the model's label or in the candidate's own asking sentence — goes out whole once one of its items does, Lorenzo 15 Oct), the stall rung-3 rescue item only on a rung-3 turn. The value spoken is the ledger's approved wording, rendered by code between the model's `say` and its question; the model never writes a data line, a refusal or a deferral. An item counts as revealed when the segment carrying it is **delivered** (voice: played) — a reveal whose segment was not delivered is not booked. `release_when` stays an authoring/pacing hint: the model's now-or-later decides timing within those bounds (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-plan-owns-decisions-design.md` D2).
3. The system prompt forbids stating any number not provided in context. But the prompt is the soft layer — the hard layer is that **the model literally does not have the un-revealed values**, so it can't leak what it never saw.
4. **Post-turn audit (deterministic, not LLM):** after every interviewer turn, scan the spoken text for numeric tokens and verify each appears in `revealed_data` or is derivable from already-revealed / candidate / orchestrator-derived values. Any unexplained number → flag, log, and in QA mode fail the run. This audit proves the "zero hallucinated data" metric. It is the base of a wider post-turn suite (see §5.4): the tiered numeric-provenance audit (word-number aware), the style/length audit, and the meta-leak strip all run here too.
5. **Orchestrator-initiated disclosure** — all through the same ledger, revealed-only and audited: (a) an exhibit with `coversLedgerItems` marks those items revealed when shown (it displayed them); since round 5 code also records which items came out only on an exhibit, so a restatement can say where the item came from (D6, §5.4 Data; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed). The ids are kept in the session flags (`flags.exhibitOnly`), not a new column: a controller ruling, approved by Lorenzo (20 Oct), because a migration would clash with `main`'s drills migrations. An item said later leaves the list, and a missing field reads as said, so saved runs replay as before. The chat and the voice room's exhibit panel both show an exhibit's notes under the table (checked by a test); an exhibit with a release order (`after` — earlier exhibits, or the `brainstorm` stage (Hollis Bay's sampling exhibit; out once asked, or under time pressure) — and `requiresItems`, 13 Oct) goes out by no path — request, covering ask, the model's handover, code's exhibit stage — before its turn (and the brainstorm itself may wait on exhibits, `brainstorm.after`: Larkspur's waits on Exhibit 3 — code hands over the next ready exhibit instead, Settle holds the model's brainstorm move or the case's own ask, nothing counts as the brainstorm before it's due, no wait under time pressure; Lorenzo 13 Oct), nor does an item only it shows (deferred out loud; open asks for it are not forced out each turn); a `required` exhibit still unshown is owed in the analysis and end gates (Lorenzo, 13 Oct): code hands it over in place of a recommendation ask or close, and under time pressure it goes out with the recommendation ask; it also has a deadline (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 26 Blake 4755c03a never got prof-001's): a lead exhibit (no `after`) still unshown when the analysis budget is spent is handed over by code, one ordered after an exhibit or the brainstorm when the exhibit budget is (§5.2 stage deadlines); prof-001's exhibit is required since 15 Oct; (b) before any recommendation ask (code-written time warning / grace ask, or the coverage-complete ask) up to two open requests are released first (Rule 11); (c) a code-written close answers the final message's explicit asks before the goodbye.

### 5.2 State machine

`INTRO → CLARIFY → STRUCTURE → ANALYSIS → EXHIBIT → BRAINSTORM → RECOMMENDATION → WRAP → SCORING`

- **Phase is derived in code** (Rule 8, `progress.ts`): the model declares what its question does (`move`: clarify, structure, pressure_test, analysis, exhibit, brainstorm, risk, recommendation, other) and the phase rises to the highest stage the turn's move and code's own actions imply — releases → their `release_when`, an exhibit → EXHIBIT, a recommendation ask → RECOMMENDATION, a close → SCORING; forward only, never past RECOMMENDATION except by close. INTRO leaves after the first exchange. Stage administration (brainstorm, risk probe, recommendation asks) is read from the recorded moves (`flags.moves`), with the wording regexes only for turns recorded before moves existed. A separate `advancedLastTurn` flag gates the interviewer's *visible* behavior shift. There is no `advance_phase` and no per-phase action legality any more.
- **Turn kinds (Plan decides, spec 2026-10-06 §6):** each candidate message is answered by one of — `model` (the interviewer model); `rec_ask` (coverage complete, recommendation never asked: the model writes its lead-in, code supplies the ask); and four code-written kinds with no model call: `close`, `grace_ask`, `time_warning`, `rung1` (stall rung 1 restates the stored last question). Code-written kinds answer the message's data requests first (synchronous Haiku classification) and still pass the distress gate. Since 9 Oct they answer only what the candidate *asked the interviewer for* (`addressedAskSentences` in `request-signal.ts`, any phase): a sentence with an addressed-ask phrasing ("could I get…", "could you share…", "do we have…", "do we know how many / how much…", "any numbers on…"; "I'd want/need the…" only before the brainstorm or as a question), a question about case data, or a numbered list right after such a sentence. **At the close only**, a question the candidate asks themselves is no ask (no addressed cue, their own statements follow, either/or or self-framed: "Is it transaction count falling, or…? Those need different responses."; batch-24 ruling 4c); on the grace ask, time warning and rung 1 it stays an ask (Lorenzo, 15 Oct). A classifier detection is answered only when such a sentence covers it (a lone data question or an "I'd want" sentence covers only what it names); conditions and next steps in a final answer are not answered (behaviour spec Rule 12 item 6 — accepted trade-off: statement-form needs like Camila's are no longer answered at the close; 9 Oct smoke run, where the goodbye released two figures for conditions). **A held item asked in a real question is answered (round 5, A2; Lorenzo's rulings on the B1 + B2 list, 20 Oct; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed).** B1 Aldermoor Tyler t21 asked "Do we know whether Kelvexa's US exclusivity runs long enough to make a build pay back?". No addressed-ask cue matched it, so code's brainstorm stage turn dropped the detection. Now a detection no asking sentence covers is kept when an item it maps to is held (in the ledger, not yet said or shown) and a question sentence of the message (in question form, below) shares a content word with that item's label, spoken name or names. A case's proper name alone ("Kelvexa") links nothing, and a statement ("I'd want that definition settled before the board meets.") stays dropped. Both limits were controller rulings, approved by Lorenzo (20 Oct). **Question form, not "?"** (round 5b; Lorenzo, 20 Oct: "Voice check (free): in the existing voice exports, how often do the candidate's questions carry "?". If often not, the held-question rule detects question form, not "?"."; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed): in the voice exports with transcripts (14 sessions, 63 candidate turns) 4 of 18 direct questions had no "?", and 1 of 3 question-form data asks ("Do we have information about total sales or pricing."). The rule reads `isQuestionForm` (`stall.ts`): a sentence with "?" as before, or one without that opens like a question — fillers, then a wh-word with a subject-inverted auxiliary (how many / much / long / often / about, which one / of), or an auxiliary and a subject (the rule built on the count, and first for the held-question rule only: round-5b controller rulings 1 and 6, accepted by Lorenzo, 20 Oct). **Every candidate-text check that gates an ask or a stage reads question form** (round 5c; Lorenzo, 20 Oct: "4. Ruling 6: list every remaining "?"-dependent check and what it gates (free). Fix any that gates asks or stages in this round; log the rest."; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed): "?" or question form in the stall's `isQuestion` (a question-only turn, the clarify budget, the near-empty hedge, the verbatim repeat), `isDataRequest` (the data_request kind, progress not a clarify) and `hasSubstantiveStatement` (an unpunctuated question is no statement); in `request-signal.ts` the want-cue ask outside the ask phases, `asksAddressed` (code-written turns: close, grace, rung 1; guard B) and both tests of the close's rhetorical-question filter; and `asksAboutAssessment` (`spoken-close.ts`, which lets the report hand-off through the close_cue withhold — a voice "How am I doing" lost its answer). Without "?", a sentence that answers itself after the question clause (", I would say / assume / think …", "yes / no because") or a cleft ("What do we do about X is raise prices") is a statement, and `asksAddressed` drops a self-answered sentence; a cleft whose subject is followed by a cognition or report verb (know, think, tell, say, expect, believe, assume, guess, figure, see, mean) stays a question ("How do we know the price is right"); "Can we assume …" is a question (controller rulings, proposed). Checks on interviewer, model, author or code text keep "?", and so do the voice ack choice (it has a question-opener fallback) and the bare sign-off test (inventory in §16, round 5c). Free replay: 0 changed decisions in all 21 slices (text personas write "?"; the change is for voice). The rule is off at `close` and `close_wait`, where the 9 Oct goodbye rule holds. Replay: Tyler's t22 says "Kelvexa is protected by patents until 2038."; b32 Larkspur Marcus t24 now gets the fleet line for his "Can the existing fleet cover the extra 1,000…?" (kept: the item holds part of the answer; controller, approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct).
- **v4.10 additions:** two more code-written kinds — `stage_exhibit` (code hands over the owed exhibit and asks about it) (for a case with `required` exhibits: the next unshown one, one per turn, until all are out — 13 Oct) and `stage_brainstorm` (code asks the owed brainstorm — the case's own `brainstorm.ask`, else a case-neutral generic question; on a turn whose own data line hands over data it asks about the figures instead and the brainstorm waits a turn, 13 Oct — except under time pressure (time up, or the hurry test the brainstorm-before-Exhibit-3 hold uses), where the brainstorm ask follows the data line in the same turn and counts; round 5, A2, a controller ruling, approved by Lorenzo (20 Oct): the plan's "same turn" brought back the batch-21 D8 fault on prof-001 b28 Fiona t10 and b31 Blake t18 in the replay) — chosen when the case would close or code would ask for the recommendation while an interviewer-led case still owes the stage, or when the stage's deadline passes (15 Oct, above), not under time pressure, each at most once (Rule 9; `stage-evidence.ts`). A case with `brainstorm.required: false` (Quillpath) owes no brainstorm and its end gate doesn't wait for one; the case's own brainstorm question counts as the brainstorm when the interviewer asks it (`brainstorm.ts`). Since round 5 a reworded one counts too (A7; Lorenzo: "credit a reworded stage ask so it isn't repeated word for word"; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed). B1 Aldermoor Raj t18 and Marcus t14 asked it in their own words, and code re-asked the case's words at t20, because the subject sat in a lead-in sentence and the question alone scored under the 60% overlap. Now, when the question alone is under 60%, the sentence before it is added, if the question carries the ask's verb and at least half its question clause ("Suppose Aldermoor builds its own US team. How could it reduce the risk of a slow start, …?"). A question that only names the topic ("What are the risks of a slow start?") still doesn't count. The plan's clause that a `say` sentence counts only as the turn's last question is moot: `say` is never read for the credit (a controller ruling, approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct). `say` is still not read for the credit. Rung 1 restates the stored question as written after a colon ("The question on the table: Lay out …"), joined by "is" only for one sentence opening with a question word (13 Oct; batch 21 "is lay out …"). Exit turns (Rule 18A) are scripted plans decided before the model: the end, one clarifying question, the refusal bound's offer to stop; each waits for the distress and conduct screens. While the pressure-test probe is open the stall ladder fires no rung. The close line follows the end reason (`time_up` / `complete` / `unresolved`).
- **Single wall clock, per-phase budgets.** There is one total case budget per case (`pacing.totalMs`, default 20 min — prof-001; the four new cases 25 min — prof-002 Larkspur since 13 Oct, entry-001, pricing-001 and launch-001 since 19 Oct, budgets ×1.25 — `caseTotalMs`) (20 min — raised from 5 on 2026-09-15, when a human-paced run showed 5 minutes allows only ~3 typed replies); phases carry per-phase budgets from case config (`pacing.phaseBudgetsMs`, uniform fallback). A pacing nudge injects "advance now if the exit criterion is met" when a phase exceeds its budget or the session falls ≥2 phases behind. **Stage deadlines (Lorenzo, 15 Oct — the core-analysis, brainstorm and recommendation deadlines, Claude's extensions, approved by him the same day; batch 26 Blake 4755c03a reached time-up with the exhibit owed on every turn, no brainstorm and no risk question; `stageDeadlinesMs` in pacing.ts):** the budgets' cumulative ends are deadlines for what's still owed — when the analysis budget is spent (prof-001 11:20), code hands over a required lead exhibit and asks back to the core analysis (`stage_exhibit`, `stage_analysis`); when the exhibit budget is (13:20), an exhibit ordered after another or after the brainstorm and the brainstorm (`stage_brainstorm`); when the brainstorm budget is (16:00), code asks for the recommendation (`rec_ask`), leaving the recommendation and wrap budgets for the recommendation and its risk question (the risk question stays the model's; a recommendation ask past this deadline also asks the risk — Rule 12 below). One stage a turn, in the end gate's order (exhibit, analysis, brainstorm, recommendation), each once; not while the pressure test is open, not under time pressure (the time warning owns the last 90 s), and a deadline yields **once** (per stage; an exhibit's once per exhibit — `flags.stageYielded`) to a turn whose checker probe, pointer or hint, or code's own probe-like flag, is due; the stage then goes on the next turn with no further yield, and a probe due then is held for the next model turn (the checker's doesn't expire; code's own probe released then is left out of the gate's handled findings and the attempt count and re-gated on the next model turn — engine-asks final review, 19 Oct, the controller's proposal, Lorenzo's by his round-2 ruling 8; before, it was marked handled and counted but never said; since round 2 — his ruling 6, 19 Oct, built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed — that held probe dedupes none of the checker's claims on its stage turn (the checker plan reads the gate's outcomes without it) and its re-gate is exempt from the two-message expiry (`flags.codeGate.held`), like the checker's own held line). A correction — the checker's or code's own — never delays a deadline: it rides on the overdue stage turn (a stage whose deadline has passed, whatever made the turn), said first, with the stage's ask in the same turn (Lorenzo's ruling R7, 19 Oct — that code's own corrections ride too and that "overdue" means past the deadline whatever made the turn are Claude's reading, his by his round-2 ruling 8, as is a probe held on two stage turns in a row when two deadlines pass together, which can be lost at a time-up close (his open point 5, accepted as proposed); since round 2 a corrected root's restatement (§5.4, his ruling 1) rides the same way; built on branch `engine-asks`, 19 Oct; not merged or deployed; batch 32 Aldermoor Marisol had a checker line on every turn from 6:32 to 22:07 and closed owing the brainstorm — before, a deadline yielded for one turn to every turn carrying a math or misquote line). The new cases' recommendation + wrap budgets are 3 minutes (150 s + 30 s; Lorenzo's 15 Oct ruling, built 19 Oct, the time taken from each case's analysis budget), so on a 25-minute clock the recommendation ask comes at 22:00.
- **Load-shedding (Rule 15):** in the final stretch (keyed to *total* remaining time — robust to the phase machine under-advancing), the prompt carries a directive to stop optional probing and protect the recommendation; entry is logged (`load_shed`) so the judge attributes thin late coverage to time, not the candidate.
- **Ending, time warning, grace ask (Rule 12) — code's, not the model's:** the time-warning turn fires on the first candidate turn inside the warning window (90s in text mode, v4.3; 30s intended for voice) — or, since 15 Oct, on the first turn after which the candidate's next message, at their own pace, would land past time-up: the remaining time is at most the time since their previous message (`timeWarningDue`; the clock at each message is saved as `flags.lastMessageElapsedMs`; batch 26 Blake's exchanges took ~92 s, t23 came 1.4 s before the window and t25 after time-up, so no warning fired) — unless the recommendation is already in. **A late recommendation ask also asks for the biggest risk (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 26 Blake 4755c03a, checker on: the recommendation came only with the time warning and time-up left no turn for the risk question):** the time-warning line and code's recommendation ask past the recommendation deadline (`recAskLate`: a `rec_ask` turn at or past the brainstorm budget's end, whatever opened it; `LATE_RECOMMENDATION_ASKS`) ask both in one question ("We're almost out of time — what would you tell the CEO, and what's the biggest risk to it?"); the stage record counts the recommendation and the risk as asked from that one line, so the case closes on the next reply that gives the recommendation. The grace ask at time-up is not in the ruling and is unchanged. If time runs out and no recommendation ask was ever delivered, the time-up turn is a grace ask and the next message ends the case. The case closes when time is up (grace ask done or not due), or when the end is allowed and either the recommendation is in and the risk probe was asked, or the recommendation was refused twice. Every close is one scripted line. Open data requests are released before any recommendation ask (§5.1).
- **Coverage-gated ending:** a background coverage agent (Haiku, run via `after()`) scores each dimension 0–100 on *evidence sufficiency* every turn; the case can't close on coverage until every dimension is sufficiently tested and the recommendation was asked (or time is up), and the interviewer is steered toward the undertested ones. Keeps the interviewer from wrapping early and leaving weak areas unprobed. Normative detail in `docs/scoring-qa.md`.

### 5.3 The interviewer's turn (declarations + words)

One JSON object per turn, constrained by `output_config.format` and streamed (`lib/agent/models/turn-schema.ts`):
`{ say, move, requests: [{what, item_ids, explicit, respond: release|defer}], exhibit, rescue_item, question }`.
The model writes `say` first (one neutral acknowledgment, streamed while the rest is written), then declares what the candidate asked for and what its question does, then writes `question` (exactly one). Code decides releases, refusals, deferrals, offers, exhibits, phase and ending, and writes those lines (§5.1, §5.2); the data line goes out once `say` and the declarations have closed. Releases are decided before deferrals and offers, so a request partly answered this turn is not also deferred out loud. An id the case doesn't have triggers one regeneration while nothing has been delivered; once `say` is spoken, a request whose ids all fail to resolve is dropped (never turned into a refusal of held data). Literal `\uXXXX` escapes the model writes ("Hub\u2013Lakeport") are decoded in the streamed sentences and fields and in the parsed turn, before any guard reads them (`lib/decode-escapes.ts`; §15 (k), built on branch `engine-asks`, 19 Oct; not merged or deployed). Field order changed 6 Oct 2026 (batch 10: declarations first put Nikhil's first speech at ~3.3s; batch 11 with `say` first: 1.65s median). Replaced 6 Oct 2026: the `speak` / `reveal_data` / `show_exhibit` / `advance_phase` / `end_case` action list — two authors for every decision produced the batch-9 collisions (spec 2026-10-06 §1).

### 5.4 Behavioral requirements (system prompt + orchestrator)

**Normative source: `docs/interviewer-behavior.md`** (the authored conduct spec, Rules 1–19 across core conduct, data delivery, struggling-candidate handling, and a conduct/wellbeing track). The prompt encodes the rules; **deterministic backstops** in the orchestrator enforce what a prompt can't reliably guarantee. This section summarizes; the doc governs.

Core prompt constraints: neutral affect / never grade mid-case (1); force structure + live math (2); no fabricated candidate claims (3); one candidate task per turn, Socratic not coaching (4); 1–3 sentence spoken turns, no markdown (5); never adopt candidate-derived figures as fact (6) — the model never issues a math correction or states a replacement figure on its own; corrections come only from a valid recompute flag (Rules 2/14, v4.3), and a suspected misquote gets "check that against the figures"; pressure-test the opening structure (7) — a request made before the probe is answered is deferred out loud with its reason, every time, and the open probe is bounded (re-ask, narrowed probe, then a waiver — §5.4, v4.9); reveal data the candidate has earned and asked for; every data request is released, refused, or audibly deferred — never ignored, never substituted (10/11); an explicit ask the model leaves unmapped is not proof the case lacks the data — a narrow alias maps it only when it names one catalog label with nothing beside it but articles and period words the item covers, and an unmapped deferral is never a refusal (v4.9) — since 19 Oct (built on branch `engine-asks`, 19 Oct; not merged or deployed) every explicit ask code would refuse because it maps to no item is first checked by the small model (Haiku 4.5; until round 5b `classifyDataRequests`, the ask phrased as an ask, against the ledger labels; since round 5b its own prompt, `classifyAsk`, against each item's label and masked line — E5 partial answers, below) — an item-less deferral (Lorenzo's ruling R1), an item-less release and a code-written turn's item-less detections (his round-2 ruling 2, 19 Oct, which overrules the round-1 proposal that left releases unchecked), and an ask emptied by the basis rule (the controller's proposal, his by round-2 ruling 8): any item → a deferral naming the ask (since round 5, E5 below decides a mapped ask); no item → refused; a timeout, an error or no answer → the named deferral, never a refusal (ruling 2). The check is capped at 1.5 s (ruling 2; was 3 s), so an item-less ask can hold its turn's data up to 1.5 s. The generic "Let's settle your structure" line is gone. **Code refuses an unmatched ask only when the check maps it to no item** (Lorenzo's round-3 ruling 5, 19 Oct: "Code refuses only when the check says the case doesn't hold it."; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed): any ledger item the check found was a named deferral, whether or not the scope rules or the basis rule cut that item (round 5's E5, below, now decides it) — this overrules the round-2 scope filter on the check's mapping (his "provisional #8 overruled") and round 1's final-review refusal of a basis-emptied ask mapped back only to the cut item; the cut and out-of-scope ids are only logged (`basisCut`, `outOfScope`). That the basis-cut refusal goes too was the controller's reading (the basis rule is a scope rule), approved by Lorenzo with his wave-A rulings, 19 Oct: "Your decisions 1–5 this round: approved". Live on prof-001's 34 item-less asks (41 checks; r3 Haiku check, $0.04): refused 33, deferred 8 (7 hollow — the case lacks the data: 2 null verdicts, 5 topical or period-cut mappings), 0 wrong releases (the verdict only sorts an item-less ask; no id or value flows from it), 0 timeouts at 1.5 s, p95 1,333 ms. An ask that matched an item a finer-cut scope rule empties (per store, by month, by category…) stayed refused without the check — ruling 2 says "unmatched ask" (round-2 controller ruling, his by his round-3 acceptance) — until round 5's E1, below. **Round 5 (Lorenzo's rulings on the B1 + B2 list, 20 Oct; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed).** **E1 — a scope-emptied ask keeps its coarse item** (B1 Aldermoor Marisol t14 and Jasmine t4, serious: "treatment mix" was refused whole and neither heard of the incumbent drug; this reverses the round-2 finer-cut exception). When a scope rule empties an explicit ask of an item that holds the asked quantity at a coarser cut (a by-class, by-month, per-store, one-off or count cut of it), that item is released under every gate (release order, pressure test, cap, exhibit), and the finer cut is refused in the same turn: the item's line, then "I don't have <the ask>." (logged `scope_coarse`). The refusal says the model's `what` (controller, approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct). A coarse item already out is not said again: the refusal goes alone, unchecked. That reads Lorenzo's 13 Oct 4b ("As I mentioned" only for a re-ask of the same item) and his refined rule 1 (controller, approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct). A coarse item a gate holds leaves the ask to the small-model check. A change item the split rule cuts beside a split ask ("the current COGS split by category" → the two input price changes; `changeForSplit`) is a sibling, not the quantity asked, so that ask is refused as before, unchecked (controller, approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct; prof-001 b31 Sofia t17 keeps its true refusal). The basis-cut path is unchanged. **E5 (a, b, d) — what the check's mapping does** (Lorenzo: "E5: build (a) and (b). Not (c). Add (d): a deferral names the item it will deliver, not the candidate's words, and a mapped item that's releasable now goes now."). One function, `decideCheckedAsk` (`turn-data.ts`), decides every verdict, and the A4 and A5 paths below call it (controller, proposed). The check's cache keeps `{ mapped, ids, outOfScope }` (since round 5b also `partial` and `missing`), shared by Stream and Settle. (b) Every mapped id outside the ask's scope: E1's form, "I don't have <X>." with the coarse item's line when one can be released (Lorenzo: never a bare refusal on the check's scope call alone); only `changeForSplit` siblings: refused. (a) Every in-scope id already out (said, or on a shown exhibit): restated through the restate path (cap 3), never deferred. A mixed verdict releases the new ids and doesn't restate the ones out (controller, approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct). (d) An in-scope id releasable now under every gate: released this turn. One not yet releasable: deferred by the item's spoken name, never the candidate's words, and booked as an open request with its ledger id, so it is followed up; on a wrap-up or close turn, "We won't get to <item> today.". No verdict (a timeout or error): the named deferral in the ask's words, as before; no item: refused. Basis-emptied asks are read for scope too, and a check-made release takes a release-cap slot in ask order (both controller rulings, proposed). (c), the scope filter on the check's mapping, is not built (his ruling). **The live check, round 5** (68 asks: prof-001's 34 and every unmatched B1 + B2 ask; `claude-haiku-4-5`, $0.0749; p50 792 ms, p95 1,356 ms, one call over 1.5 s; replayed through the new decisions, `Case Interview Runs/replays/2026-10-20/engine-asks/r5-haiku-check.md`): 39 refused, 10 released, 6 restated, 2 hollow named deferrals (no verdict, nothing held), 1 deferral by item name (all released later), 1 "We won't get to" (the timeout), 9 not reached in the replay. **0 wrong releases.** 9 releases or restates don't answer the ask (§15). Hollow deferrals fell from 7 (round 3) to 2. **E5 partial answers and the check's own prompt** (round 5b; Lorenzo, 20 Oct: "E5 partial answers: the check reports per mapped item whether it fully answers or is only the nearest held data. Partial → E1's form ("I don't have <the asked part>." + the item's line). Tighten the check prompt to the item that holds the answer, with the r5-haiku negatives (#48 → Option B is closer; #64 and #67 → cohort_notes). #12 is fine (Exhibit A shows margin by year)."; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed). The check has its own prompt, `classifyAsk` (`data-requests.ts`); the shared detection prompt, `classifyDataRequests`, is unchanged for the message classifier, post-turn and scoring (controller, accepted by Lorenzo, 20 Oct). It sees each item's id, label and line as the interviewer says it, every figure masked as "#" (digits and spelled-out amounts; `maskFigures`, `askCheckCatalog`, cached per case), so no figure reaches the classifier (controller, accepted by Lorenzo, 20 Oct). Per mapped item it answers "full" or "partial" (only the nearest data: the asked part is absent from the line; a line that gives the asked quantity for the asked period is full however worded), and for a partial the "missing" part in the ask's words, no numbers. The prompt maps to the item that holds the answer (a same-topic item that doesn't is no mapping), says a selling-price or revenue item never answers a cost ask and the reverse, and carries his four examples (#48 option_b over company_size; #64 and #67 cohort_notes, #64 worded without a case figure; #12 full). On `claude-haiku-4-5` the reply is prefilled with `{"items":[` and stops at `]}` (`stop_sequences`; the cut text is put back before parsing), and the parser takes the first balanced JSON object holding `items`; `claude-haiku-5-5` would use json_schema instead. Decisions (`decideCheckedAsk`): full in-scope items decide as before (a, b, d). A partial item alone takes E1's form: not yet out and releasable, its line then "I don't have <missing>."; already out, the refusal alone (4b); held by a gate or the release order, the refusal alone. A mixed full + partial verdict gives the full items and refuses each partial's missing part beside them; the partial item itself is not given (controller, accepted by Lorenzo, 20 Oct). A missing part is said only when valid (controller, accepted by Lorenzo, 20 Oct): no digit, not opening at a preposition, and either a span of the ask bounded at list boundaries (its start or end, a comma, and, or, plus, including, as well as), said in the ask's text, or a phrase whose content words all come from the ask; a piece of the ask cut mid-phrase is invalid, and so is a part the item's own line states (with the whole ask on the line the item counts as full). Otherwise one partial → the ask's own words; in a mixed verdict → no refusal for that partial, never a false refusal beside a full item. A figure from the ask that a mapped line states is dropped from a pair in the refusal ("I don't have the fare trend across FY2024."; the implementer's addition, flagged); a phrase led by a case name reads "data on the …". Old recorded verdicts read all full. The `itemless_defer` record carries `partial`, `missing`, latency, tokens and cost. The message classifier's path (A4, A5) has no partial form (§15). **The live check, round 5b** (the same 68 asks; `r5b-haiku-pre.md`, `r5b-haiku-check.md`): the first run ($0.0943) had 16 nulls, prose around the JSON → the prefill; the second ($0.0943) 0 nulls and 15 calls over 1.5 s, each `{"items":[]}` then prose → the stop sequence (gain inferred, not re-measured). Replayed as delivered: 0 wrong releases, 0 refusals of an ask an item fully answers, 0 hollow deferrals (10 asks without the stop sequence), and **5 asks with a bare partial answer** (#18 half, #36, #49/#50 borderline, #63, #66), each Haiku labelling a near item full; its labels change run to run. Lorenzo's bar ("0 bare partial answers") was not met; his ruling (20 Oct): the qualifier guard, no model switch now. **The qualifier guard** (round 5c; Lorenzo, 20 Oct: "1. Bare partials: build an engine guard. If an ask names a qualifier the item's line doesn't contain (a period: FY2024, "since <date>", "today/current", "beyond month N", "later/earlier cohorts", "trend"; a scope: industry, competitor(s), market; or a second topic with no item), that item counts as partial whatever Haiku labels it, and the refusal names the qualifier ("I don't have FY2024 fares.", "I don't have industry benchmarks."). Mutation tests from #18, 36, 49, 63 and 66 verbatim, plus true full answers that must stay full. No model switch now."; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed; `ask-qualifiers.ts`, `qualifierGuard` in `checkAsk`). It reads the check's verdicts only — not items the model names itself, the message classifier's path (A4, A5), `refusal_covered` / `refusal_remapped` or the coverage path. `askQualifiers` reads the ask's periods (FY / CY years, quarters, bare years; "since <date>"; today / current / now / this year; beyond / after / past month N; later / earlier / newer / older / recent cohorts; a trend: trend, trajectory, over time, year over year, has moved / changed), scopes (industry; competitor(s) / competitive / competition; market, but not market share / size / growth; benchmark(s)) and, with two or more conjuncts, its topics (split at commas, ";", "&", "and", "or", "plus", "including", "as well as"; a piece with under two content nouns joins its neighbour; never split inside a question-shaped ask, parentheses, after "between" or inside an open clause). `unmetQualifiers` keeps those no mapped item's label (with its period note) or line (the server-side ledger value, unmasked) states. A year is met when the item names it, or when the line names no year and the item covers the case's compared years (read from its lines naming exactly two years: Larkspur FY2023 and FY2025; an item with a change and no period, or whose periods carry years, spans them); when the case names no compared years and the line has no year and no today / current / now marker, an asked year is waived (`anyYear`, the implementer's addition, narrowed by a controller ruling, proposed). A trend is met by a change (up / down from, rose / fell, from … to, stable / flat / unchanged / has not changed, "no material changes", "did not change", "the same … as" only before a period), a series over time (two or more month / quarter / week / year points, "month to month", "by quarter") or a label that covers both periods. "since <date>" (read with its FY / CY prefix) is met by "since <date>" on the line, by the date with a change, or by the year among the item's years when the line states a change or covers two periods; "beyond month N" by the phrase or a later month; today / current by the token, a current-period label, or an item with no period of its own and no past marker. A scope is met by the words a label or line already carries (controller rulings, proposed): competitor by competing / rival / other players / standard / low-cost / other airlines or brands / compares with / versus; market by market / category / demand / patients / eligible / industry / sector / competitor(s); industry by typical(ly) / often / category / sector / market / demand / competing / competitor(s); benchmark by industry / typical / comparable / historical / often / past or previous launches, and "average" only with an industry / market / typical / comparable word in the same label or line — a company's own average is no benchmark. "usually" and market's total / overall / size / adults were dropped (they hid true gaps). A topic is met when one item states at least half of its nouns (or of its nouns not common to the case, on 3+ items). A period or scope inside one conjunct is judged only on the items answering that conjunct, and a period said in two conjuncts belongs to each by its place (controller ruling, proposed). Which items turn partial (`guardedItems`): with one conjunct, every mapped item; with more, the items answering the conjunct an unmet period or scope sits in, plus the items answering none. A missing second topic doesn't make the items answering the other topics partial: they keep Haiku's label and every gate (a held item keeps its named deferral and booking), and only the missing topic is refused beside them (controller ruling, proposed: his "that item counts as partial" read as the item whose line lacks the qualifier). A guard-made partial obeys E1 — not yet out and releasable: its line, then the refusal; out or gated: the refusal alone — and the M6 false-refusal veto (`factAnchors.covers`) still applies to each part. The refusal (`guardRefusal`) puts the qualifier on the ask's own head noun phrase: a year or "current" before it ("FY2024 fares", "an FY2024 load factor", "current waste rates"; the ask's article kept before a year, "a" → "an" before a vowel sound), "since …", "today" and "beyond month N" after it ("Pro's CAC since January 2025", "the fleet since FY2023"), cohorts as a possessive ("later cohorts' upgrade rates"), a scope head as its scope words ("industry benchmarks"), a missing benchmark with every word before it less scope words ("fare benchmarks"); a part with a word or figure the ask lacks falls back to the conjunct as asked; parts join "a, b or c", and Haiku's own valid missing parts are merged in ask order. A figure in a part must have been said by the candidate (the audit allows it), else no guard and Haiku's verdict stands. The `itemless_defer` record keeps Haiku's own `partial` / `missing` (a replay serves the same verdict) and adds `guard`; with a guard, Haiku's whole-ask partial is logged `partial_dropped` (`whole_ask`). **Proof:** the 68-ask probe 13 flags, 0 false; the Haiku rerun at 29469ee ($0.0824 of the $0.12 cap; `r5c-haiku-check.md`) — 12 guard rows on 11 asks, none against a line that states the qualifier, none releasing anything; 0 wrong releases, 0 refusals of a fully answering item, 0 hollow deferrals, 0 bare partials, 0 calls over 1.5 s (p50 509 ms, p95 803 ms, max 958 ms: the "]}" stop, measured). **In waves B1′ / B2′ (batches 36–37, 20 runs)** it fired 3 times, every refusal true (one wording, "I don't have late-stage oral competitors.", reads as "none exist"); the waves' bare partials and false refusals came from outside it — the check's own wrong full or coarse labels, items the model names, `refusal_covered` with no remainder, and the coarse path's refusal whose ask no cover matched (Hollis Bay Marisol t8: the coverage step had read the same ask with the same covers on pass 1 and matched none, and the covers veto reads those covers, so it would have missed too; attribution corrected by the 21 Oct data-path audit, applied on Lorenzo's ruling, 21 Oct; fixed in round 6 by the synonym fold onto `retailer_support`'s "how many stores"; §15, after round 5c). **Since round 6, on tagged cases,** the guard is the qualifier half of the answer gate, which runs on every path that gives a data line ("Round 6", below). **Only an item-backed deferral holds the model's figure question** (Lorenzo's round-3 ruling 3, 19 Oct; built on `engine-asks`): Settle withholds a model question about figures on a turn that holds a ledger item — gated ids (not on a wrap-up turn), item-backed deferrals, the gate's deferrals — or before any figure is out; the check's item-less named deferrals and "We won't get to" lines hold no data and don't withhold it (b31 Sofia t10's math probe returns). **On a wrap-up or close turn** (`wrapUpTurn` in `turn-data.ts`: kinds `close`, `close_wait`, `grace_ask`, `time_warning` and code's recommendation ask `rec_ask`, or a RECOMMENDATION/WRAP-phase turn after the recommendation is received) every deferral is said as "We won't get to <ask> today." instead of "I'll come back to …" — item-backed deferrals, the gate's, those over the release cap, and the check's named deferrals, mapped or unanswered (his round-2 ruling 3 for the check's; his round-3 ruling 4, 19 Oct, for all: "Close turns: all deferrals, item-backed included, become "We won't get to <ask> today."; if the ask label is long or a list, "We won't get to that today.""; built on `engine-asks`). A long label (more than 8 words as said) or a list (two or more asks grouped, or a comma or " and " joining two items) is "We won't get to that today." (pool `wont_get_to_that`; the thresholds were a round-3 controller ruling, Lorenzo's since he approved the round-3 decisions 1–5). An item said "won't get to" leaves the open-requests queue (it is not released later unless asked again) and stays an asked-but-unanswered gap for the report (controller; approved with the round-3 decisions 1–5). Pool `wont_get_to`, his wording; that it is one line, not a pool of five, that `rec_ask` counts as wrap-up, and that no-verdict asks hear it too were round-2 controller rulings, his by his round-3 acceptance. Withhold-until-asked and jailbreak-resistance (FR-5/FR-8) still hold — and a jailbreak still can't surface un-revealed numbers (§5.1). The prompt states each rule once and carries a PRIORITY block (turn note > time pressure > coverage/pacing/open requests > defaults; numbers and attribution never overridden except by a recompute figure) — behavior doc, Precedence hierarchy, v4.8.

Deterministic backstops (all in `/lib/orchestrator`, logged; QA-gated in the harness):
- **Numeric provenance audit** — every quantity in an interviewer turn must trace to revealed ledger values, a candidate-attributed figure, or an orchestrator-derived value; word-numbers and ranges normalized; unit-bearing unmatched quantities block, bare counts log. **Block withholds** (v4.3): sentences carrying a blocked figure are stripped before the turn is spoken (`enforceNumericProvenance`) — the audit previously only logged, and a persona run delivered unrevealed revenue.
- **Exact lookup of table cells and ledger figures (13 Oct, Lorenzo after Larkspur; `cell-lookup.ts`)** — beside the misquote grammar (F2), every cell of a case's `table` exhibits is a fact (subject = row, quantity = column or row label, period, value, kind, $M or $ scale), out once its exhibit is shown or the ledger line stating it is revealed. A candidate's figure is a misread when its sentence quotes that quantity (named next to the figure, or a "from X to Y" chain off a correct figure of the subject), in the same kind and scale, matches none of its figures (for the subject and period when named) and sits within 10% of the nearest one. Not quotes: changes between periods, the case's math-step answers, computed results ("that's", "="), per-flight/per-passenger rates, derived quantities ("new", "lost", "combined"), hypotheticals in the figure's clause, projections in its sentence. The correction is the ledger line that shows the cell, in its own words (the misquote path). Charts (prof-001's exhibit) stay with the grammar. **Quote grammar, 19 Oct (§15 (p) with (c); built on branch `engine-asks`, 19 Oct; not merged or deployed):** a figure ending a sentence ("The shelf price is $4.49."), thousands as one figure ("$1,200", not "$1"), and a scale word ("$77 million" against the item's "$80 million", compared at full scale; a scaled figure quotes only an item stated at scale; "$1,200,000" read at the item's scale) are read; an arithmetic sign skips a quote only up to the end of the anchor's clause, and not a quoted figure used as an operand later; "you gave / you said" links an anchor to its figure; cells written "$80 million" or "250 million" are lookup facts. Not caught: a figure before its quantity ("the $77 million annual selling cost") — not now (Lorenzo's round-2 ruling 7, 19 Oct; a widening needs the labelled sets rechecked). The plan no longer dedupes the checker's claim for a misquote flag the gate holds (`checker_disagrees`), so its probe still goes; since the final review (19 Oct) the plan reads the gate's own decisions (earlier turns' `code_gate` rows and this turn's; since round 2 the rows are sorted by turn before "last decision wins", so a held probe's re-gate is the outcome that counts — Lorenzo's ruling 6, 19 Oct) and a code flag of any kind dedupes the checker's claim, or counts as code's probe, only when the gate released it — one ruled `derived`, `not_rated`, `stale`, `expired` or `checker_disagrees` leaves the checker's claim standing. Validated on Larkspur: `scripts/step-binding-check.ts --misquotes`, labels in `evals/misquote-labels.json`. **Ledger lines no exhibit shows (13 Oct, Larkspur ruling b):** read figure by figure for the quantity named next to each ("variable cost of $11,000 per flight", "the average fare fell from $240 to $160" — the case's first and last table periods, "a 75% load factor", "80 seats", "$20 of extras per passenger"); not figures: ranges, years, sizes ("80-seat"), differences ("30% lower"), changes ("up 40%", "a 10% increase"), bounds ("fewer than 5%"), durations, billions; the subject is a table subject the line names. Out only once that line is released. A rate the line states ("per flight") is matched when the candidate says it; with no rate said, a figure without one is preferred. **Report-only until checked** (`ledgerLookupLive`, no case live): logged as `misquote_report_only` — no correction, no MISQUOTE hint, no echo, no checker dedupe; `step-binding-check.ts --misquotes` lists them as `misquote-report-only`.
- **Recompute + unit-check** — per-turn, deterministically recompute figures the candidate states against the case `math_steps` (respecting `alt_answers`) and detect nested-percentage conversions, injecting a hint so the interviewer probes/corrects. **Source spans** (v4.3, `lib/scoring/math-spans.ts`): a number counts toward a step only near one of the step's `cues`, in the same clause, stating the step's `unit`; a step is checked only once its ledger `inputs` are revealed; prompt-fact steps are `live: false`. The hint never carries the step description (it can hold unrevealed values). A per-step attempt counter drives Rule 14: probe, then supply the figure; case-breaking errors corrected at once under time pressure. **Since 13 Oct (Lorenzo, after Larkspur) no code-written correction of a computed figure comes from a deterministic flag alone:** a step, arithmetic or pricing flag probes, and past the probe (or under time pressure) says nothing; the correction comes only through the math checker — code's own figure for the quantity the checker names, once the checker's figure agrees (3% / 0.5) and the input is bound — named in words with the figure as money is written ("Hub–Lakeport contribution per flight in FY2023 is about $4,200, not $11,429."). A checker claim on a figure code flagged is no longer a duplicate when it carries that agreed correction (code's probe counts as the first attempt; never a second probe); a figure code corrected as a misquote stays a duplicate. The recommendation flag (`rec_math_probe`) is a probe only for these kinds. **The same flag is the bound step plus the claimed value** (Lorenzo, 13 Oct, item 4; Owen c2cdbcc3 escalated on a first mention): the attempt counter and the checker's key are `<step or pricing kind>@<value>` (`attemptKey`, `checkerKey`), so a new wrong figure for a step is a new error, probed. Unit and period lines ("That's 25% of COGS, not 25 points of revenue") stay code's. Before spans, the check produced all three false corrections in the 27–28 Sep persona runs. **Step flags are live only on a validated case** (`stepFlagsLive`, Lorenzo 13 Oct after Larkspur): a case's step binding goes live only at zero false flags on its transcripts; until then its step flags are report-only (logged as `recompute_report_only`; no hint, no attempt, no recommendation flag, no checker dedupe). Only prof-001 is live. **Binding fixes (13 Oct, item 3):** a money step may declare `scale` (`unit` / `million`) and binds no figure at another scale ("$24M" is no per-flight $4,200); `unit: "count"` takes no $ or % figure ("$5 more per passenger"); a number glued to letters ("FY2023") is no figure; a figure the case holds in kind (the prompt, a revealed line, any step's worked figures — written figures only) is not a wrong answer to another step (the new routes' 56 passengers, $9,200, $11,200); nor is the result of correct stated arithmetic in its sentence, or a figure 100× off the step's answer; `flagNotCues` exclude a sentence from mismatches only (a correct figure there still verifies), and a sentence continuing the last one with "plus/less/minus" or an operator shares its exclusions. Stated arithmetic: money mixes scales ("26.4/2,000 = $13,200" holds), a leading minus or "negative" is the result's sign, and the right size with the other sign is unclear. Validation: `scripts/step-binding-check.ts` lists every step and arithmetic flag on a batch's transcripts and the new-case e2e scripts, with hand labels in `evals/step-binding-labels.json`.
- **Style + meta-leak** — 1–3 sentence / no-markdown length audit (data read-outs exempt), and a strip of internal planning that leaks into spoken text ("the candidate has…", "let me pressure…").
- **Stall ladder (Rule 13)** — graduated restate → narrow → directive-rescue for struggling candidates; logged as `intervention` events ("assisted ≠ covered").
