- **Vetoes on the model's words** (`vetoReason`, shared by Stream and Settle) — a sentence of `say` or `question` is withheld, never rewritten, for: a fabricated candidate turn, narration, system vocabulary, a copied check-in, an unsourced figure, a doubt probe on a verified figure, a challenge to an assumption about requested data, a goodbye (ending is code's), the model's own data talk in `say` ("I don't have…", "…aren't something I have in this case", "…is available, so I'll give you that"; dropped without stopping the stream); a refusal or deferral sentence, in `say` or the question, of an item code releases, restates or shows on an exhibit that same turn (`refuses_released`; round 5, E2; B1 Larkspur Tyler t22, "I have no ramp assumption to give you yet." before code's ramp line; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed). Stream holds a sentence with refusal wording until the requests close, then judges it against the turn's checked decisions, the cached small-model check included (controller, approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct). A refusal of another item is kept, and fact-check reads "I have no / nothing on X" as a refusal — and in the question, a refusal statement on a turn whose data line refuses (13 Oct, batch 21 Raj t14: refusals were said twice), a supplied recommendation from BRAINSTORM on, a question inside `say`, the "is that the margin problem, or does it include cost that grew with revenue?" probe aimed at a percent-of-revenue claim (`growth-probe.ts`, Lorenzo 15 Oct: only of claims in dollars or as cost growth rates — the probe's own quoted figure decides, points or a share on its base are a ratio claim; since 16 Oct also any growth probe quoting no figure a landed flag covers, `growth_probe_unchecked` — the gate below; quoting none, the candidate's message must size in dollars or state a cost growth rate; the prompt's rule says the same); and in the question, a handover statement ("Here's the cost structure over time.") when another sentence is left — code already announced it ("Here's an exhibit: …"). A withheld question becomes "Go on."
- **Rule 11 data-request tracking** — the turn's data decisions are logged as its `data_request` rows (subtype = the decision) with a `classified` marker, so the background re-classification is skipped; still-unreleased requests are injected into the next prompt as OPEN DATA REQUESTS. On a code-written turn, detections no addressed ask covers are not answered: they are recorded in the `code_written_requests` check (batch scorecard flag `code_written_ask_dropped`) and, when the case goes on (grace ask, time warning, rung 1), logged as `explicit: false` rows — which never become open requests or coverage gaps; the `classified` marker is still written. Ledger membership and revealed state are deterministic.
- **The candidate's own asks on model turns (round 5: A1, A4, A5; Lorenzo's rulings on the B1 + B2 list, 20 Oct; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed).** The message classifier (`classifyDataRequests`, Haiku 4.5) starts beside the model call whenever the message has an asking sentence (`addressedAskSentences`; from the brainstorm on, guard B's late set), so it usually adds no wait. After the model's requests close, a classifier detection that no explicit declaration covers joins the turn as an explicit ask and is decided like any other: released under every gate, or refused, restated or deferred through the check and `decideCheckedAsk`. A declaration covers a detection when they share a ledger item, or when the declaration names it (two of its naming words, or its only one; case proper names don't count; an offer never covers). The model's own declarations and offers go out unchanged. Code waits for the classifier only when some asking sentence is undeclared, at most 1.5 s (`ASK_WAIT_MS`); a timeout leaves the turn as the model wrote it. Checks `uncovered_asks` and `probe_turn_requests`. **A4** (B2 Hollis Bay Jasmine t5, "how many bags of chips does Hollis Bay sell a year?", which the model turned into an offer of the planning assumptions): t6 now says "Chip sales are flat, … I don't have the number of bags of chips Hollis Bay sells a year." and then the offer. That needs the count rule (§5.4 Data) and a launch-001 `why_now` cover, "bags of chips" (the count rule approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct; the cover: controller, proposed). **A1, probe turns** (B1 Larkspur Marisol t11 and t25: the model declared nothing and the checker's probe took the turn): on a model turn whose question is the checker's probe, pointer or hint (not a correction, a pressure-test step or a code fallback; controller, approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct), the data line goes between `say` and the probe ("I don't have evidence on whether load factor would fall if fares went back up to $200. You said “$18,000” — can you show me that calculation step by step?"). "Do we know how quickly / fast / long / soon …" is now an addressed ask. **A5, a model release the message never asked for is checked** (B2 Hollis Bay Marisol t9: her year-two marketing and recurring-lease asks got `longer_term_goals`). An explicit model release of an item not yet out, whose label, spoken name and names share no content word (by stem; case proper names removed as whole phrases, `namingWords`) with the candidate's message and the question it answers, waits for the classifier's verdict. If a detection maps the item, it goes. If none does, the item is taken off, and the message's own asks are decided through `decideCheckedAsk`. No verdict or a timeout keeps the release (fail-open). Exempt: an earlier open ask for the item, a case group the message names, a message that names nothing ("Could you share them?"). Logged `release_checked`, with latency and wait. The word test reads the message, not the model's `what` (written from the item's label, so it can't catch the fault), and the check is the message classifier, not the item-less check on the model's `what` (controller rulings, approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct). Marisol's t10 now hears one refusal of both asks. 0 of prof-001's 371 recorded model releases reach the check; 3 of 522 across batches 28–35. Cost: one Haiku call on each model turn whose message asks (≈ $0.002 a call, not measured). Latency: none when the model declared every asking sentence; otherwise up to 1.5 s before the data line, and a held refusal-shaped `say` sentence can wait too.
- **Round 6 — the data path on tagged cases (21 Oct; Lorenzo's go-ahead for round 6 and his rulings on the four points, 21 Oct, §16; plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-21-engine-asks-round6.md`, from the data-path audit `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-21-data-path-audit.md`; built on branch `engine-asks`, be1217b..e51a4841, not merged or deployed).** Everything in (a)–(d) and the tagged-only fixes in (e) switch on per case with `factAnchors.tags` (`FactBase.answerGate = tags !== undefined`). prof-002, launch-001, entry-001 and pricing-001 carry tags. **prof-001 carries none and is unchanged:** r6-final has 0 changed checker decisions and 0 changed interviewer lines on b28–b31 and b33, on and off (controller ruling, proposed: prof-001 gets round 6's data path only after a replay with tags is reviewed). (a) **Cover matching** (`coverageOf` / `coverageIn`, `fact-check.ts`; Lorenzo: "Normalize matching before adding phrases: plurals, a small synonym table ("how many" = "number of", "flights" = "departures", and similar). Match the candidate's own sentence as well as the model's rewording. … A cover match vetoes a refusal. It may release the matched item only if the item's tags (company; forecast vs current; quantity) also agree with the ask. Otherwise defer, through the existing E5 stop sequence."). Covers stay the one source for "the case holds this"; every read site (the coverage step R1, the names remap R2, per-store mapped asks R3, the check's scope R4, the M6 veto R5, the model-words vetoes R6, `release_checked` R7 — traced in the plan before any change) sees the new matching. **Normalization** folds the ask and the phrase alike: plural and possessive endings (`-s`; `-es` after ss / x / z / ch / sh; words in `-ss` kept) and `COVER_SYNONYMS` — "number of" / "count of" → "how many" (not after "a"), departure → flight, door → store, pricing → price (as a noun only). Each folded token keeps its span, so `FURTHER_CUT`, `GLOBAL_FINER` and the count phrase read the original words. **The candidate's sentence:** the asking sentence sharing the most content words with the model's `what` (`askSentenceFor`, case names aside; ties to the first) is matched too; its items join the ask's, `rest` and `coarse` come from `what` (from the sentence only when `what` matched nothing: Hollis Bay Tyler t5 gets the A4 coarse form, `why_now` + "I don't have annual chip bag volume."), a sentence hit counts only when its phrase shares a content word with `what`, and each hit is read in its own list piece of the sentence. **Entity exclusion:** a hit is dropped when its list entry (else the whole ask) names another entity and not the item's own (`tagsAgree`, (b)). **Geography:** a hit is dropped when the entry names a region (Europe / European / EU, UK, Canada, Asia, international) outside the hit's own phrase that the item's label and line don't name; such an entry is refused unchecked ("I don't have European net price per patient per year…", audit row 20; controller, proposed). **The in-total veto** (controller ruling C1, proposed): a count asked in total — "total number / count", "in total", "altogether" outside the phrase, in the hit's own clause, with the count in the phrase or the four words before it — drops that hit (only that one) when the item's label and line state no total ("total(s)" or "altogether" with a figure, or a figure in the two tokens before the counted noun: "30 million households", "14,000 flights"); b37h Marisol t27 "the total number of stores carrying Hollis Bay" stays the check's true refusal while t7 gets `retailer_support`. A period-only list entry ("FY2023 vs FY2025") joins the entry before it. **The tag gate** (`coverGateMiss`): every cover-matched id, whatever path releases it (R1, `covers_added`, R3's scope exemption, `answersAsk`), passes only if `tagsAgree` (on the ask's group plus the matching clause of the sentence) and `outsideAskScope` (count off, read on the covered list entry with the cover's spans: a cut inside the matched phrase doesn't count, Lorenzo's 15 Oct ruling 7) both pass; a coarse hit passes on tags alone; in a list the passing items go and the rest drop (logged `cover_gate`). **Cover-vetoed deferrals:** an ask whose every cover hit is vetoed on tags, or whose only hits were entity-excluded, never goes to the Haiku check — it gets the named deferral in the ask's words, holding no item, with no call (`cover_deferred`; controller ruling, approved by Lorenzo, ruling 4: "Vetoed cover → deferral without a Haiku check: fine."), tracked until it resolves ((d)). A scope-only veto before the pressure test defers by the item's spoken name and books the item (Task 68b); on a satisfied turn it takes E1's form, the item's line + "I don't have <the cut>." unchecked (X1; controller, proposed). **`covers_added`:** on every explicit model-mapped ask the items the covers name for it (tags and scope agreeing; not another request's this turn) are added — "average fare by route" → both route fares (audit row 6). (b) **Item tags** (`factAnchors.tags`, `lib/orchestrator/item-tags.ts`): `clientWords` (the client's own names, read outside a question's frame, so "Do we know …" names nobody), `entities: [{ id, words, items, rival? }]` (another company or product, the words that name it, the items about it; `rival: true` on prof-002's `competitor` and entry-001's `nimbrel`, not launch-001's client-owned `chips`), `forecast` (items stating a forecast, plan or estimate); items in no entity are the client's. `tagsAgree(ask, item)` — **entity**, one way only (controller ruling, approved by Lorenzo for now, ruling 4): another entity's words exclude a client item when no client word is said, and a different entity's words exclude an entity's item when its own entity isn't named; client words never exclude an entity's item (the missing direction, §15). An entity named on the item's own label or line counts as the item's own, coarsely: the line plus the refusal of its group (Task 68b-5; controller ruling, proposed). The words after versus / vs / compared with / relative to / against / than name nobody (`COMPARED`); client words are read everywhere. **Forecast:** a current marker (today, current / currently, now, at present, this year; not a leading "Now," or "for now") against a `forecast` item. Entity and forecast / current are read from the clause of the candidate's sentence matching the ask (`askClauses`) as well as `what`; period, scope and topic from `what` only (controller rulings, proposed). Quantity agreement is the existing scope rules, no tag field (controller, proposed). (c) **The answer gate** (`answerGate`, `turn-data.ts`; Lorenzo: "The guard on every path that gives a data line, not just the check's labels, with the company and forecast/current tags inside it. When it finds a topic unmet, refuse that part ("I don't have <part>") rather than releasing the item as a coarse answer."): round 5c's qualifier guard in round-6 grammar (`gateReading`) plus the tags, read per group of the ask (`askGroups`: list entries, a bare entry read with the one before). **Topic — on the Haiku check's verdicts only** (Lorenzo, ruling 1: "accept narrowing it to the Haiku check's verdicts"; on the interim replay the noun test refused ≈ 11 true answers on model-mapped asks — "price point", "churn", "ramp"): on an ask of two or more conjuncts with a topic unmet, the items stating no conjunct are dropped (never given, restated or coarse) and the topic part refused; the check's coarse path never gives a dropped item (audit row 10); a missing topic beside full items is refused as in round 5c; a drop that leaves only items already out says the refusal alone. **Entity and forecast / current — on the check's verdicts:** a mismatch drops the item and refuses its group (audit rows 2, 18, 19); a tag part a kept item's line states is not refused. **Off the check** (the model's mapped asks, the alias, `covers_added`, the message's detections, a code-written turn's kept detections, the names remap) the tags are read only with `TAGS_ON_NONCHECK_PATHS=1`, **off by default**; on, a mismatch defers (tracked, (d)), never refuses. The replay showed one wrong deferral with it on (b34a Marcus t10: "Hold that thought on the share of covered lives where Nimbrel has exclusive or preferred status", which the case doesn't hold), so by his ruling 1 those paths read no tags; no audit fault is left uncaught by it (rows 2, 10, 18 and 19 are caught on the check path). Task 68's cover gate on the coverage step and `covers_added` keeps reading tags (no false refusal, no wrong deferral in the replay). **Period and scope — on every path, on tagged cases:** E1's partial (the line and the refusal when not out; the refusal alone when out or gated). Round-6 grammar (tagged only): TREND also reads growth / grew / grown / over the last / past / recent (few) years / in recent years (row 22); a trend tied to one year ("through / during / within / in <year>", "<year> trend") is met only by two dated points inside it (row 15); a line stating how long a level takes to reach, or the first year's level, meets a forward or ramp trend, never a past one (`RAMP`); a rival entity's own item meets a competitor or benchmark scope. Every gate part is said unchecked (`gateRefused`, never in `toCheck`) after the M6 covers veto (`partial_dropped`, decision `answer_gate:<path>`); a unit with nothing left to give and no part left to say gets the named deferral. Logged `answer_gate` `{ what, path, refuse, drop, partial }`. Not run on forced or fulfilment releases or on exhibits. Proper adjectives keep their capital in code's refusals, deferrals and offers (European, American, US, EU, UK, Canadian, Asian, and the case's `sttKeyterms` names). (d) **Deferrals that resolve** (Lorenzo, ruling 4: "The deferral must resolve through the E5 stop sequence (delivered, or "We won't get to that"), never left hollow."): code's item-less named deferrals on a tagged case — cover-vetoed, entity-excluded, the gate's nothing-left one, and the tag mismatches when the switch is on — are kept open in `flags.openDeferrals` (no migration). One resolves when a later turn books another item under the same ask words (released, restated or shown; a prompt-fact restatement too) — never by its own vetoed or mismatched items — or refuses the same words (`how: 'refused'`), or else at the first wrap-up turn (`wrapUpTurn`), once and by name: "We won't get to <ask> today." (`wontGetToNamed`; a carried deferral never hears the bare "…that today."). Only booked (heard) deliveries count; a deferral made on a wrap-up turn is "We won't get to …" already and isn't tracked (controller rulings, proposed). Logged `deferral_resolved` `{ what, how, turn }`. (e) **The audit's code-only fixes** (8ba9d5bc, 92774e92): row 8, `ONE_OFF_ASK` reads "launch" costs; row 11 (tagged only), the WANT cue reads "(what) I'd want is", which starts the message classifier — allowed by Lorenzo's ruling 2 within ~2% of turns and ≤ 1.5 s: it fires on 2 of ≈ 1,400 corpus candidate turns (0.15%), 914 and 1,088 ms live, the wait capped at `ASK_WAIT_MS` 1,500 ms, logged `cue: 'want_is'` with latency and wait, and reported in its own table by `scripts/check-cost-report.ts`; row 13, BASIS reads "how … was / were estimated / derived / calculated" (not "how many / much"); row 14, CUT reads "by source"; row 16 (tagged only), a mention is a stated need, and so an offer, only with two distinctive label words in the need clause (case proper names aside), the item's spoken name, or an all-capitals label acronym of three or more letters ("CAC"; controller, proposed) — accepted by his ruling 3 once each lost offer was a soft want or still answered (§16); row 6 (tagged only), an unflagged mention whose item an addressed asking sentence names whole (`saysItemName`: every non-function word of a phrase of two or more words, proper names kept, per sentence) is an ask and released; row 17, on code-written turns a question-form sentence (or the clause after its last colon) naming a content word of an unreleased item's label is an asking sentence; row 21, a split / breakdown / "versus" ask of a `$` figure an item's line states once, with no parts of it, is outside that item's scope; M3, `figureAsWritten` keeps the decimal before a scale ("How did you get to $9.0M?"). Unscoped (no prof-001 corpus hit, so prof-001's replay is unchanged): rows 8, 13, 14, 17, 21 and M3; tagged only: rows 6, 11, 16 and X1. (f) **Must-reach** (case field `mustReach: [{ item, before }]`, `before` an exhibit id or `"recommendation"`; Lorenzo: "Must-reach mechanism. Code releases a case-declared item at a declared point if the candidate hasn't asked by then. prof-002 only for now: competitor_entry before Exhibit 3 (the network fix); new_route_rampup before the recommendation ask. The report should note when an item was volunteered."). prof-002 declares those two; no other case declares any. On a turn whose decisions hand over exhibit X (any path), or that asks for the recommendation (`rec_ask`, `time_warning`, `grace_ask`, or a model question `stageQuestions` reads as the recommendation ask), each item due there is released first in the data line, past the release cap and with no "On what you asked about earlier:" lead — unless it is out, released, restated or shown this turn, held by the release order, or volunteered before (`flags.mustReach`). At a point only Settle sees (an exhibit code hands over inside the question, a model recommendation ask) it goes at the end of the data line, led by "One more thing before the exhibit:" / "Before your recommendation:" when other lines precede it (controller, proposed). Never on `close` or `close_wait`. Logged `must_reach`; one `intervention` / `volunteered` event per heard item. The report: the judge's data section lists "<label> was volunteered by the interviewer (not asked for) (turn N)" and the judge doesn't credit the candidate for asking it; the live-run md has a "Volunteered by the interviewer" line. Must-reach changes a Larkspur wave's comparability with earlier ones. An item whose line wasn't heard is not volunteered again (§15). **Proof (r6-final2, §16):** of the audit's 22 data faults, every one marked "deterministic fix" is caught (row 11 with a live classifier call, row 17 coarsely); row 7 is a residual Lorenzo accepted; 0 new false refusals and 0 new wrong releases over 70 changed data turns; no added Haiku check call on any turn.
- **Verified figures + probe withholding (Rule 2 v4.5/v4.6)** — the live check also reports figures the candidate stated *correctly* (`recompute_ok`, with whether the work was shown); a pre-send pass withholds any doubt probe on a verified figure and any explain probe where the work was shown or already asked. prof-001 has verify-only bean steps (confirm, never flag). The unit-check hint is suppressed when the conversion was verified. Batch 2 had five doubt probes on correct math; batch 4 had none. **A stamp needs the claimed quantity (13 Oct; batch 21 stamped four wrong claims correct):** cues under six letters match as words ("rest" ≠ "restore"); a range end ("10–12%") verifies no step; a step whose answer the case prompt gives is a quote unless the step's inputs for one period are stated with it; shown work that mixes periods verifies no step; a flag wins in its sentence (no same-value stamp, no arithmetic stamp); stated arithmetic is `scope: 'arithmetic'` — its hint line (`arithmetic_ok`) and the judge's line ("ARITHMETIC HOLDS") say the operation holds, not that the claim is verified. The probe guard and the interviewer-error marks still treat an arithmetic stamp as verified work (Owen's conceded "58/42 = 1.38" stays protected).
- **One goodbye (Rule 12)** — every close is code-written (one scripted line, after answering the final message's addressed asks — see Turn kinds); a goodbye in the model's words is vetoed. Refusal / deferral / offer lists are spoken with each item's first letter lower-cased unless it looks like an acronym, number or name. A promise (deferral, hold, offer) drops period qualifiers ("…, both years", "over 2 years") and says an item asked for by its catalog label by the item's authored `spoken` name ("the coffee bean price change", "what changed on those two routes"), else a name derived from the label; refusals keep theirs (13 Oct, batch 21 D8). Fallback questions are case-neutral ("Which area would you dig into first?").
- **History shaping** — scripted silence check-ins and pause lines are kept out of the model's history (it copied them); the check-in restates the stored last question.
- **Pressure-test state and request guards (Rule 7, 7–8 Oct; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-pressure-test-and-request-guards.md`)** — code owns the pressure test: `not_asked → awaiting → satisfied` in `flags.pressureTest`; asked = a delivered question containing a Rule 7 probe (text, early stages; the `move` label is neither needed nor enough) in the model's own words or code's own probe — never a code-written fallback (13 Oct: "Which part of the problem would you dig into first?" had counted; 1 of 166 saved runs with turn files); satisfied = a Haiku 5.5 judge (`probe_judge`, beside the distress check, fails closed; Haiku 4.5 until 8 Oct) says a reply since the question answers it. **"Nothing is missing" (Lorenzo, 19 Oct; voice session 0f1b28ea):** to a what's-missing (MECE) probe, a short reply that says nothing is missing (`saysNothingMissing`: ≤ 40 words, no addition, hedge, "though"/"but" or named gap) counts as answered when the judge didn't count it — no re-ask, never the narrowed "name one thing your structure doesn't cover"; code asks which branch they'd start with, once (`ask_branch`), unless the model already asks where to start or the reply already says where they would; scoring is told (event `pressure_test_nothing_missing`) to judge completeness against the case's structure key and name any gap in the report. A re-ask whose lead names the structure says "it" (no "your structure … your structure"). Until satisfied, releases of data meant after CLARIFY (and exhibits showing it) become deferrals; scoping facts, the rung-3 rescue item and forced releases on rec-ask/grace/time-warning turns are unchanged. After it, pending requests are fulfilled up to the release cap. Bounded fallbacks: a duplicate probe gets one code re-ask, then rotating plain questions; a test the model never asks is asked by code once. **Guard B:** on an explicit data ask (phrasing detector, before the brainstorm — "I'd want:" with a colon too; from the brainstorm on, an addressed ask with the close's rhetorical-question filter, `lateAskCues`, so next steps don't arm it; 19 Oct, built on branch `engine-asks`, 19 Oct; not merged or deployed) `say` is held and a turn whose requests close empty is written once more. **Judge and structure check:** the judge counts only statements about the structure (data-request lists never count); a structure check (`structure_judge`) gates the code-asked probe — with no structure on the table code asks for one (once), a thin structure offered as the approach counts, and a check timeout asks nothing that turn. **Bound and waiver (behaviour spec v4.9 Rule 7; since 10 Oct, after batch 18 v2 wave 1):** every reply while the probe is open counts, whatever the judge says and whoever writes the turn; only a model turn carries a ladder step — reply 1 one code re-ask, reply 2 a narrowed probe, and on the first model turn at or after reply 3 a **waiver** (state `waived`, not satisfaction; "Let's leave the structure there."; held requests released through the cap, the rest deferred out loud; event `intervention / pressure_test_waived`). Actual maximum: reply 4 when stall rung 1 (once a session) lands on reply 3; the time warning, grace ask and recommendation ask can each add one and release open requests themselves. With no structure ever given: five held turns after code's structure ask → waiver ("Let's move on to the analysis."). Every gate-held ask is answered with its reason each turn; a model question about undelivered figures is replaced and delivery claims in `say` are withheld; the awaiting turn note shows in any stage. The judge reads the probe as heard (original + code's follow-ups); a hedged pick with a reason answers a which-first question, and declining one part of a compound probe doesn't cancel an answer to another. **Scoring** gets a neutral record of a waiver (`summarizePressureTest`): whether the probe was delivered, the questions and replies, and that code closed it — not an assist, not an unanswered question, not by itself a weakness; the never-asked path is reported as not administered.
- **Background math checker (live since batch 23, 13 Oct; `math-checker-live.ts`)** — on each candidate message with a quantitative claim, Plan starts the model check (Sonnet 5.5, `MATH_CHECKER_CONFIG`, "off" switches it off) beside the interviewer call; the turn never awaits it (the runner hands it to `defer` or leaves it running, and writes its `math_checker` verdict row only once the turn is accepted; a cancelled turn aborts it; errors and the 30 s timeout (Lorenzo) are logged — a row and a warning — with no effect). Later turns read landed verdicts: a flag on a claim the deterministic checks flagged in the message (recompute, pricing, unit, arithmetic, or a misquote code corrects) releases code's line and is itself dropped as a duplicate (the gate, next bullet); a checker "correct" on a code-flagged figure drops code's line (logged `disagrees_with_code`); a checker error on a stamped figure drops the stamp (and a word-quantity flag carries no figures — its error is the words); a claim the candidate has since withdrawn ("let me redo", "that's my error"), revised (a sentence opening "Actually," with a figure) — a concession withdraws only the claim it answers: the one the interviewer just questioned (the checker line delivered on that turn, or the model asking about its figures), one whose own figure the message names, or one the message redoes to its right figure (Lorenzo, 14 Oct; batch 24 Owen, Marisol) — a right figure that is also a case figure counts only in a sentence naming the claim's quantity (the checker's `quantity`, less plain words and "dollar"; Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 26 Marisol 2456aa9d t7's "Let me redo it in dollars", about COGS, said the revenue growth 15%, her labor-dollars claim's right figure), restated (same pricing claim; the same math step — since 19 Oct only at a different value: said again at its own figure the error stands, Lorenzo's ruling R2, built on branch `engine-asks`, 19 Oct; not merged or deployed) or answered with the right figure is stale and dropped. The right figure includes its equivalent forms (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 25): "1+x = 1.237" is 23.7%; for a "needed" price claim, a price stated as the answer ("about a 24% price increase", not a scenario like "a 10% rise recovers…") to the nearest point, even when it is also a case figure; the price for the prompt's prior margin (a redo for another target — Owen's "8-12%" to recover 16 points redone as 24% for the 24% margin); and the right figure on the other period's revenue, said with its base ("18.4 scaled to today's revenue" for 16 points). **Since round 4 a figure closes a flag as stale only when it is stated for the claim's own quantity** (Lorenzo, 19 Oct, ruling 3: "a figure closes a flag as stale only when it's stated for the claim's own quantity. That's my 15 Oct redo rule applied to the stale check"; b33 Blake cb23e17b t17, whose "6 to 7 points" was closed on the "10" of "a 10–12% price increase"; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed). The figure must be in a sentence naming that quantity: its quantity words, or, for a code-computed pricing kind, the words that name it (`KIND_CUE`: gain / recover / margin; margin; volume / break-even / elasticity / traffic; need / takes / price). The kind words are the controller's reading, proposed and awaiting Lorenzo. The figure is never a price that a gain, new-margin or break-even claim was computed from. This applies in the "right figure stated" branch, the rebased branch and the redo's figures. The R2 "restated" branch was unchanged, so b33 Marisol 15:2 still closed there until round 5 (below). A figure of the same message built on an answered one (its sum or difference with a figure beside it that isn't itself flagged — Marisol's "6.3" = 16.0 − 9.7) goes with it; a restatement answers nothing. **Round 5: each figure is tied to its quantity** (E6; Lorenzo, 20 Oct: "the stale reader also closes flags it shouldn't … Don't widen the synonym match. Tie each figure to its quantity at the figure level (L7's residual) and prove it on both the missed and the false-close cases."; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed; `figure-binding.ts`). `boundFigures` ties each number in a later message to the phrase it modifies. That is the noun phrase right after it ("600 customers", "$4.5 million of first-year profit"), else the one before it, reached by walking back over arithmetic and linking words ("fixed costs $3.0 + $4.5 + $1.5 = $9.0 million"). A figure beside ×, /, + or − is an operand (an input); one after = or ≈ is a result; "it / that / this" refers back. A figure closes a flag only when it is no operand and is tied to the claim's own quantity: by the checker's quantity words (the phrase's head noun one of them), code's kind words, or the step's case `cues`, and for pricing at the same price. `namesClaim`, `redoneToRight` and the R2 restated branch read per figure the same way. A figure tied to no quantity (no phrase, plain words only, or a pronoun with no claim figure before it) falls back to its sentence, the pre-E6 rule (F1). Without F1 the binder turned 7 real prof-001 redos into stale probes or corrections (r5-46). F1 and the binder's own rules were controller rulings, approved by Lorenzo (20 Oct: "Accepted as built: E6 binder rules"): the head-noun test; the checker quantity's conditions dropped (with, at, from, by, given, if…, but not vs / versus); code's kind words name the checker's value only with its quantity; "from" joins a phrase; an operand's own noun of up to three words is part of its chain; an operand labelled by "at" or a colon counts as stated. Logged, not built (Lorenzo, 20 Oct): b35q Marisol 7:2, "Then LTV = 30 months × $80 monthly margin = $2,400", is Pro's LTV ("Pro's" only in the sentence before) and still closes her Starter $1,675 — a false close that needs cross-sentence subject binding. No engine-wide synonyms: a vocabulary miss goes into the case's step cues (launch-001's `launch_costs_total` takes "fixed costs", Lorenzo). Fixed false closes: Quillpath Marisol t31's "600 customers" against her $900 LTV; Tyler t29's "0.20 × $4,000" against the 4× ratio and "$676" at $60 against "$405" at $40; the residuals b33 Marisol 15:2, b30 Blake 23:0 and b32 Larkspur Marisol 17:2. Fixed misses: Hollis Bay Tyler t11's "$9.0 million" against 8.8 and t13's "$4.5 million of first-year profit" against $3.9M; Aldermoor Tyler t13's "$240 million" against $225M. b33 Blake t17 stays not stale, and every redo test passes. In `math-spans.ts` a not-cue opening with a symbol ("$60") now matches after a space, and one ending in a digit no longer matches a longer number ("$60" in "$600"). **The correction follows the wrong input (Lorenzo, 19 Oct, §15 (o); built on branch `engine-asks`, 19 Oct; not merged or deployed; batch 32 Aldermoor Marisol's $2,000 survived 6:32 → 20:37 behind its derived figures):** a flagged claim built on another step's wrong figure (`rootOf`: its span carries a registered error's figure — not a case figure, not its own, stated no later) gets no probe of its own — it rides on the root's line (`derived`) on the turn the root is said, else it waits; a raised, open root carried into a new message comes back there with its next Rule-14 attempt (Claude's reading of (o), Lorenzo's by his round-2 ruling 8); once the root closes the claim goes to the report ("built on a closed input") — unless the root was corrected live, or (since round 5b) its own correction can be said. **R7 reversed** (round 5b; Lorenzo, 20 Oct: "R7 reversed: when a root goes to the report without a code line, the derived claim gets its own vetted correction once, through own-quantity framing (b33 Marisol 11:0 "about 4.2 points of revenue, not 5.8"). The root probe at t12 stays."; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed): when the root goes to the report with no code line said, a claim built on it takes the root's place in the queue for its own correction, once — `framedCorrection`, or the row fix for a claim that owns a misread input; no probe, pointer or hint; the decision is `correct` on the claim's own key and names the root. A minor one is shed under time pressure, as a flag is. With no true line, or when that line was said once already, it goes to the report as before ("built on a closed input"). **One derived correction per root** (round 5c; Lorenzo, 20 Oct: "3. Ruling 11 changed: one derived correction per root, not per derived claim; the root's other derived claims go to the report."; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed; replaces round-5b controller ruling 11, "per derived claim"): the first claim in the queue's order gets the root's one correction; the root is booked in `flags.mathChecker.derivedOnce` (root → claim id; no migration), kept in the undo snapshot so a line not heard unbooks it; every once path (the closed root, freed claims, E7's in-loop and waiting paths) checks it first, and the root's other derived claims go to the report ("one correction per root, said already (ID)"), a second claim in the same turn on the next turn, once the booking stands. The report line reads the root's outcome and quotes the correction said; an E7 input root's report text reads that root's decisions (`onRootOf`). A conversion said on the once path counts as the root's one derived line (controller, proposed). The root's own line and ruling 1's restatement are untouched. b33 Marisol 31e33506: the t12 root probe stays, and t14 says "Bean inflation's contribution to the COGS increase, in points of revenue, is about 4.2 points, not 5.8." **A misread input is corrected by its third probe** (round 5, E7; Lorenzo: "Quillpath Marisol's probe chain: serious" and "For Quillpath Marisol the true line is the row fix ("Starter's 12-month cancellation is 40%; 21.5% is Pro's"), never "about 5%, not 21.5%" against her annual reading. If code can't write a true line, it goes to the report."; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed). B2 Quillpath Marisol swapped the two cohort rows (Pro's 21.5% read as Starter's, Starter's 40% as Pro's). Both are case figures, which the checker drops when keying and linking claims, so each figure built on them got its own first probe: 9 probe turns before a correction. Now a claim's own figure stated in the data's unit, as read (not computed) and next to the data's own words for it, is a misread input (`dataFigure`), unless she uses it for its own row. A claim links to the input when its span carries it in its unit or as a fraction, when it carries a linked claim's figure, or when another flagged claim in the same message states the input (controller; approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct: "the E7 co-occurrence rule"). Probes on the input or its chain, by code or by the model, are counted together (`flags.inputProbes`, undone like `stepProbes` when a line isn't heard). At two, the next code line on the input is the row fix, written from the exhibit's cells: "At month 12, 21.5% of Pro customers and 40% of Starter customers had cancelled." (Lorenzo's wording, approved 20 Oct; built from the exhibit's column, row labels and cells: a row label that is one past-tense word, "Cancelled", gives "had cancelled"; any other label, or a cell without %, keeps "The <label> figure at <column> is A for X and B for Y."). Every figure in it must already be out in its unit (FR-4). A chain claim made before the fix is covered by it; one made after hears it once more (round-2 ruling 1), then goes to the report. A claim whose own figure is a misread input never hears `framedCorrection`'s "not X": it gets the row fix, else a pointer, hint or the report. With no row fix possible, the input and the claim go to the report and no third probe is said. The model's own probe on a closed input is withheld (`closed_to_report`; controller, proposed). Replay: Marisol hears the row fix at t12, and nothing after it probes 21.5%, 40% or their chain; every other slice, prof-001 included, is unchanged. **The correction is restated once** (Lorenzo's round-2 ruling 1, 19 Oct; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed; b28 Jordan t19, b29 Jordan t9): code records a root closed by a delivered correction (`flags.mathChecker.corrected`, the line said and its right figure), and the first later claim still on the root's wrong value — one made before the correction was said counts too (the controller's reading, his by his round-3 acceptance) — is selected once as `restate`, its line **the root's delivered line, word for word** — a correction or a conversion line, never a rebuilt "X, not Y" (Lorenzo's round-3 ruling 1, 19 Oct: "The restatement must repeat the original correction line verbatim, never a new "X, not Y" template."; built on `engine-asks`): b28/b29 Jordan now hear "COGS rose 16 points, from 42% to 58% of revenue; 38% is its growth on its own base." again, not "…16 points of revenue, not 38" (a "not X" on a figure true as growth on its own base). A conversion records its delivered line too; a root with no recorded line is never restated; the report never calls a root a conversion closed "an earlier wrong figure". It takes the turn's one math line like a correction, ranks below a conclusion-changing flag and above age (the controller's middle ordering, his by his round-3 acceptance — the trade-off: Aldermoor Marisol 11:5's first probe expires behind it), and rides on an overdue stage turn (R7); a waiting flag whose own correction is that root's delivered line word for word is closed by it (the controller's option (c), his by his round-3 acceptance). Later such claims go to the report ("built on a corrected input — restated once already"). Ruling 1 covers claims built on a root: a same-key restatement after its correction keeps "restated after the flag closed" (controller, his by his round-3 acceptance). **A correction is said at most twice in all** (Lorenzo's round-3 ruling 2, 19 Oct: "the 13 Oct recommendation re-correction counts as the restatement, so a correction is said at most twice in total"; built on `engine-asks`): the original and one more — a restatement or the 13 Oct re-correction below, not both. A heard re-correction marks its key, and every key whose line is the same, restated; a key restated (or sharing a restated line) gets no re-correction. The count is kept per key and per delivered line (`flags.mathChecker.said`, the line's normalised text; per line too was a round-3 controller ruling, approved with the round-3 decisions 1–5): a correction, restatement or re-correction whose line was said twice is not said, and its key closes report-only ("its correction was already said twice"). b29 Jordan's third saying (t20) is gone; the risk question it displaced is back. A restatement that ends shed, expired or unresolved at the close carries its root, and its report text quotes the root's correction. A root closed when its attempts ran out keeps "built on a closed input"; code's own finding on it yields to a raised root; the report reads it as "built on an earlier wrong figure". Since round 5b a claim on a root left for the report that is shed, expired or unresolved at the close records that root, and the report reads "…built on an earlier wrong figure of theirs, which was probed and left for this report." Concessions include "I was loose/sloppy", "my earlier range/number/estimate was wrong/off/understated", "I made the same mistake again". A later "correct" verdict on a restatement of an open flag's result (its first figure or word quantity, the same named quantity) closes it only when code's own figure for the flag's quantity agrees with the checker's (3% or 0.5, the corrections' rule); otherwise it is logged `disagrees_with_code` and the flag stays open for its next attempt (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; Jasmine's "a third"). At most one checker flag a turn, conclusion-changing first, none beside a deterministic flag or a misquote correction; under Rule 14 with the deterministic checks' shared counter (key: the step id, the pricing kind, else the claim's words; a model probe on the claim since counts as the probe; **at most two probes per step per interview, whatever the value** — `flags.stepProbes` counts code's probes and the checker's probe, pointer and hint per step (the key without its value); a capped step gets code's correction if it can write one, else the report, and its flag closes; a word quantity — half, a third, a quarter, a fifth, twice — identifies a restated claim like a figure; Lorenzo, 13 Oct, Larkspur ruling c): first the vetted probe, then code's correction, else the pointer probe, else the hint probe, else unresolved for the report; time pressure corrects a conclusion-changing flag at once and sheds a minor one. It reaches the model as a MATH CHECK FLAG hint on a model turn; on other code-written turns it waits — up to two later messages (Lorenzo), then it expires to the report. **A line counts as delivered only if it is heard (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 26 Jasmine 074b0534 t31: the probe on "about 5 points" was logged as used and the model asked for the recommendation instead):** Settle checks the delivered text holds the vetted line (`lineHeard`: case, quotes, dashes and spacing aside; a paraphrase is not the line — it also skipped the vetting); if the model's question left it out, code's line replaces the question (a correction goes before it — "say" is already spoken); if a pressure-test step or a stage code administers took the question (a stage hold wins), Plan's booking is undone — attempt, handled list, step count — the decision is logged `undelivered`, and the flag stays pending for a later turn (check `checker_line`). **A due stage question wins over a checker probe (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 26 Jordan 6bd9edf3 t19 lost its risk question to "Walk me through that calculation."):** a stage is due at or past its deadline (§5.2: the brainstorm at the exhibit budget's end, the recommendation at the brainstorm budget's end) or after the time warning, the risk question once the recommendation is in — and only while the stage is still outstanding; not merely because the model asked it (Plan's `stagesDue`). When the model's own question asks a due stage (`stageQuestions` in stage-evidence.ts, the stage record's own reading) and leaves the probe out, the question stays and the probe is held back: the booking is undone, the decision logged `undelivered` with the due stage named, and `flags.mathChecker.held` keeps its id — a held line doesn't expire, is raised first on the next turn, and code's coverage-triggered recommendation ask yields that turn to it (as a deadline yields once to a probe). A line is held at most once: if the model again asks a due stage without it, code says the line first, then the stage question. A correction is no probe — it goes first and the stage question follows, the same turn. At the close a held line holds the close one turn whether or not it changes the conclusion ("Before we wrap up — " and the line), at most once an interview (`flags.heldCloseHold`, a counter apart from `recMathProbed`), never at time-up. **The final answers are checked before the close** (Lorenzo, 16 Oct; `close-hold.ts`, `flags.closeHold`): from the recommendation reply (the first message after the recommendation ask the stage record holds) on, a checker flag holds the close whatever its rating — code's "Before we wrap up — " probe, or Rule 14's correction — once per figure (the same key, or its step at the same value; the checker's line heard on a model turn counts); the closing message's own deterministic flag goes first; such a flag is never shed under time pressure nor expired by the two-message wait. The redo — the reply to code's probe (any `rec_math_probe`) — holds nothing and is not waited on. A final answer the checker ran on whose verdict hasn't landed holds the close one turn (`close_wait`: the stage's natural follow-up), never for the follow-up's own answer or the redo, nor twice in a row. **At most two wrap-up probes an interview** (every code "Before we wrap up" probe or correction, `closeHold.probeTurns`; Lorenzo, 16 Oct): the recommendation reply's figures first, then the largest relative error; the rest go to the report, and with none left there is no follow-up. Only time-up overrides it. Before the recommendation the earlier rules stand. The hold acts on the checker's vetted **flag**, not on a bare "error" verdict: an error code's vetting clears (low confidence, within tolerance, a word quantity that holds — "most of the gap" for 8.5 of 18 points) holds nothing. None goes out on a turn where code flags or corrects a misquote (Lorenzo). **The close holds one turn** for an unresolved conclusion-changing flag on a message before the recommendation (code says "Before we wrap up — " and the line; once a case, not when time is up); a root's rating there is the highest riding on it (Lorenzo's round-2 ruling 4, 19 Oct; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed): the selection carries the highest conclusion-changing rating, final-answer flag and hold rank among its riders, so a minor root whose rider changes the conclusion or sits in the final answers holds the close, once per root (`probedAlready` and `heldCloseHold` keyed by the root; its line heard on a model turn counts as its one extra final-answer probe — a round-2 controller ruling, his by his round-3 acceptance); anything left at the close goes to the report. Scoring adds a MATH CHECKER section (each flag, code's figure when code wrote the correction, its outcome), drops checker-flagged stamps and treats a doubt on a checker-flagged figure as supported. A verdict whose own derivation finds the candidate right ("Candidate's figure is correct.") is no flag (13 Oct, Larkspur). A checker probe asks only about the candidate's own claim, in two steps (Lorenzo, 13 Oct; the two-step rule from his Step-4 rulings): the first probe names only what is in the claim (the sentence of the message the checker's span comes from, and the sentence before it, which "That …" refers back to) plus the target (the prompt's % and $ figures and their changes, so "Does that account for the full 18 points?" may be said). A draft that names a case component (a content word of the case's ledger and exhibit text, less plain words and the prompt's) or a figure outside that is trimmed of its last ask when what is left passes ("…against the 18, and where does overhead sit?" → "…against the 18?"), else replaced by the neutral fallback. **Period words and the prompt's figures don't lead** (Lorenzo, 19 Oct, round-4 ruling 5: "Period words and figures already in the case prompt don't count as leading"; b33 Marisol 31e33506 t9, where "a 40% rise over two years" was withheld as "names 2"; built on `engine-asks`). Every figure in the case prompt counts as theirs. A period phrase ("two years", "2 years", "18 months") is set aside only when the prompt uses that period, and a year or FY label only when the prompt names that year. That narrowing is a controller ruling, proposed: a duration the prompt never uses can be a leaked answer. "Year one" and "last / this / next year" are always set aside. An unrevealed or right figure still withholds the probe. The untrimmed draft is kept as the **hint**, Rule 14's second probe, when it names only components and figures the candidate raised in any message; it is asked only at the second attempt, when code can't correct and no pointer passed (the pointer keeps its rule: it carries a revealed figure). No probe carries arithmetic. The neutral fallback quotes their figure only when it is a short figure ("Walk me through how you got to 10–12%."), else "Walk me through that calculation." / "…how you got there." for a word quantity. **A late bare probe quotes its claim** (Lorenzo, 19 Oct, round-4 ruling 4: "A bare probe said later than the turn right after its claim must quote the claim"; b33 Blake cb23e17b t22; built on `engine-asks`). A probe, pointer or hint is bare when it names no figure, no three words of its claim, and doesn't put the claim to them. Said later than the turn right after its claim (every checker line, since a verdict lands a turn late), it first quotes the claim in the candidate's own words. That is the claim's own figure as written, when it has one that is not a case figure ("You said “$4.8M” — what were the steps in that calculation?"). Otherwise it is at most 15 of their words around the claim, with "…" where cut. The cap is a controller ruling for voice length, approved by Lorenzo (20 Oct). A model line that already carries the claim's own figure, or attributes the claim with its words, counts as heard. **The probe's opener varies, wording only** (round-4 ruling 6: "build them only if wording-only, with the quoted words unchanged"). The leading "You said" of a checker line, whether code's quoted form or the checker's own, rotates through You said / You mentioned / Earlier you said / You put it at / You had (pool `probe_opener`, no repeat within the last 3 uses). The quote and the rest of the line are byte-identical. The last two lead-ins are used only before the claim's own figure. The heard check and fact-check's echo reader treat every lead-in as "you said". One error, one count (Lorenzo, 13 Oct): a restated error joins its flag, matched by the candidate's own figures (a registry in the session's `mathChecker` flags). The count is the stored counter or the interviewer turns since the error was first stated that asked about its figures, whichever is higher, so the model's own probes count. After two probes code says its correction once; otherwise the error goes to the report ("Probed twice; not corrected — left for this report."). The flag then closes: the probe guard withholds any interviewer sentence about its figures (`closed_to_report`), and the model is told to move on. Code also writes a correction for a relative change stated as points (`relativeAsPoints`), whatever quantity the checker named — for a revealed share line named in the claim's span, else anywhere in the claim's message (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; Jordan 78c89dba t5), never for the prompt's own figures or a line's own change in points. **A closed error said again in the final recommendation gets one more flag** (Lorenzo, 13 Oct; Jordan 099f33e9 t25): in the reply to a recommendation ask, code matches a closed flag's figures in a unit the candidate said them in (from the session's `math_checker` flag rows: "about 40 points" matches, "inputs ran 40%" does not; the checker's own verdict on that message lands after the close, so it isn't waited for). It goes through the `rec_math_probe` path: code's correction again with "Does that change your recommendation?" when it wrote one — unless that correction has been said twice already, or its key was restated (round-3 ruling 2, above: the re-correction is the restatement), else "Before we wrap up — walk me through how you got to <figure>." (a figure said as "38-point" is said back "38 points") Once a case (shared with the other recommendation math flags), after a misquote correction and a deterministic flag, and not when time is up. The figure must be said in a unit the closed claim was said in — money, %, points — never a bare number (Larkspur: a closed "two separate effects" matched "$2M in year one"). Code-written lines say "an" before a figure spoken with a vowel sound (`articleFor` in `lib/number-words.ts`: an 8%, an 11%, an 18%, an 80%, an 800, an 8,000; a 1%, a 10%, a 100) — the pricing probes (`recompute.ts`) and code's corrections (`codeCorrection`). **Units and scale in code's lines (round 5, E3;** Lorenzo: "Unit/scale slips in code lines: false, not serious. "$210" for $210k counts too."; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed). `codeCorrection` reads the step's `scale`. A `usd` step at `scale: 'million'` says "about $9 million, not $8.8 million" for a bare "8.8" or "$8.8" (B2 Hollis Bay Tyler t12 heard "about $9, not 8.8"); a figure written with M, million, mil or mm keeps its form; one written in full ("$8,800,000") puts both sides in units. The "not X" side carries the same unit and scale (Lorenzo): a bare money figure takes "$", separators and the scale. A bare percent or points figure stays bare, because code's unit there would assert a base the candidate didn't state (controller, approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct). A step can be `unit: 'ratio'` (pricing-001's LTV/CAC steps): its line says "about 2 times, not 2.5 times", never "$2" (Quillpath Marcus t22), and a ratio claim is keyed by its x / times figure (`starter_ratio@2.5`, not `@750`). A quoted figure keeps its suffix (k, K, thousand, M, million, bn, billion): "You put it at “$210k”", not "$210" (Quillpath Tyler t28). prof-001's correction lines (points, percent, price rise) are byte-identical (pinned in tests). The grammar "costs … is" stays logged (L14).
- **Step-check roots (round 6, 21 Oct; Lorenzo: "Step check. The deterministic step check may, without the checker agreeing: link derived claims to the flagged root and stop probing them; ask one neutral input question at the root (for example, "Which row did you use for cancellations?"). It may NOT voice a correction without the checker's agreement."; `math-checker-live.ts`, `plan-turn.ts`, `settle-turn.ts`; built on `engine-asks` 027a8591, 7a698ed2, 328a96d3, 6b664619, e51a4841, not merged or deployed).** It runs only on cases whose math steps declare `dependsOn` (step ids, read transitively; validated known and acyclic) or `inputQuestion` (ends in "?", no digit: no figure, no arithmetic). Only pricing-001 does: `starter_ltv` asks "Which row of the Starter cohort table did you use for cancellations?", `pro_ltv` "Which row of the Pro cohort table did you use for cancellations?", and twelve Starter steps depend on `starter_ltv` (controller ruling, proposed: the other cases' chains aren't capped yet). prof-001's steps declare neither, so it is unchanged. **Roots:** a deterministic step flag (live, or `recompute_report_only`) on a root step — one with an input question, or one other steps depend on — at the candidate's value v registers `step:R@v` (logged `step_root`; `flags.stepRoots` with each root's linked keys). Its figures are v plus the flag's span figures that are no case figure, no step's right answer and no one-digit integer. A step flag on a dependent step, or one carrying a root figure, links rather than registering a root of its own. **Linking** (in `planCheckerFlags`, before selection): a checker or code claim is linked when its step depends on R (while the root stands), when its span carries a root figure that isn't its own, or when it is built (`rootOf`) on a key already linked. A linked claim is never probed, never a rider, never given a pointer or hint; its key closes, and the recommendation re-flag skips it; a released code flag linked to a root says nothing (`step_root.codeLinked`). The root's own claims (R at v) are not linked: the checker probes or corrects them as always. **Expiry** (links by dependency only; figure and `rootOf` links stay): a root stops linking by dependency once R is restated at a different wrong value — outside both v's tolerance and the right answer's (the step's absolute tolerance, $60 for `starter_ltv`); that value's own flag registers a new root — or once the root key is closed or corrected, or once a dependent claim's span carries R's right answer (figures read at their own scale), counting checker claims only from the root's registration on. A restatement at the right answer ends nothing by itself (Marcus's hypothetical "LTV around $600" while he kept 14 months; the proof's finding, e51a4841; controller ruling, proposed). **Corrections:** linking stops probes only. A linked claim's checker-agreed correction still goes out, once per root step (`flags.mathChecker.derivedOnce['step:R']`, round 5c's one derived correction per root; controller ruling, proposed); the root's own correction (the checker's flag on R at v) is separate. **The step check never voices a correction:** every correction still needs the checker's flag. **The input question:** once per root step per interview (`flags.stepRootAsked`), said in the checker probe's slot as a `probe` row (`id: step:R@v`), on a model turn with no checker line, code math line or misquote correction, not under time pressure, with the step under its two-probe cap and the root at most two messages old. It counts as one step probe and the checker's attempt 1 on `R@v`, so the checker's next line on R is its correction. Code says it when the model leaves it out; unheard, its booking is undone; it is never held for a due stage. A fresh root's question goes before a linked claim's derived correction (`holdLinked`, logged `step_root_first`; controller, proposed). **Report:** a linked claim reads "Not raised on its own: built on starter_ltv's 405, flagged by the step check — left for this report." Report-only step flags register roots and may ask the question (controller ruling, for Lorenzo: a false binding asks an unneeded neutral question, §15). **Replay r6-final2 (b37q):** Raj's eight chained lines became the input question (t11), one derived correction ("$1,400, not $1,205", t19), two residual probes ($940k t27, $905k t29) and the root's own correction ("$600, not $405", t35): a ladder ending in the input's correction, at most firm by his rule. Jasmine: the input question, a derived correction and the root's own correction. Marcus (M2): improved, not caught (§15).
- **The checker gate on code's own lines (16 Oct, Lorenzo; batch 29 Marisol 64224460 t15 — "Other inputs rose 11.8 [points]", right, got code's misquote line "To correct one figure: other input costs … are up 37.5%", while the checker rated the message all correct; `gateCodeFindings` in `math-checker-live.ts`).** No code-written correction (a misquote's data line, a unit slip's correction) and no code probe (a deterministic step, arithmetic, pricing or unit flag — its RECOMPUTE hint, or code's "Before we wrap up — …") is said unless the checker also rates that claim wrong: its vetted flag on a claim of the same message, matched by key or by the figure in the claim's value (as the dedupe matches). A misquote's line also needs the checker's right figure to be one the data line states or a change it implies (3% or 0.5), so a flag on another reading releases nothing; a unit line names its quantity and period, so the flag is enough. The message's own verdict never lands within its turn (no in-turn wait — Lorenzo), so code's line goes out a turn later at the earliest, carried in the verdict row (`codeFlags[].finding`) and released by the first Plan after it lands; the attempt counter, the step-probe cap, the doubt licence (`flaggedFigures`, `flaggedThisTurn`) and the deadlines' yield to a math line all count released lines only. A verdict that rates the claim correct (`checker_correct`), doesn't rate it (`not_rated`: no claim on the figure, unverifiable, low confidence, a flag the vetting dropped), or flags it on another reading (`checker_disagrees`) drops the line; so does an answer since (`stale`) or a wait past two messages (`expired`, never in the final answers). Every decision is a `code_gate` event and check; a message the checker doesn't run on (no quantitative claim, or the checker off) gets no code line at all. A misquote the checker rates correct clears its figures from the truth check's echoes. A delayed probe names the figure as the candidate said it ("You said a 10% price rise recovers 5 points of margin. Walk me through how you sized that."; "You put the 25% share of COGS at 25 points — points of what?"). The recommendation reply's lines (`rec_revision`, `rec_math_probe`, the closed-error flag) go out on the turn after the reply, from the reply's released findings; a closed error restated in the reply needs the reply's verdict to flag that figure. At the close, a final answer's code flag holds the close once it is released; the closing message's own findings make it a pending final answer (the follow-up, `close_wait`, as built); at time-up they go to the report. **The growth probe** ("the margin problem, or normal growth?", the model's own sentence from RIGOR) is withheld unless it — or the sentence before it — quotes a figure a landed flag covers (`growth_probe_unchecked`), so never of the message it answers; the prompt says the same. Not gated: the period question (a figure right on the candidate's own base — Lorenzo, 15 Oct), which asserts no error.
- **Corrections on the candidate's own quantity (17 Oct, Lorenzo; session 9ccf0836 T24, batch 30 Noor t16; `own-quantity.ts`, read in `planCheckerFlags` when a correction is chosen).** Code says "not X" only when the candidate stated their figure in code's quantity or unit. What they stated is read from the claim's sentence, the one before it and their next two messages: their figure with a unit attached ("6 points of revenue", "6 revenue points", "$100M" for a dollar step, the step's own noun for a count; a bare "6 points" only where the quantity has no other points reading — a margin gain, a points step without a share base — and never on a share-base step, where it is ambiguous: Lorenzo, 17 Oct), a margin named in the claim's sentence for a pricing claim, a base named for their figure ("10 points of the 37.5 percent increasing COGS", "10% of the increase") or for their units ("in points of COGS, not in points of revenue" — a negated unit counts for neither), or a cost line taken back to a level ("COGS of 58 falls back toward 42"). Four outcomes: (1) **right on their own quantity** (3% / 0.5; a revenue-points step read on the share base's level — prof-001's COGS at 42 for a change or prior step, 58 for a current one — or as a share of that line's increase; a price rise read as current share / target − 1): one code-written conversion line — "In revenue terms, that's about 4.2 points." for a revenue-points step, else code's correction without its "not X" ("From today's 6% margin, recovering 16 points of margin takes about a 20.5% price rise."); (2) **code's quantity or unit stated**: today's correction; (3) **wrong on a base they stated**: the correction on their quantity ("In COGS terms, bean inflation's contribution to the COGS increase is about 10 points, not 15."; "Getting COGS back to 42% of revenue takes about a 38.1% price rise, not 45%."); (4) **no quantity stated**: if their figure is right on a reading code computes (`readingsOf`: code's quantity; for a revenue-points step its share base — points of COGS on the step's base level, a share of the line's increase; for a price rise, a cost line back to its prior level), the conversion as in (1) and the step closes; if it is wrong on every reading, code's figure without "not X" (logged `correct`) and the step stays open for the normal probes (Lorenzo, 17 Oct). A conversion is logged `converted` (with how the figure was framed) and closes the step: its key, the marker `converted:<step>` (a later checker flag on the step is left for the report, `unresolved`, when it restates a converted figure or is right on a reading; a new figure wrong on every reading reopens it — the probe first, "not X" later only with code's unit stated: Lorenzo, 17 Oct), code's deterministic probes on the step (capped), and the candidate's figure (`convertedFigures` in checker memory → `closedFigures`, so the model's own doubts on it are withheld, `closed_to_report` — needed because a figure that is also a case figure, like 9ccf0836's "10" = 25 × 40%, no key keeps). A closed-error restatement never raises a converted key. A model sentence that negates a converted figure ("not 38", "isn't 38%", "rather than 38", "instead of 38") is withheld like an unsupported doubt (`negates_converted`, `vetoReason`, both fields); the checker line then counts as unheard and code says it itself. The report leaves a conversion out of the checker's WRONG list (the figure was right on a reading). The conversion lines stay neutral as written — no "Right," or "That works" (Lorenzo, 17 Oct). A relative change stated as points ("COGS rose 16 points, from 42% to 58% of revenue; 38% is its growth on its own base.") has no "not X" and goes out as written. Unchanged: the unit check's lines (they fire only on a figure said in revenue or margin units — code's unit — and are already worded on the candidate's base) and the misquote line (a quote of a named ledger item). The own-quantity readings exist only where the case has share bases or a pricing model (prof-001); on the new cases a correction is "not X" only with code's unit stated, else code's figure without it. **Round 4 (Lorenzo, 19 Oct, ruling 1; b33 Marisol 31e33506 t14, where "In COGS terms, … about 10 points, not 5.8" was said on her revenue-points 5.8; built on `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed):** the base the candidate named is read first.
  - "Points of the N-point COGS increase" is revenue points when N is the COGS line's change in revenue points (16 on prof-001, within rounding). A form without N names no base. Holding to his exact words, rather than the plan's "close variants", is a controller ruling, approved by Lorenzo (20 Oct).
  - "Points of revenue" said with their figure anywhere in the window wins over a COGS reading.
  - **The guard:** "In COGS terms … not X" fires only when every mention of their figure that carries a unit names the COGS base. A mention names it through an allowlist (controller; approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct): "of" (or "in … terms"); then only the / old / current / today's / total / prior / our / its / two years ago's; the base; at most one of line / base / spend / costs / terms / bill; then the phrase's end. The sentence must carry no COGS change or level in revenue points (16; 58 / 42).
  - Otherwise the checker's revenue-terms line is said where the revenue base is established. Where neither base is established, code's figure is said with no "not X". This departs from his literal "use the checker's own revenue-terms line", which would be a "not X" on an unestablished base (controller; this departure approved by Lorenzo, 20 Oct).
  - 9ccf0836 T24 still gets its conversion line.
- **The pointer passes the probes' leads check (18 Oct, Lorenzo; batch 31 Owen 8b041a8f t16: "…the cost structure in Exhibit A, where COGS is 58% of revenue and labor and overhead are largely fixed?" — an assumption stated as an exhibit fact, and leading; `vetCheckerClaims`).** A pointer is withheld when it names a case component or a figure outside the claim's own sentences and the prompt's figures (`probeLeads` against the claim's span, as the first probe), besides the earlier rules (a question, provenance and truth, naming a revealed figure). Its turn then gets the second-attempt hint, or the flag goes to the report. On the corpus every pointer said (5) is withheld — two get the hint, three go to the report (§16).
- **Code's lines vary: five versions of every line without case facts (17 Oct item 4a; widened 18 Oct, brief B; `lib/orchestrator/phrasing.ts`; the full list `docs/code-line-variants.md`, written by `scripts/variant-list.ts`).** One registry, `PHRASE_POOLS`, holds 55 pools of five versions that differ in sentence structure, the first the line said before: the data-line connecting words (deferral, refusal, exhibit handover, restatement — two since round 5: "As I mentioned" for an item said, and `restate_exhibit`, "As the exhibit shows", for one that came out only on an exhibit (D6) — earlier-ask lead-in, misquote lead-ins, the gate's hold, the unmapped hold, the offer), the wrap-up lead, code's structure probe (lead + one of three asks), its re-asks, narrowed asks and waivers, the structure ask, the bounded fallbacks by phase, the "how did you get to {x}" question, the loop guard's move-on lines, the stage asks (exhibit, generic brainstorm, recommendation, risk, late recommendation, grace, time warning), the revision questions, the close-hold follow-ups, the stall lead, the opener's hand-off and the close. `{x}` (the ledger line, an exhibit title, what was asked, the candidate's figure) is verbatim; `{X}` opens a sentence; no version makes the slot a verb's subject (what was asked is often plural). **Choice:** no version again within its pool's last 3 uses in a session (`flags.phraseUses`, written by Settle from the heard text, so a cut line isn't counted); the first use is the original line, except the pools that rotated by seed before (`SEEDED_FIRST`: fallbacks, stage asks, revisions, follow-ups, opener, close), which keep pickScript's first choice. A pure function of the uses and the turn's seed, so Stream and Settle agree. **Held to the lines said before** (`tests/orchestrator/code-line-variants.test.ts`, against the registry as of club-beta-2, `fixtures/code-lines-before-b.json`): every version has the figures and case terms of an original and adds none — no lead, no new claim — and reads to every live and scoring recognizer (about 40: the close cue, rec/risk/brainstorm asks, stage questions, probe and ask intents, the close-hold regexes, where-to-start, the model-echo vetoes, delivery and reveal promises, grading, the style audit, growth probes, math talk, F1/F3/F4, the evaluators' patterns) exactly as an original does; each heard version is recorded as itself. **Recognizers take every version:** `poolPattern` reads a slot anywhere in a line (a lead-in by its words before the slot, as before); the evaluators and the echo veto read the lines retired from item 4a too (`RETIRED_POOLS`), so saved runs read as they did; F3 reads the new refusal shapes (first, since it reads the first pattern that matches); `CODE_LINE_TALK` takes every deferral, hold and offer. **The checker's neutral probes** ("Walk me through how you got to {x}." / "…that calculation." / "…how you got there."; Lorenzo's ruling, 18 Oct) vary too: the vetting is unchanged; when code selects the flag (`math-checker-live.ts` `neutralProbe`) the template is matched exactly and said in one of five versions — first use by seed, the most repeated line across batches — and that exact line is the selection's, the decision's, the model's hint's and the heard check's (`lineHeard` against the line chosen; another version doesn't count); the wrap-up probe's ask uses the same pool. The checker's own drafted probes are spoken as vetted. **Silence lines** (text mode): the check-in (once a silence, again after the next) and the technical pause (again on a later silence while pause budget is left) vary, with the use memory recorded by `runSilence` (they don't pass through Settle); `isSilenceLine` and the copied-check-in strip read every version. Not varied (Lorenzo, 18 Oct): a case's own brainstorm ask (case text), the conduct, exit and voice-limit lines, the pause-expired line (it ends the session). **Data lines:** prof-001's ledger lines in five pre-written versions, `cases/variants/prof-001.data-lines.json`, **on by default since 18 Oct** (Lorenzo read all 55; `DATA_LINE_VARIANTS=0` is the off switch — the tests that pin ledger wording set it): checked on load beside the case schema (`lib/cases/data-line-variants.ts`; every version states exactly its item's figures, periods, units and direction, keeps its listed phrases, else boot fails), named by the fact checks as its item and raising no false fact (tests); a release or restatement says one by seed (an item is usually released once a session) and never one of the item's last 3; corrections keep the ledger line. **The opener:** a case prompt that ends in its own question gets a hand-off that doesn't ask again (`opener_after_q`); the others keep the asking invitations.
- **No "your recommendation" before one is received (16 Oct, Lorenzo; batch 29 Jordan ea27d1f6 t11 → t13).** A turn whose question code replaced (the checker line, a pressure-test step, a fallback) saves no `recommendation` move label — the label says what the delivered question asks, and the reply-to-a-recommendation-ask test (`replyToRecAsk`, `recReplyAt`) reads it. The recommendation reply's own lines — `rec_revision`, the rec math probe, the closed-error flag — need a recommendation received (the stall state's `recommendationDelivered`); and code's revision questions ("Does that change your recommendation?") fall back to "Does that change your analysis?" when none is (a backstop; Plan keeps those turns out).
- **Decision log** — every check records pass/act/skip per turn as a `check` event.
- **v4.10 (round-3 fixes, batch 18):**
  - **Math, three outcomes** — case steps + stated arithmetic (`arithmetic-check.ts`) + nested conversions checked numerically (`unit-check.ts`, case `shareBases`); flags name the claim, quantity, units, base and timeframe; **the base is the candidate's own (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 27 Sofia cdaa9c6c t18 — "COGS is 58, and if beans are 25% of COGS that's 25 points of revenue" was corrected to "It's closer to 10.5, not 25.", a false correction: the check always took two years ago's 42, and the hint scripted the line):** a base level the candidate states for the line ("COGS is 58", "today's 58% COGS line", "0.25 × 58", "25% of 50", "a 42-point base"; a fraction is a percent, a stated change is never the base) is the one the conversion is checked and corrected at; with none stated, the case share's own year, else both years. A figure right on the candidate's own base is no unit error: with a share of another year (Marisol eb601063 t7: "0.25 × 58 = 14.5"; the 13 Oct period flag, retired) it is at most a question which year the share is from (`PeriodQuestion`, a "PERIOD (no error to flag)" hint line — no flag, no attempt count, no correction, nothing marked wrong for the judge). **Every code-written unit correction names the quantity, the base and its period** (`unitCorrectionLine`: "Two years ago, 25% of the 42-point COGS line was about 10.5 points of revenue, not 25." / "Today, 25% of the 58-point COGS line is about 14.5 points of revenue, not 25." / with no base and the candidate's own share, both years), in the hint's correction and the rec hold alike; the bare "It's closer to X, not Y" is gone, and no other deterministic flag is corrected by code (`codeMayCorrect`: unit only). The unit path doesn't wait for the checker. The checker's own corrections follow the same two rules: a step of one year corrects no claim whose sentence states the base line at another level (probe only — Sofia's verdict had bound her claim to the two-years-ago step, and its derivation used 42 too, so code's "agreement" wasn't independent), and pricing corrections name today's margin ("From today's 6% margin, a 10% price rise lifts margin about 8.5 points, not 5."; said by the model without that lead-in, the line still counts as heard, so it is never said twice); a break-even stated as a range is checked (wrong only when the whole range misses); a break-even is also right at a variable-cost share the candidate states in a sentence about variable costs (`statedContributions`; Raj's "call it ~65%" → 11 / (35 + 11) ≈ 23.9%; Lorenzo, 15 Oct) — accepted, never used for a correction; a points-change misquote stated as a range before a change noun ("a 38 to 40 point swing in COGS") is corrected by code. **Doubt needs a flag** (`math-talk.ts`, `probe-guard.ts`): a sentence doubting the candidate's math is withheld unless a flag names the figure (`probe_guard` on verified, `unsupported_challenge` on unverified); explain probes once per figure; a withheld challenge takes its setup with it and a coherent question replaces it. The keyword unit hint is gone.
  - **Stage record** (`stage-evidence.ts`, `flags.stageEvidence`): delivered + outcome per stage, from what was said or shown, never a label alone; owed exhibit / brainstorm administered once before a normal end, also in place of the model's early recommendation ask. **The only reading of the stages (Lorenzo, 13 Oct):** the end gate (`stagesFromRecord`), the close and what's owed all read it; it reads as the end gate's transcript reading did (closing grammar or a labelled move in matching words; the recommendation asks counted), which is gone. The reply to a recommendation ask is the recommendation received when it commits to an action (`answersRecommendationAsk`, stall.ts; Lorenzo, 14–15 Oct): recommendation wording, a sentence opening on what to do ("Raise…", "Lead with…", "Roll out…"), or an ordered plan opening on its first action ("Price first — roll a 10-12% increase…"); not a refusal, more brainstorm, a list of areas in order ("Pricing first since it's the obvious one…") or a risk answer.
