The risk question counts in any risk wording once a recommendation is on the table (`asksRiskQuestion`, spoken-close.ts; Lorenzo, 14 and 16 Oct): "which single risk…", "which of those two risks worries you more" — with or without a question mark (Raj 2eb0fdd3, batch 28 t18) — "which one would most undermine…", a contingency ending "how would that change your plan?"; the wave tools' stage minutes read it as the record does (`stageQuestions`: risk wording, or a risk move in risk words). **A risk the candidate names in their own recommendation with its mitigation** (`namesRiskAndMitigation`: "the key/main/biggest risk …", "the risk is …" with "pilot", "hedge", "phase", "so I'd" … in that sentence or the next two; only in the message that brings the recommendation in) **is the risk stage, answered unasked** (`spontaneous`): `stagesFromRecord`'s `riskAsked` and `caseFinished` read it as done (Lorenzo, 16 Oct; batch 29 Raj 2a86cb76 t17).
  - **Data**: no refusal of what the case holds (Lorenzo, 13 Oct): before code says "I don't have X", an unmapped ask is read by the false-refusal check (F3) and then against the case's `factAnchors.covers` phrases (`coverageOf`) — a covering ledger item is given like any ask ("As I mentioned: …" when out; since round 5 "As the exhibit shows: …" when it came out only on an exhibit, D6), a covering prompt fact is restated ("As I mentioned: <its say>", logged `clarify`), a question is read whole, a list entry by entry with only the uncovered entries refused (a phrase is matched in the whole ask, so one spanning a list's "and" — "fares and volume" — covers each entry it touches), and an entry cut finer than the case (by region, per item) is not covered unless the case's phrase carries the cut ("transaction volume per store"), nor one with a further cut beside the phrase ("broken down by customer segment", "per day"); an item a `covers` phrase names for an ask stays in that ask's scope when the model maps it; prof-001's average ticket covers transaction-volume asks (Lorenzo, 15 Oct), and a per-store ask its covers answer whole gets the ticket — added where the model left it out — and not revenue per store (a mapped item that is only the per-store cut, named by neither the covers nor the ask, is dropped; the store count stays; Lorenzo, 16 Oct); `outsideAskScope` keeps a qualified ask's scope (applied to declared and classified requests) — a fixed/variable cut is both words ("variable-versus-fixed", "fixed and variable"), the fixed part of something, or something that "is fixed"; "the fixed costs" alone names a cost line, not a cut (Lorenzo, 13 Oct); the repeat restate (merged from `repeat-request-restate`) only restates in-scope items — "As I mentioned: …" only for a re-ask of that same item (Lorenzo, 13 Oct): an ask for a split within one item, by the case's names for it ("the split within that non-bean bucket"; `splitOfItem`, the name right after "of/within/inside", not a list of parts), drops that item and isn't expanded to the item's group, and is refused ("I don't have …") when no ledger item is left, even beside an exhibit the model handed over for it; unmapped asks while the gate holds are never promised and are handed back once (`unmappedToResolve`); an ask covering ≥2 unreleased items of an unshown exhibit gets the exhibit; a group ask ("what's inside COGS", "a split of COGS by component") is read from the model's label and from the candidate's own asking sentence (`askingSentences`: addressed asks, guard B's cues, "I'd want…"): a declared ask mapped into a group the sentence names gets the group's items still to come, each in its own label, without moving items another of the turn's asks holds (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 25); a delivery claim with no delivery is withheld. Since 19 Oct (built on branch `engine-asks`, 19 Oct; not merged or deployed): the live false-refusal guard (`stream-turn.ts`) and F3 read covers too (`findFalseRefusals` with `{ covers: true }`: an object no item name hits goes through `coverageOf`, §15 (r)); and an ask for the basis of a figure ("the basis for the 70%", "where that estimate comes from", `BASIS`) is outside the scope of an item whose line doesn't say where its figure comes from (§15 (q)). **Round 5 scope rules (built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed).** Each is a finer cut, so the item is a coarse item under E1: its line, then "I don't have <the cut>.". (A3) A one-off, one-time, upfront, setup, start-up or stand-up cost asked against an item whose value states only an annual or monthly figure (B1 Aldermoor Marisol t23, whose one-off ask heard Option A's annual line restated; Option A was on screen, so she now hears the refusal alone). Its mirror, a recurrence ask ("recurs", "ongoing", "first-year-only", "in year two", "later years") against an item stating only a first-year or one-off money figure (only launch-001's `launch_costs` today; from the A5 review, controller, proposed). The count rule: "how many …" or "number of …" asked of an item whose value states no figure (no digit, number word, or "no / none / zero"), unless a cover carries the count ("how many stores" stays whole on `retailer_support`). A cover a count ask reaches only at the coarser cut gives the same (`coverageOf`'s `coarse`). The count rule was a controller ruling, approved by Lorenzo (20 Oct); it is what gives Lorenzo's expectation for Hollis Bay Jasmine t5. Spoken asks read as noun phrases: "how many X" → "the number of X", "how much X costs" → "what X costs", and a refusal of an ask still worded as a question says "data on whether …" (controller, proposed; the logged `what` is unchanged; F3 skips "data on whether / how …"). **Round 6 (tagged cases, 21 Oct):** covers are matched with plurals, a synonym table and the candidate's own asking sentence, dropped across another entity, a region or a total the line doesn't state, and gated by the item's tags and scope before anything is released — "Round 6 — the data path on tagged cases", above.
  - **Meta answer**: a report hand-off answering a question about assessment passes the goodbye veto on a continuing turn.
  - **Stale turns**: every session update is conditional on `status = 'active'`; a turn that finishes after the session ended writes nothing.
- **Fabricated-turn guard** — any sentence in which a line opens as another speaker ("\nuser …") is vetoed; the stream parser keeps a newline-led continuation inside its sentence so the check sees it (live run 58cb8061 had the interviewer write the candidate's brainstorm, which was then scored).

**Conduct & wellbeing track (Rule 17–19, Part IV of the behavior doc).** A separate track evaluated *before* case rules: C1 self-directed frustration (ignore), C2 directed hostility (warn then terminate), C3 harassment/slurs/threats (terminate immediately), C4 prompt injection (redirect + log, never terminate), C5 distress (break persona, offer pause/stop, never terminate). **Preemption is scoped (v4.3):** C2, C3, and C5 replace the case turn; C4 adds a one-clause redirect to the normal case turn, which still answers every legitimate request in the message. Quoted, reported, and generic-"you" speech never triggers C2 (logged as `C2_excluded`). The C5 offer states the stop option first, ends on the candidate's choice, adds the 988 line for risk-to-self signals, and stops the case clock until the candidate replies. Two detection layers (v4.6): the regex floor, and a Haiku classifier in parallel with the interviewer call whose distress verdict discards the draft turn before anything is delivered or persisted (eval: 16/16 distress, 0/10 controls; the regex alone 0/16). Posture: overreacting beats underreacting (Lorenzo, 2 Oct) — case-scoped "bomb every interview" still fires. Termination/abandonment → no score (see §4). The lexicons were adversarially hardened (July 2026) against a probe corpus now locked into `tests/orchestrator/conduct.test.ts`: the pre-hardening lexicon scored 14/23 false positives (including a C3 *terminate* on "I'll find you the exact number" and C2 warns on client-directed case speech like "your margins are terrible") and 13/30 misses (including "Ignore all previous instructions"); the rebuilt patterns require both a meddling/probing shape AND a model-operation object, and score 0/0 on the corpus. **Still pending:** human review of the lexicons against real pilot-population data — the probe corpus is authored, not observed, so it cannot stand in for what actual candidates say.

**v4.10 conduct/wellbeing changes.** C3 has its model layer (`conduct-model.ts`, Haiku 5.5, new `BACKGROUND_MODEL_ID.conduct` role): beside the interviewer call on every message, gating delivery like the distress check; a C3 ends the session before anything of the draft is spoken; ambiguous word-list stems are decided by it; a model-only C2 is logged, not warned; fails open to the lists. After a declined C5 offer the distress check is given the earlier disclosure and only a new or worse signal re-offers; bare sign-offs never reach it; "nothing left to pause for" declines. The candidate can stop (Rule 18A, `candidate-exit.ts`, `session-end.ts`). Unchanged and pending as product-policy decisions: under-18 consent/retention (proposed Rule 20) and when the crisis line is included.

---

## 6. Scoring service (runs once at case end)

Heavy work at the end protects live latency (FR-11). Implemented in `lib/scoring/score-session.ts` (shared by the score route and `scripts/live-run.ts`). Pipeline — order is load-bearing:

0. **Pre-scoring:**
   - **Transcript-artifact repair** (`transcript-artifacts.ts`): empty and duplicate turns removed; fabricated-speaker continuations cut from interviewer turns. Runs before everything else so no later pass sees a contaminated transcript.
   - **Data-request backfill + split** (`data-coverage.ts`): classify any candidate→interviewer exchange that has no `data_request` rows, then split ledger data the candidate asked for and never got (a coverage gap) from never-requested and not-in-case requests (Rule 11).
1. **Deterministic checks** (`/lib/scoring/deterministic.ts`, no LLM):
   - For each `math_step`, check the candidate's stated result against `answer` (or any `alt_answers`) within `tolerance` (FR-14), classifying each as `non_issue|minor|case_breaking|unmentioned`. Only numbers with a source span count (same cues/unit rules as the live check), each result carries its span, and a step whose inputs were never revealed is not scored (v4.3 — the closest-number rule reported a false per-store error in most persona reports). `alt_answers` credits genuinely ambiguous quantities (e.g. margin-impact vs actual-spend COGS increase) so a defensible-but-different computation isn't mislabeled an arithmetic error.
   - Run the data-leak audit over the full transcript (§5.1) → QA metric.
   - The math checker's record (`checker-record.ts`, 13 Oct): its flagged claims and their outcomes, for the judge; a stamp it found wrong no longer counts as verified (§5.4). **A later redo is credited** (round 5b; Lorenzo, 20 Oct: "13:0: the report credits a later redo of a claim already left for the report."; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed): a flagged claim with no correction that closed `unresolved`, `expired` or `shed` after a probe, pointer or hint is credited when a candidate message after that decision's turn redoes it to the right figure — read by `isStale`, figure-bound as E6 binds; only its "right figure stated" reason counts (a withdrawal, a revision with no figure, a restatement or the right figure for another quantity gives none). The line reads "The candidate redid it correctly later (turn N): "<the sentence the redo is bound in>"", in place of "Restated after it had been probed or corrected." or after any other outcome (`score-session.ts` passes the candidate turns, case data and revealed ids to `formatCheckerSection`). The scored report only; live behaviour is unchanged (controller, accepted by Lorenzo, 20 Oct). b33 Marisol 31e33506's 13:0, 9:0 and 13:1 are credited for her t19 "So beans were 0.25 × 42 = 10.5 points of revenue." Not credited: a concession-led redo ("Let me redo it …"), and claims only `derived` on the root (9:1) or only probed (§15).
2. **LLM judge** (`judge.ts`, Opus 5.5): evaluates the full transcript against the **8-dimension consolidated rubric** (`docs/Case Interview Feedback Rubric.pdf`, encoded in `lib/scoring/rubric.ts`) + case **answer key**. Returns, per dimension: rating (`needs_work|meets_bar|strong`, displayed as needs work/adequate/strong), **what went well** and **what needs work** (each point with verbatim candidate quotes, FR-12), **missed opportunities**, and a **coverage caveat**. The judge prompt is fed deterministic context so it doesn't re-derive or misjudge: the **math-check results** (trust these over re-derivation; wrong math can't appear in "went well"), the **data actually revealed, with requested-but-never-provided ledger data in its own coverage-gap section** (never charged to the candidate, including in the top fix — Rule 11), and the **intervention log** ("assisted ≠ covered" for stall rescues; time-pressure coverage for load-shed). It is also told to distinguish an arithmetic error from a wrong-*quantity* conceptual error. **Calibration (v4.3):** "strong" requires every element of the dimension's strong anchor, evidenced — the persona runs rated 73 of 88 dimensions strong. Normative scoring spec: `docs/scoring-qa.md`.
3. **Report verification** — a report that misstates the transcript is the worst feedback failure:
   - **Interviewer-error marking** (`interviewer-errors.ts`, v4.3, before the judge): deterministic marks from the event log — unbacked corrections, empty releases, unanswered requests, C2 warnings, concessions, and C5 exchanges. The judge receives them; after the evidence audit, a needs-work item whose only evidence is a marked candidate turn is dropped.
   - Deterministic evidence audit (`evidence-audit.ts`): every judge quote must appear in candidate turns (normalized, ellipsis-fragment-aware); fabricated quotes are stripped, and points left without evidence are dropped (a strength needs ≥1 real candidate quote; a weakness is dropped only if every quote it cited was fabricated — omission claims carry none).
   - Claim-verifier pass (`verifier.ts`, Opus 5.5): re-checks every `needsWork`/`missedOpportunities`/`topFix` claim against the transcript and drops what it contradicts (catches false omission claims, which contain no quote to check). Also checks **error claims** against span-checked correct figures and the interviewer-error marks (v4.3). Fails open — a broken verifier response ships the unverified report rather than blocking scoring. **Since 19 Oct (§15 (m); built on branch `engine-asks`, 19 Oct; not merged or deployed):** it also checks every "Better" model answer and the facts of every strength — figures, turn references, what the candidate said, not judgments (Lorenzo's ruling R3); an unsupported Better answer is blanked and the moment kept (R6), an unsupported strength dropped. Code's `auditBetterFigures` lists the Better answers' figures that no case value or one operation of two explains, as hints in the prompt ("FIGURES NOT FOUND IN THE CASE DATA"); an audit failure can't block the report. With every strength and Better answer a claim, the call's `max_tokens` is the judge's 32,000 (streamed; was 16,000 — a response cut at the cap has no parsed output and skips all verification), and every call logs its `stop_reason` and output tokens (engine-asks final review, 19 Oct). **Round 5 (E4; built on branch `engine-asks`, not merged or deployed):** a new rule, "Check every statement that the candidate said, never said, claimed, raised or asked something against the quoted turns. A statement the transcript contradicts is unsupported. Read a conditional ("if we're priced above…") as a conditional, not a claim." It answers B1's Larkspur Marisol report ("the 12-month turnaround question also surfaced only then"; she never raised it) and Aldermoor Marcus's ("claiming Kelvexa is 'priced above the incumbent'"; a conditional). The strengths rule now reads "said, claimed, raised, asked or did". Proven by prompt tests only; the effect shows in a paid re-score or the next scored wave.
   - **Answer-key and caveat-text pass** (`answer-key-pass.ts`, v4.5, before reconciliation): removes needs-work items naming an answer-key idea the candidate never raised (case `answerKeyIdeas`) and stage faults under a not-run caveat; a dimension it empties is re-rated one level up.
   - Dimension reconciliation (`reconcile.ts`, Opus 5.5, last): merges a concept stated as both strength and weakness within one dimension, drops faults that rest on requested-but-never-provided data, logs concepts faulted in 3+ dimensions. Skipped when nothing can collide; fails open.
   - **Conclusion mandate** (`conclusion-mandate.ts`, item 4 narrowed; Lorenzo, 27 Oct; after reconciliation, before the strong gate and the stage caps): every OPEN checker claim marked as changing the conclusion — the checker model's own call, or code's escalation on a figure in a `clearRecommendation` turn — must be named in the written report, at most three a report (code's escalations first, then the latest turns). `formatCheckerSection` tags those rows `[CHANGES THE CONCLUSION — NAME IT IN THE REPORT]` and mandates them in the judge prompt; this pass then adds a needsWork item for any the report still leaves out — on synthesis for the recommendation's own figure, else quantitative (judgment last) — quoting the claim's span when the span is found in a candidate turn with the evidence audit's own predicate, and with no quote when it is not. Where it sits is the resolution of the three passes that would otherwise undo it: after the quote audit and the verifier (which could drop the item), after reconciliation (which could merge it away, and which is why the pass adds nothing when the report already names the claim), and before the stage caps (so its quoted item is the "real, quoted evidence from elsewhere" Rule 9 asks for, and the dimension is caveated rather than not assessed). The five-run re-score of 27 Oct ($2.57) found the judge naming all 11 mandated claims on its own, so the backstop added 0 — it is a floor, not the mechanism. `qa.conclusionMandateAdded` counts what it had to add.
   - **Strong gate** (`strong-gate.ts`, round 3, after reconciliation): the judge fills a per-dimension checklist of the strong anchor's elements with quotes; "strong" survives only if every element is met or had no occasion, half are met, and the quotes are real; overall "strong" needs no needs-work and at least half strong. Batch 4 (with the checklist in the judge prompt): strong skill ratings 62 → 32 of 80.
   - **Caveat floor** (`caveat-floor.ts`, v4.3, after reconciliation): a dimension with a coverage caveat is never rated below meets_bar; the judge may mark a thinly-evidenced caveated dimension `notAssessed` (shown as "not assessed", stored as a NULL rating). (Its first two live runs silently failed to parse — the model reasons in prose before the JSON — fixed 2026-09-14.)
   - **v4.10 — challenge marks** (`challenge-marks.ts`, beside interviewer-error marking): every delivered doubt on the candidate's math is checked against the verification recomputed from the transcript — on verified math `confirmed_interviewer_error` (the next candidate turn protected, except quotes resting on a clause a check flagged), on unverified math `unsupported_challenge` (its own judge section, nothing dropped). The judge also gets the claims code settled (verified wrong / a sample verified right).
   - **v4.10 — stage caps** (`stage-caps.ts`, after the strong gate, before the caveat floor): the judge is given the stage record (or one rebuilt from the transcript for older sessions); a dimension whose primary stage never happened keeps its rating only with quoted evidence (spontaneous work counts) and a caveat, else it is not assessed. A partial interview (Rule 18A) is told so, its exit turns are marked never-graded (`candidate_exit`), and its overall rating is NULL.
   - **v4.10 — caveat floor, evidence-based**: a caveated needs_work becomes meets_bar only with quoted strengths and no surviving quoted weakness; with a surviving weakness it stays; with no evidence it is not assessed. **Since 19 Oct (§15 (l); Lorenzo's ruling R4; built on branch `engine-asks`, 19 Oct; not merged or deployed):** any assessed dimension with no quote in wentWell or needsWork is not assessed, whatever its rating or caveat (it runs after the verifier, so a dimension the verifier empties is caught); a strong overall is re-capped against the dimensions still assessed (`capStrongOverall`). Over 203 saved prof-001 reports it flips 1 dimension (already covered by the old rule) and 0 overall ratings.
4. **Report assembly** (`report.ts`):
   - Per-dimension rating + structured feedback sections.
   - **Model answer** for structure, key math, recommendation (FR-13) — sourced from the case keys so it's verifiable, not invented.
   - Deterministic math results layered in (overrides any LLM claim where ground truth exists).
   - Overall rating + **single highest-leverage next fix** (FR-15).

Persist everything to `scores`. Log `scoring_runtime_ms`. The three Opus 5.5 calls **stream** (a non-streaming 32k-token request trips the SDK's 10-minute guard — every batch-4 scoring run failed before this) and use server-side refusal fallbacks.

**Persona-band check (9 Oct, `lib/eval/bands.ts`, `scripts/batch-bands.ts`):** each persona's brief implies expected ratings per dimension (`evals/persona-bands.json`, frozen per batch version; a refused stage accepts needs_work or not assessed; strong-only expectations also accept meets_bar because of the strong gate). Batch reports are compared cell by cell (in band / one / two steps off / unexpected (not) assessed), by confidence, with unscored runs and simulator-broken runs listed apart. Smoke run (Tobias): 6/6 in band after widening. A blind comparison against a free-ChatGPT baseline on 12 batch interviews follows the batch (spec §2.7).

**Validation hook:** the report format and judge prompt must support offline benchmarking against ex-MBB coach scoring (product PRD §15 risk). Build a script that runs the judge over a labeled sample and reports correlation. If scores don't correlate with expert scoring, the feature's core value is unproven — surface this early.

---

## 7. API / server surface

All mutations via **server actions** or route handlers; case keys and ledger values **never** cross to the client.

| Endpoint / action | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `POST /api/session` | New session; opening message is the **verbatim case prompt** (deterministic — the candidate must see the exact scenario/numbers; not a model turn) + a rotating invitation. Auth + club-code gated. |
| `POST /api/channel/[id]/turn` | One candidate turn → orchestrator → interviewer turn. Returns immediately on the final turn (scoring is separate) with `scoring_suppressed` on conduct-terminated/abandoned sessions. |
| `POST /api/channel/[id]/silence` | Text-mode silence tick (`{ silentMs }` since the interviewer's last turn) → scripted check-in at 60s, technical pause at 180s (clock stops from that point; ≤5 min per pause and per session, warned in the pause line), session abandoned if the pause runs out, or nothing (Rules 13/16/19; `lib/orchestrator/silence.ts`). Idempotent per silence; `silentMs` bounded server-side. No client idle timer calls it yet — `scripts/live-run.ts` does. |
| `POST /api/channel/[id]/score` | Runs scoring for a completed session (idempotent; guarded to `status = completed`). Split out so the client can show an "evaluating" state and so a failed pass is retryable. `maxDuration = 300`: three thinking passes take minutes, not the old ~30s. |
| `GET /api/report/[id]/pdf` | Streams the report as a PDF (server-rendered; ownership-checked). |
| `scripts/live-run.ts` (dev only) | End-to-end run with a simulated candidate against real models and the real DB (fast by default; `--pace=human` adds think + typing time; `--persona=N` plays a pressure-test persona from `scripts/personas.ts`, whose `[pause Ns]` silences drive the silence endpoint's logic); writes transcript, report, and QA metrics to `Case Interview Runs/`. Route handlers are thin wrappers over `lib` (`start-session`, `post-turn`, `score-session`, PDF `render`), so the script exercises the same code paths. |
| `case/[id]/report` (page) | Renders the assembled report (no raw keys); persistent case-prompt banner on the live page. |
| `logEvent(...)` | Append analytics events (§13). |

**Security non-negotiables:**
- `data_ledger` values, `*_key` fields, and `math_steps.answer` are **server-only**. The client never receives them, even gzipped in a bundle. (Legacy per-case `rubric_anchors`, where still present, are likewise server-only; the live rubric is the generic 8-dimension one in `lib/scoring/rubric.ts`.)
- RLS enforces per-user session access.
- Exhibits are sent to the client only when the orchestrator shows one (a requested or handed-over exhibit, §5.1), and only the displayable chart data (not the `interpretation_key`).

---
- **v4.10:** `POST /api/channel/[sessionId]/end` (text End button) and `POST /api/voice/[sessionId]/end` both end through `lib/orchestrator/session-end.ts`: `{ status, report, partial }` — the client scores when `report` is true.


## 8. Voice pipeline (M2 — builds on the proven text core)

### 8.1 Cascade
Audio capture (browser, VAD) → streaming STT (Deepgram) → orchestrator → interviewer LLM (streamed JSON turn) → streaming TTS (Cartesia, sentence-chunked) → playback. The orchestrator's turn is streaming-ready since Oct 2026 (Plan → Stream → Settle, specs `2026-10-05-streaming-turn` and `2026-10-06-plan-owns-decisions`): `runTurn(…, { onSegment })` delivers each segment as it passes — `say` sentences, then code's data line, then the question — and books a reveal only when its segment is delivered. LiveKit Agents provides WebRTC transport, endpointing, and barge-in.

### 8.2 Latency budget (≤1.5s perceived, end-of-speech → first AI audio)
Per product PRD §8.2. Techniques: stream STT, stream LLM tokens, **start TTS on the first complete sentence**, keep the per-turn prompt lean, pre-warm connections. `turn_latency` logs `firstSegmentMs` (turn start → first delivered segment), `streamed`, the turn `kind`, the model call, `distressWaitMs` (how long the C5 verdict outlasted the model — nothing is delivered before it), and `steps`: every step of the turn as ms since the turn started (reads, plan, model request / first token / first sentence / each field closing / done, first delivery, data line, distress verdict, hint check, settle, persist; `lib/orchestrator/turn-timer.ts`), also printed as a `[timing]` log line. Segments carry a `kind` (`say` | `data` | `tail` | `scripted`); `first_useful_delivered` marks the first segment past `say`. **The latency target is useful content — the data line or the question — not first sound;** an acknowledgment or `say` doesn't count (`scripts/useful-latency.ts` reports it per batch, split by declared request count).
- **Replay, 5 Oct (50 batch 7–8 turns, streamed):** Sonnet 5.5 first token 1.35s median, first complete sentence 1.64s, whole turn 2.18s; Haiku 4.5 first sentence 1.05s but failed data-gating and demeanor on hand-read turns; Gemini Flash returned the JSON in one chunk; effort `low` no gain.
- **Batch 9 (5 Oct, streaming runner, old action list):** streamed turns' first segment 1.66s median / 2.14s p90 vs 2.35s / 3.42s whole turn; 72% of turns streamed. The distress check outlasted the model by up to 9.0s (smoke run) — a timeout on it is an open decision.
- **Batch 10 (6 Oct, one persona, Plan-owns-decisions):** first segment ~3.3s — the declarations preceded `say`, and the data-hoover persona's 8–11 requests a turn cost 350–580 output tokens before any speech.
- **`say` first (6 Oct, built):** batch 11 (Nikhil) first segment 3.27s → 1.65s; batch 12 (10 runs) 1.45s median / 1.87s p90; batch 13 (10 runs) 1.43s / 1.97s; whole turn ~2.5s. The model's first token (median ~1.37s) is now essentially the whole wait — code adds ~0.1s and the distress verdict (~0.75s) is never waited on. Summarized refusal/deferral lists still not built.
- **M0 Phase A (6–7 Oct, `scripts/voice-latency.ts`, recorded candidates replayed as real-time speech through Flux and the real orchestrator; 41 turns):** Flux end-of-turn 0.2–1.0s median by persona (p90 to 1.4s, occasionally 3.8s); our turn to first segment 1.5–2.0s (Sonnet's first token is almost all of it — DB reads ~30ms, Plan ~2ms, Settle + persist ~40ms); Cartesia first audio ~0.14s. **End of speech → first audio: median 2.49s, p90 3.31s — the gate fails without an acknowledgment.** Transport (LiveKit) not yet measured.
- **Instant acknowledgment (built, `lib/voice/acknowledge.ts`):** a pre-synthesized, code-chosen backchannel plays at Flux's confirmed end-of-turn, so the first sound is end-of-turn + playback; the model's turn is told (`runTurn({ acknowledged })`) and the saved interviewer line starts with it — **only a filler that plays (18 Oct, Lorenzo's ruling):** one skipped before the turn runs (`fillers_off`, `rate`) isn't passed to the turn at all (no saved prefix, no "you have already said" note, no repeat gate); one skipped after the turn started (`speech_resumed`, `reply_ready`, `cut`) is reported in the heard report (`ackHeard: false`) and left out of the saved line, so the model's history and the scorer's transcript never hold it (its turn note was already sent). **Since 17 Oct (branch `voice-feel`, Lorenzo's ruling after his voice test, session 314295e0, and after hearing a first version):** answers, questions and data asks get thinking sounds only — one beat ("Hmm…", "Mhm…", "Okay…", "Alright…"; a data ask also "Let me see…", never a promise of data) after a message under 40 words, two beats ("Hmm… okay…", "Hmm… alright…", "Mhm… okay…", "Mhm… alright…") after an answer of 40 words or more (`LONG_ANSWER_WORDS`, by length only, no latency prediction) — never a confirming word; **the filler starts 500ms after end of turn** (`FILLER_DELAY_MS`, `VOICE_FILLER_DELAY_MS`) and doesn't play if by then the mic has heard the candidate again, the turn was cut, or the reply is ready (logged as `ackSkipped`; cut turns log `cutMs`); each beat is its own clip with trailing intonation (the ellipsis spelled out to the TTS), joined in code with 400ms between beats and 300ms after the last (`ackBeats`, `joinBeats`, `lib/voice/ack-audio.ts`; `VOICE_FILLER_GAP_MS` / `VOICE_FILLER_TAIL_MS`). **After a thinking filler, the reply's first spoken segment opens with a connector** synthesized with it — "So," on ~75% of filler turns, else none (`lib/voice/connector.ts`): never a word the filler used, never the same choice three filler turns running, none when the line already opens like a connector or filler; code-written lines too, a correction's wording unchanged after it. **The connector is in the TTS text only** — not in the segment, the saved turn, the captions or the heard checks; its words are skipped in the TTS word timestamps so the heard cursor stays on the line. **On every voice turn** (`runTurn({ voice: true })`, acknowledged or not), a model `say` sentence that is only an acknowledgment or only names back the candidate's point with no figure is dropped (`lib/orchestrator/say-after-ack.ts`: `double_ack`, `echo_after_ack`); never dropped: a figure — a percentage, points or money attached to a number, a share or multiple in words ("half", "doubled"), or a number matching a case or candidate figure; a count of structure ("three buckets", "two-branch") is not a figure — the checker's line, pushback or a steer, comfort, an answer to a "what does X mean" question. After the stuck filler ("Take your time.", "Sure, take a moment."), a short sentence of only granting words ("Of course.", "No problem.", "Of course, take your time.") is dropped too (`double_ack`; 9a2c19eb t4, 19 Oct). A short sentence (≤ 5 words, not a question) whose every word is in the filler just played ("Take your time." after "Take your time.", "Take a moment." after "Sure, take a moment.") is dropped in `say` and in the question alike (`repeatsAck`, `double_ack`), and a code-written question opening with it drops that opening (`dropAckOpening`; the stall line "Take your time. The question on the table: …") — session 9ccf0836 T30, 17 Oct. The stuck pool ("Take your time.") is unchanged and gets no connector. Before 17 Oct: a two-beat confirming pool ("Okay, got it.", "Understood, okay." …), no connector, and only bare acknowledgments after an ack were dropped. Not played on the eager signal (declined 7 Oct). **Fits the kind of message (9 Oct, `ackKind`, rules, no model):** a candidate message of 40 words or fewer is classed stuck (strong stuck or holding cues: "I'm stuck", "I don't know", "hold on", "let me think") → "Take your time."; an explicit data ask (Guard B's cues) → "Okay, let me check." (never a promise — code may still refuse or defer); a question ("?" or a question opener) → the question pool; anything else, or a longer message, gets the answer pool. Two phrases per kind, no repeat. Hand-checked on batches 12–14 (12 overrides, all right) and the 8–9 Oct live voice turns (8 of 9 right; random picks sounded wrong on ~9 of 23). A Cerebras classifier was considered and not needed.
- **Pronunciation and keyterms (17 Oct, Lorenzo's brief, item 2; `lib/voice/pronounce.ts`, `lib/voice/keyterms.ts`).** **TTS:** "COGS" is said "cogs" (whole token, case-sensitive), "MECE" always "mee-see", in any case ("Mece", "mece"; Lorenzo, 17 Oct, after hearing "mee-see" and "mee cee" side by side: the hyphen is best) — in the TTS text only (like the "So," connector): the saved text, captions, transcripts and heard checks keep the line as written. Cartesia's word timestamps are folded back onto the written tokens (`WordFolder`: the connector's words skipped, then each written token's spoken words joined — first start, last end), so `heardChars` counts the written line (Cartesia returns "mee-see," as one word; "mee cee" came back as two, which the fold would also handle). Everything else reaches Cartesia as written (no normalization in code): "&", "$480M", "$6.80", "10–12%", "P&L", "SG&A", "EBITDA", "SKU", "FY2023", "Hub–Lakeport", "vs.", "~" — the demo (`scripts/voice-pronounce-demo.ts`) records how each is said. **Mechanism (chosen: code substitution at the TTS boundary):** testable and versioned with the code, independent of the Cartesia model (works on sonic-3.5), no account-side state; a Cartesia pronunciation dictionary (`pronunciation_dict_id`, created through Cartesia's API; IPA or sounds-like; case-insensitive matching needs sonic-3.6, mixed-case keys match exactly) also rewrites the text before the model, so its word timestamps would need the same fold, and it lives outside the repo. **STT keyterms — off by default (Lorenzo, 17 Oct) until an A/B on recorded speech shows their effect; decision rule fixed in advance (Lorenzo, 17 Oct): they go on only if the "with" run hears zero "costs" as "COGS" and fixes at least one of "Cogshare"/"cog share" or MECE, else they stay off:** with `VOICE_STT_KEYTERMS=1` Flux opens with `keyterm` = the generic terms plus the case's `sttKeyterms` (case-authoring.md), at most 50 (Deepgram's advice: 20–50); the agent logs whether they are on. The risk to check: a "COGS" boost turning "costs" into "COGS". `scripts/keyterm-ab.ts` streams one recording to Flux without and with them and diffs the transcripts (paid; estimate first). **ASR spellings in the checks (`lib/orchestrator/asr-terms.ts`):** "Cogshare", "cog share", "cogs share" are read as "COGS share" — in what the checks read (planTurn normalizes this message and the history), while the saved transcript keeps what was heard (`TurnCtx.heardText`); prof-001's COGS quote anchor also takes "cogs share" (no change on any of its 4,663 saved candidate messages). Why: 9ccf0836 heard "Cogshare"; the checks match "COGS" in any case but not "Cogshare" or "cog share" — a misquote and a unit flag were missed on the same sentences; both fire now.
- **The filler toggle (17 Oct, Lorenzo's brief, item 3; `components/voice-room.tsx`, `lib/voice/filler-pref.ts`, `VoiceTurnController.setFillers`).** One button on the interview screen, "Thinking sounds: on/off": the thinking fillers and the "So," connector after them. On by default, remembered in this browser (`localStorage`; when storage is blocked, for the page only), rendered on by the server. The browser sends `{ type: 'fillers', on }` on the data topic when it changes and before every `ready`, so an agent that joins or rejoins knows it before its first turn. The agent applies it from the next turn: each turn keeps the setting it began with. Off: no thinking filler (logged `ackSkipped: fillers_off`) and no connector; the comfort line "Take your time." (words, not a thinking sound) still plays. Every turn record carries `fillers: true|false`.
- **How often a thinking filler plays (18 Oct, Lorenzo's go on item 4c; `fillerAllowed`, `fillerRateFrom` in `lib/voice/acknowledge.ts`).** Always after an answer of 40+ words and on a data ask; after a shorter reply only when the turn before had none (never two short-reply fillers running). Config, tuned by ear: `VOICE_FILLER_LONG_WORDS` (40) and `VOICE_FILLER_MAX_RUN` (1 — the turns in a row with a filler a short reply may follow; 0 none after short replies, a large number every turn as before); the agent logs the rate it runs. A filler skipped by the rate is logged in the turn record (`ackSkipped: rate`), as are the toggle's (`fillers_off`) and the delay's (`speech_resumed`, `reply_ready`, `cut`); any skip restarts the run. The comfort line "Take your time." is not rationed. On 9ccf0836 the default drops t4, t8, t13, t16, t29: 16 of 21 turns.
- **Thinking filler — built, simulated, removed (7 Oct).** In the playback simulation (`lib/voice/playback.ts`, `scripts/playback-sim.ts`, 221 logged turns, durations estimated) the full filler delayed useful content by ~2.1s median; a short filler stopping at its next pause once Sonnet was ready filled the gaps (≤0.4s silence) but sounded scripted in the demo. Without it the silence between the acknowledgment and Sonnet's first word is ~0.3s median, up to ~1s.
- **Where the time goes (7 Oct, measured on 221 live text turns unless marked):** first useful content 2.13s median / 2.69s p90 from turn start (first sound 1.43s). By declared requests: none (41% of turns) 2.26s — the question waits for the whole turn; 1–2 requests 1.89s — the data line ~0.5s after the first token; 3+ 2.40s. Sonnet's first token (~1.35s) is the largest part and has resisted every optimization tested — a near-empty prompt was no faster (12-turn test), and neither concurrency nor an idle connection changed it (not established as an immovable floor); the API's own first token drifts 0.74–1.37s median between runs minutes apart, so latency comparisons must be paired and same-time. Haiku calls are off the speech path (distress verdict 0.72s median, held 0/221). Estimated (not measured end to end) end of speech → Sonnet's first word ≈ 2.7s median at Flux 0.7/0.5 (detection 1.14s on synthetic clips + first segment 1.43s + TTS ~0.14s); → first useful content ≈ 3.4s.
- **Speculative turns — IMPLEMENTED, DISABLED (no live caller).** `startDraft` (`lib/orchestrator/speculative-turn.ts`) runs `runTurn` on Flux's eager transcript behind a `DraftGate` (`speculation.ts`): every delivery, `commitScripted` and `persist` wait on the gate, deferred work is dropped unless the draft is accepted, and a cancelled draft (TurnResumed) can never play or commit, however late its model call finishes. A draft is accepted only if the final transcript is identical and a re-plan now (no classifier call) gives the same fingerprint — the rendered prompt with its m:ss clock blanked, history, turn kind, phase and every clock-derived decision. Draft model usage is tagged `speculativeDraftId`. Covered by mocked tests (real Plan and Settle, faked model). Expected saving at 0.7/0.5: ~125ms per turn (estimate from the synthetic sweep, not measured live) (eager lead 161ms median where eligible; clips with a premature end counted as zero); 23% of drafts cancelled. Open before enabling: abort a cancelled draft's API call; a live measurement of savings and wasted tokens.
- **Rejected or parked:** a Haiku-written opener in parallel with Sonnet (`lib/agent/opener.ts`; v1 silently corrected candidates and stated their conclusions as fact, v2 sounded robotic, real content still at ~2.0s); prompt layout `cached-history` (same latency, −17% interviewer cost, but more questions built on undelivered data in a 4-run A/B — parked pending a fix); a serial pre-call request classifier (Haiku: 818ms median, 1975ms p90); suppressing `say` in the output format (it costs ~75ms to write); acknowledgments on the eager signal. **Compact prompt (7 Oct, `REPLAY_ARM=compact-ab`, 20 stratified turns, paired, same model, settings, schema and checks): flat** — first useful content +19ms median (compact − full), faster on 10/20; input −29%, all of it cached; one request-handling regression (deferring data the case doesn't have). Prompt-latency work stopped; `system-compact.ts` kept, unused. Future experiments, not started: shorter questions; earlier question delivery (measure question completion, model completion and useful delivery separately first).

### 8.3 The LiveKit integration caveat (validate in M0)
LiveKit's default agent loop wants to own the LLM call. **It must not** — our orchestrator owns the turn, because data-gating and phase logic live there. Wire LiveKit so that on endpointed candidate speech, it hands the finalized transcript to *our* orchestrator (e.g. an `llmNode` override that calls `runTurn` with an `onSegment` sink), and the segments our orchestrator delivers are what gets sent to TTS; playback acknowledgements resolve the sink so reveals are booked only when heard. The model layer is ours. Validate this wiring works within the latency budget in the M0 spike before committing to LiveKit.

### 8.4 Hosting note
Long-lived WebRTC voice sessions don't fit Vercel serverless functions. Run the voice agent/orchestrator process on a persistent host (LiveKit Cloud, or a small always-on service — Railway/Fly/Render). The Next.js app on Vercel stays the web frontend + non-voice APIs. **Flag this split clearly; don't try to force voice onto serverless.**
- **As built (voice deploy, 18 Oct; `docs/voice-deploy.md`):** target LiveKit Cloud agent hosting (`lk agent create` from the repo root, which builds the root `Dockerfile`); a Railway/Render background worker runs the same image. The image runs `node --import tsx scripts/voice-agent.ts start` (`npm run voice:agent:start`): TypeScript as is, `@/` aliases from tsconfig, the loader inherited by each job's forked process; only `lib`, `db`, `cases`, `assets` and the entry are copied (`.dockerignore` is a whitelist — no `.env*`, no runs). The worker needs no disk: the TTS ledger and session records are in the database (§4), the acknowledgment clips ship in `assets/voice/acks` (`VOICE_ACK_SYNTH=0`), recordings stay off. Database: the Supabase pooler in **transaction mode** (port 6543), the same URL as Vercel — see the deploy doc for why. LiveKit Cloud injects `LIVEKIT_URL` / `_API_KEY` / `_API_SECRET` and ignores a custom load threshold.

### 8.5 Turn-taking
Barge-in: candidate interrupting stops playback and starts listening (FR-17). Tunable silence threshold so thinking pauses don't cut the candidate off (FR-18).
- **End of turn = Flux EndOfTurn** (end-of-turn 0.7, eager 0.5), not VAD silence. Phase A: 3/41 turns ended mid-answer, all at thinking pauses ("Um, and is…", "Let me do that. Ten percent is…"). Synthetic sweep (7 Oct, `scripts/endpoint-sweep.ts`: 40 clips with thinking pauses, a pause after a complete sentence, hesitation before a figure and self-corrections × 5 settings, no model calls): at 0.7/0.5 detection 1.14s median and 13/40 clips cut off — a 1.5s pause after a complete sentence at every setting, a 1.2s mid-sentence "um" on 5/8; 0.6/0.4 ~0.1s faster with 19/40 cut off; 0.9/0.6 ~0.4s slower with 6/40. No setting is both faster and safer; human recordings decide any change. Live, session 9ccf0836 (17 Oct): end of turn a median 0.50s after the last voiced frame (p90 1.49s); 7 of 28 turns ended mid-calculation and were cancelled when he resumed — the filler now waits 500ms (§8.2); settings unchanged (Lorenzo). Recovering from a cut-off (stop the interviewer and keep listening when the candidate resumes) is Phase B work; LiveKit's turn-detector model is the planned guard. Eager EndOfTurn drives only the disabled speculative draft — never speech.
- **Silence check-in (voice; Lorenzo's brief, 18 Oct; `lib/voice/turn-controller.ts`):** after `VOICE_CHECKIN_MS` (default 90,000; `0` = off) in `listening` with no candidate speech — no STT words (interim or final) and no Flux StartOfTurn; room and keyboard noise don't count (19 Oct) — and nothing playing, the interviewer says one soft line from the `voice_check_in` pool (5 versions, the first "Take your time — feel free to think out loud."). Once a silence: again only after the candidate speaks, and the next silence counts from their last speech. Each push-back (the first speech of a silence: `by` final / interim / start_of_turn, `silentMs`) and each skip (`reason`: playing, tts_allowance, tts_cap, audio_blocked, not_listening) is a record line `{ checkInEvent }`, so a session shows why it didn't fire. Never while the interviewer thinks or speaks (the timer runs only in `listening`); interruptible like any reply; no clock pause and no session end (unlike text mode's check-in at 60s and pause at 180s). **The first silence after the case prompt waits `VOICE_CHECKIN_OPENER_MS` (default 180,000)** until the candidate first speaks (Lorenzo, 18 Oct: 9813d6ce's first answer came 1:49 after the prompt). Not a turn (no `session_turns` row — the rec-ask check, the math checker and the opener replay read the last interviewer row as the question on the table — no stage, clock or turn-count effect), but said: **the model's history gets its heard text on the interviewer message before it** (Lorenzo, 18 Oct, so it doesn't say "take your time" again; the agent logs a session event `intervention/voice_check_in` `{ text, atMs, silentMs }`, read with the turn's reads, `lib/orchestrator/voice-check-in.ts`), and **the export's transcript shows it at its time** ("**Interviewer — check-in** (m:ss): …", *(cut off)* when partly heard). Its own record line: `{ turnSeq, afterTurnSeq, at, checkIn: { line, silentMs, playback, heardChars, heardText, startedAtMs } }`. Watch: text mode keeps its check-ins out of the history because the model copied them (batch 4).
- **Interviewer captions (18 Oct, Lorenzo's item 7; `lib/voice/caption-timing.ts`, `components/live-caption.tsx`):** decoupled from the heard cursor — display only. When a segment's audio starts the agent sends `caption_segment` (its whole text and each written word's Cartesia start, on the trimmed timeline); the page shows it at once, words not yet spoken dimmed, and brightens each at its start, timed locally from the message's arrival plus an audio offset (150ms; `?captionOffset=<ms>` on the page to tune it live). A word not timed yet is placed by the rate so far. Cartesia streams its timestamps with the audio, so words timed after the start follow in one `caption_words` per segment, when the TTS finishes — at most two messages a segment, none per word. On a cut or barge-in the agent sends what was heard at once (`caption`), and the turn's final caption is what was heard, as before. Heard accounting is unchanged (word end + `HEARD_MARGIN_MS`).
- **Echo guard (19 Oct, Lorenzo's ruling (b); `lib/voice/echo.ts`):** on laptop speakers the room hears the interviewer and the transcript cut the opening (97cc44af: "chain with", 2 words = the barge threshold; f3b07c57 "money"). A barge that would cut (speaking: `VOICE_BARGE_MIN_WORDS`, 4 on the hosted agent since 19 Oct, 2 in code; thinking: any word) is scored against the interviewer text playing or played in the last 10s (`Playout.spokenSince`, ack clips included): the share of its words found in order (LCS over tokens; number words as digits, "%" as "percent", "&" as "and"). At ≥ 0.8 it is ignored as echo, else it cuts. Every barge is a record line `{ barge: { state, transcript, words, score, decision } }` (an ignored echo again only once it has grown by 3+ words). Not guarded: an echo that arrives as a final after the interviewer stopped (it would start a turn) — a candidate may repeat the interviewer's words on purpose. **The page:** "Headphones work best — on laptop speakers the interviewer can hear itself and cut off." above Start (the small grey line is gone); a per-turn health line from the browser (`lib/voice/health.ts`, message `health`): the longest frame gap (visible tab only) and long-task time (Chromium; null elsewhere) since the last line, the audio element count and the audio context's state, written to the records as `{ turnSeq, health }` at the end of each interviewer turn.
- **What counts as heard (`lib/voice/playout.ts`):** a segment played to its estimated end with no cut is booked in full; after a cut, only words whose TTS end time is at least `HEARD_MARGIN_MS` (150ms) before the cut. The agent's timers (`realClock.at`) never run before their time — Node can fire ~1ms early while playout ends are fractional, which booked uncut replies without their last word (session 9813d6ce, 18 Oct; §16).
- **The acknowledgment** (above) is skipped when the instant conduct floor flags the message (distress, hostility, injection) — the conduct response comes first. Subtle distress caught only by the model layer can still get an acknowledgment.

---

## 9. Non-functional

- **Latency:** §8.2 hard gate. Instrument per-turn latency from day one of M2. Note for M2: recommendation-ask turns make one synchronous Haiku classification before responding (Rule 11 force-release, §5.2).
- **Cost:** instrument `$/completed case` (STT + LLM + TTS minutes + scoring) from the first session; per-call token usage is logged as `llm_usage` by component. Internal ceiling + per-user case cap during validation. Turns run Sonnet 5.5 without thinking ($2/$10 per MTok, vs Opus 4.8's $5/$25 — the interviewer's resent history dominates input, so this is the largest saving); scoring is three Opus 5.5 passes with thinking (judge, verifier, reconciliation; $4/$20 per MTok, thinking tokens billed as output). **Measured on batch 4 (Sonnet 5.5 + Opus 5.5, 10 persona runs, ~20-minute cases): app LLM cost $0.70 per run, of which scoring ~$0.45 (up from ~$0.19 on Opus 4.8 — thinking tokens).** The older figures below were measured on Opus 4.8; every turn also makes two background Haiku calls (coverage agent, data-request classifier) and two parallel ones (same-turn request detection, C5 distress), plus synchronous Haiku calls on recommendation-ask turns and question-based hint turns. Measured on the 2026-09-14 simulated runs (~2-minute cases, 12–13 interviewer turns): interviewer ~105k input / ~1k output tokens, judge ~12k / ~4k, verifier + reconciliation ~9k / ~1.5k, Haiku ~35k / ~2k — roughly $1 of LLM per case before STT/TTS. Interviewer input dominates because the full history is resent each turn, so cost scales with turn count. The cost lever is the per-user case cap; if `$/completed case` breaches the ceiling, dropping the turn model back to a smaller one is the first candidate. **Accounting (8 Oct):** every figure is priced from `lib/llm-pricing.ts` (an unknown model id throws; cache reads/writes counted), and every Anthropic call — background, scoring, simulator, aborted streams — is metered through one client; experiments run under a required budget that refuses calls at the cap. Coverage cost grows with transcript length (it resends the transcript every turn): ~$0.16 per 30-turn run on Haiku 4.5 (8 Oct prefix runs) — the main reason to move it to Haiku 5.5.
- **Reliability:** graceful degradation — if voice fails mid-case, fall back to the M1 text channel so the session survives (this is nearly free because text is the base layer).
- **Privacy:** explicit consent for audio storage; don't store audio by default (`session_audio` empty unless consented).
- **Browser:** latest Chrome/Edge/Safari desktop; mic-permission flow.
- **Test suite:** `vitest.config.ts` caps workers at 50% of the cores and sets a 20 s per-test timeout; the two pool-history seed loops carry 30 s (round 5b; Lorenzo, 20 Oct: "Make the suite reliable at full parallelism (cap workers or raise those tests' timeouts)."). At quiet load the slowest test is 1.5 s (13× headroom) and a default run takes about 9 s; the high-load case (load ≈ 27) is unproven.
- **Scalability:** designed for tens of pilot users but bursty (recruiting calendar) — connection pooling, pre-warmed voice workers during known spikes. Don't over-engineer beyond pilot scale.

---

## 10. Content & case authoring

Cases are **human-authored JSON** in `/cases`, version-controlled, validated against a **zod schema** on load (reject malformed cases at boot, not mid-session). Schema per product PRD §11. 8–12 cases across profitability, market entry, M&A, market sizing, ops/cost.

**FR-20:** no case ships without an answer key; ex-MBB author + second reviewer. Enforce: the loader fails any case missing `structure_key`, `data_ledger`, `math_steps[].answer`, or `recommendation_key`. Rubric anchors are generic (the 8-dimension rubric in `lib/scoring/rubric.ts`); per-case `rubric_anchors` are deprecated/optional. Additionally, every case needs a ledger-consistency block in `tests/cases/consistency.test.ts` (prompt ↔ ledger ↔ exhibits ↔ math steps reconcile). Authoring rules — including speakable ledger values, per-phase `pacing` budgets, `alt_answers` for legitimately ambiguous math steps, and the **source-span fields every math step must declare** (`cues` required, `unit`, `inputs` — ledger ids validated at load — and `live: false` for prompt-fact steps; v4.3) — live in **`docs/case-authoring.md`** — as do two rules added after the 2026-09-14 runs: the stated root cause must be derivable from the ledger (prof-001's key claimed coffee beans explained a COGS jump that was only possible if beans were ~95% of COGS), and exhibits list the ledger items they display in `coversLedgerItems`. Fields added 13 Oct for the AI Casing cases (`prof-002` Larkspur Air, `entry-001` Aldermoor Pharma, `pricing-001` Quillpath, `launch-001` Hollis Bay): exhibit `after` / `requiresItems` (release order, validated acyclic), exhibit `notes` (footnotes shown under the table), exhibit `required` (owed before the end; its predecessors must be required too; prof-001's exhibit too since 15 Oct; the phase budgets set its deadline, §5.2), ledger `spoken` (an item's name in a promise — authored for every item, all-case test), case `brainstorm` {`ask`, `required`}; and all-case tests that labels, exhibit titles, spoken names and code's stage lines carry no figure the prompt lacks (FR-4), and that every math step lists its `inputs`. Fields added 21 Oct (round 6, built on `engine-asks`, not merged; §5.4 "Round 6" and "Step-check roots"): `factAnchors.tags` {`clientWords`, `entities` [{`id`, `words`, `items`, `rival`?}], `forecast`} (opts the case into round 6's matching and answer gate; unknown ids rejected; entity objects strict), `mustReach` [{`item`, `before`: an exhibit id or `"recommendation"`}] (validated), and math-step `dependsOn` (known steps, acyclic) and `inputQuestion` (ends in "?", no digit); covers are linted with `scripts/cover-lint.ts`.

A ledger item's `spoken` name is what code says for it in its own lines (deferrals, holds, offers); lists say a shared "as a percent of revenue" once, and put a comma before the last item when an item has its own "and" or "including". prof-001's overhead is spoken "overhead including rent" ("COGS, labor, and overhead including rent as a percent of revenue"; Lorenzo, 15 Oct), its data line "Overhead and rent are 14% of revenue, up slightly from 12%.", and its quote anchor names "overhead and rent", "overhead including rent", "overhead", "rent", "occupancy".

---

## 11. Out of scope (do NOT build — explicit)

The coding agent must not pull these in. Scope creep is the default failure mode.

- Candidate-led (BCG/Bain) mode — fast-follow, not now.
- Panel / multi-interviewer simulation.
- Behavioral / PEI / fit questions.
- Mobile-native app (desktop web only).
- Monetization / paywall / billing logic (free during validation).
- On-the-fly AI-generated cases (all cases human-authored).
- Drills, peer matching, B2B2C dashboards (separate workstreams).
- Speech-to-speech black-box API (rejected — loses the data-gating control FR-4 requires).
- Per-turn scoring (scoring is end-of-case only, for latency).

---

## 12. Build order (each step shippable + testable before the next)

1. **Schema + auth foundation.** Supabase project (new keys), Auth + club-code gate, drizzle schema, RLS, case loader + zod validation. One seed case.
2. **Orchestrator + data ledger (text, no LLM yet).** State machine, phase gating, the reveal mechanism (originally a `reveal_data` tool call; since Oct 2026 code-decided releases, §5.1), the deterministic post-turn data-leak audit. Drive it with scripted candidate input in tests.
3. **Interviewer agent (text; Opus 4.8 at build time, Sonnet 5.5 since Oct 2026).** Turn loop (tool calls at build time; a streamed JSON turn since Oct 2026, §5.3), system/anti-hallucination/anti-jailbreak prompts, pushback flag. **Gate: run the 50-case-style hallucination harness on the seed case → zero invented numbers.**
4. **Scoring engine (Opus + deterministic).** Judge, math checks, report assembly with model answer. **Gate: feedback beats ChatGPT in a blind test on the same answer (product PRD M1 gate).**
5. **Text case UI + report UI.** Full end-to-end text case in the browser. Transcript view. This is a *complete, usable product* minus voice.
6. **Content: author 8–12 cases.** Each with keys + reviewer sign-off. Re-run the hallucination harness across all cases → **0 incidents (DoD #2).**
7. **M0 voice spike (parallelizable from step 1).** Validate LiveKit + Deepgram + Cartesia latency ≤1.5s median AND the orchestrator-owns-the-LLM wiring (§8.3) on the seed case. *Status (7 Oct): Phase A done (Flux + Cartesia + the real orchestrator, no transport). The gate passes on first sound only with the instant acknowledgment; on useful content it fails (≈3.4s estimated end of speech → data line or question, §8.2). Filler removed; speculative turns implemented and disabled; end-of-turn settings kept pending human recordings; compact-prompt A/B flat — prompt-latency work stopped. Phase B (LiveKit, mic, barge-in, playback-confirmed reveals) not started.*
8. **Voice layer (M2).** Implement the voice `CandidateChannel` against the proven orchestrator. Barge-in, VAD threshold, text-fallback on failure. **Gate: realism ≥4.0, latency gate held.**
9. **Analytics + cost instrumentation** wired throughout (do this incrementally, not last — §13).
10. **Validation pilot** with 5–10 clubs.

Steps 1–6 contain zero voice code. That's the point.

---

## 13. Analytics (instrument as you build, not at the end)

Log: `case_start`, `mic_check_result`, per-turn latency, phase transitions, data items revealed, exhibit shown, `case_complete` vs `abandon` (+ abandon phase), `scoring_runtime_ms`, `$ cost per session`, post-case ratings (realism, usefulness), blind-comparison opt-ins. These feed the success metrics and go/no-go gates directly. Append to `analytics_events`.

Evaluation runs only (9 Oct): `scripts/live-run.ts` also writes `<run>.turns.jsonl.gz` beside each run — every model request body and raw reply, the state the prompt was built from, all check verdicts, timed delivery, recorder errors, plus the commit, persona and simulator spend — so runs replay exactly; git-ignored (≈1 MB per interview). The free replay (`tests/replay/runner-corpus.replay.test.ts`) re-runs those saved turns on today's code. Since round 5 (engine-asks, 20 Oct) it serves each run's recorded small-model check verdicts (`lib/eval/recorded-checks.ts`: the same turn and exact ask first, else the run's nearest turn; none recorded, no verdict); `REPLAY_CHECKS=<json>` overrides them (the round-5 live Haiku check's verdicts); and a code-written turn's dropped asks (`code_written_requests.dropped`) are served as explicit asks with the ids of that turn's mention rows. Its limits: the model's recorded `say` and question are reused, so a changed code line can sit beside the old one; a new check call gets no verdict, so it reads as a named deferral; the message classifier returns nothing on saved model turns, so A1, A4 and A5 show only in tests and live waves; a changed turn moves the turns after it (a cascade). The simulated candidate reads an exhibit as the UI shows it: rows, then `Note: <notes>` (`exhibitAsText`, `lib/eval/exhibit-text.ts`; round 5, D6). Before that the simulator never saw the notes.

Also logged (added during the build): `llm_usage` (tokens per call, by component — the `$/case` input), `scoring_qa` (one per scoring run: transcript artifacts, evidence strips and point drops, verifier drops, data-request gaps / not-in-case / backfills, reconciliation merges / gap drops / cross-dimension repeats), `turn_latency`, `phase_repair`, `data_force_released`, `fabricated_turn_stripped`, `data_revealed` with `via: 'exhibit'` for exhibit-covered ledger items, `spoken_close_resolved`, `session_paused` / `session_resumed`, `case_abandoned`, and `provenance_blocked` (v4.3 — a turn had a figure withheld; target rate is near zero, and every event is a model fabrication caught). `scoring_qa` also carries (v4.3) `interviewerErrorMarks`, `markClaimDrops`, and `caveatFloors`.

The small-model ask check, per session (round 5c, `engine-asks`; Lorenzo: "In the next wave, log the classifier's cost and added latency per session."): `lib/eval/check-cost.ts` and `scripts/check-cost-report.ts` (`npx tsx scripts/check-cost-report.ts "<batch or wave folder>" [more] [--out=<file>|-]`; default `<folder>/check-cost.md`) read each run's `check` events (`itemless_defer` with `latencyMs` and `timedOut`; `partial_dropped`) and its `llm_usage` rows with component `ask_check` (late rows of a timed-out call included once): calls, cost, timeouts, late rows, added latency (sum and max of the waited turns; the whole call's latency), turns waited, `partial_dropped`. `scripts/persona-batch.ts` writes `check-cost-<wave>.md` into the batch folder after each wave and prints a one-line total (a failure only logs). Runs from before the `ask_check` component show cost n/a. Every wave grade counts `partial_dropped` and says whether a part of the ask was left without a word (Lorenzo, 20 Oct: I5 accepted, counted in the waves). Batches 36–37: 34 calls, $0.0596, 0 timeouts, max 1.26 s, 6 `partial_dropped` rows (one, Hollis Bay Tyler t5, left the ask without a word).

---

## 14. Definition of done (acceptance)

1. End-to-end interviewer-led **voice** case, median turn latency ≤1.5s (p95 ≤2.5s).
2. **Zero** numbers stated outside the data ledger across a 50-case QA run (deterministic audit proves it).
3. Interviewer withholds data until asked, challenges unevidenced assertions, holds character against jailbreak, and follows the conduct/wellbeing track (`docs/interviewer-behavior.md` Rules 17–19) on abusive/distressed input.
4. Report shows all 8 rubric dimensions (rating + went-well/needs-work with transcript-sourced quotes + missed opportunities + coverage caveats + model answer); deterministic math checked programmatically; report claims verified against the transcript (interviewer-error marking + quote audit + verifier incl. error claims + dimension reconciliation + caveat floor); no report item rests only on the candidate's reaction to an interviewer error.
5. ≥65% blind preference for our feedback vs ChatGPT on the same answer.
6. Text-fallback works on voice failure; state persists across a network drop.
7. All §13 events flowing to analytics.

And the structural one this whole doc is organized around:

8. **Steps 1–6 (the entire text product) ship and pass their gates with no voice library in the dependency graph of the orchestrator, agent, or scoring code.**

---

  - **The checker gate (16 Oct, built — §5.4, §16) — all five approved by Lorenzo (16 Oct, his decisions):** (a) no verdict — the checker off, not run on the message, or an error — means no code line; (b) the model's own doubt is licensed by a code flag only once the checker has released it (`flaggedFigures`, `flaggedThisTurn`); (c) a unit line needs the checker's flag only, a misquote line also its right figure among the data line's figures or their change (3% / 0.5); (d) the period question is not gated (it asserts no error); (e) the growth probe is withheld unless it quotes a figure a landed flag covers, and the RIGOR prompt line says so; (f) the true lines the gate drops on the replay (8: five small Larkspur data misquotes the checker tolerates or doesn't claim, a bean-share "30%" the checker bound to another quantity, a wrap-up probe and a unit probe the checker had no claim on) and the 7 more growth probes it withholds are accepted as the price of the gate.

- **Voice feel (17 Oct, branch `voice-feel` off `club-beta`; not merged, not deployed; Lorenzo listens first) — awaiting Lorenzo:**
  - **If pointers come back (Lorenzo, 18 Oct):** the strict check (f3a3b06) withholds every pointer the corpus had (5 in 268 runs). If they are wanted again, vet them with an allowlist — revealed figures, the case's line names, question scaffolding; anything else withheld — not a blocklist of phrasings, which fails open on wordings it didn't anticipate.
  - **Voice capacity — found 18 Oct (free check, report only), logged, not fixed:** (1) **TTS budget:** ≈ 2,550 characters a session at batch 31's text pace (2,268–2,775; the full 20-minute ones 2,508 and 2,753), ≈ 3,700–4,000 in voice (9ccf0836: 3,676 heard in 18 min, more and shorter turns; turns cut before audio not counted; the filler clips are cached, not per session) — so the 90k monthly cap (`VOICE_TTS_CHAR_CAP`, less the 2k reserve a turn needs) allows ≈ 22 voice sessions a month, ≈ 35 at text pace (Cartesia Pro is 100k). (2) **Concurrency:** `npm run voice:agent` runs the LiveKit worker in dev mode, where its load threshold is Infinity — it never reports itself full and accepts every dispatched session, each in its own process; the limits are elsewhere: Deepgram's concurrent Flux streams (an apparent one-stream limit on the credit account, above — unverified for two streams, a paid test), Cartesia's concurrency on the plan (not checked) and the machine. A session past a limit: its agent fails to open Flux (or TTS) and never says hello; the browser stays on "Waiting for audio" — no timeout, no message. **Built 18 Oct (voice deploy, brief A5):** a global cap, `VOICE_MAX_CONCURRENT` (default 1, a Vercel variable): the token route counts the other live voice rooms in LiveKit's room list (`case-*` with someone in them, or created in the last 30s) and refuses past the cap with 429 before any dispatch; the browser shows "Voice is busy right now. Try again in a few minutes." — on that refusal, and when no interviewer audio arrives within **30s** of connecting (or of the audio-unlock click; 15s until 19 Oct — a LiveKit Build-plan agent can cold-start 10–20s after idle, which 15s called busy), showing "Connecting to your interviewer…" while it waits, then leaves the room (`lib/voice/capacity.ts`). Two Starts within the same second can both pass (the count is not a lock) — acceptable at pilot traffic; the watchdog catches the loser. The hosted worker runs in start mode (load threshold 0.7 CPU; LiveKit Cloud enforces its own defaults). **For when concurrency goes above 1 (logged 19 Oct, no change):** at each job start the hosted worker's load passes 0.7 for ≈ 2.5s (0.71–0.73 in the three 19 Oct jobs; the job process forking and loading) and it marks itself "at full capacity, marking as unavailable" — a second session dispatched in that window would find no worker on the one replica; raise replicas or start a pre-warmed process before allowing two.

Fixed: `lib/scoring/checker-wait.ts` (a check row with `started` and no verdict or error row is pending); the score route waits up to `MATH_CHECKER_TIMEOUT_MS` before scoring and logs `checker_wait_timeout` if it fires; the UI calls `…/finalize` first and shows "Finalizing your report…" (text and voice).
  - **Found 18 Oct (Lorenzo's check on 4a), logged, not fixed — the same item deferred four times is a stall nothing counted:** of the 15 runs in batches 27–30 with two or more deferrals, 14 deferred different items; batch 29 Marcus 51056656 deferred "the cost structure" at t2, t4, t6 and t8 (the model's own `defer` while it pressed for a structure the answer-first persona wouldn't give), and the data went out at t10 when the pressure test was satisfied. The stall check classed every one of his messages as analysis (figures in a derivation), so no rung fired. The variants don't hide it from the logs (`data_decisions.defers` names the item each turn), but they would vary its sound. A possible rule — a same-item deferral on two turns running counts as a no-progress turn for Rule 13 — is not proposed for build yet; awaiting Lorenzo.
  - **Item 4b — ruled by Lorenzo (18 Oct): go on the interviewer prompt only (the checker's prompt shares a call with the verdicts and is not changed); built (`lib/agent/prompts/system.ts`, the "question" rule); one paid batch to measure. The proposal as it stood:** of 391 model questions in batches 27–30, 16% open "With those figures…", 16% "You said…" / "Earlier you said…" / "You mentioned…" / "You named…" (74; 3 or more in 8 of 45 runs, at most 4) — 53 the model's own, 20 the checker's drafted line said verbatim, 1 a RECOMPUTE probe; 9ccf0836 has four. Proposal: one prompt line in the question rules — "Don't open two questions in a row the same way. To come back to what they said, name the point itself ('On the 38% —', 'Your bean figure:') or just ask; 'You said …' at most once every few turns" — and the same instruction to the checker's probe drafts. It changes the model's words, so it needs a paid batch (the usual 10 personas, ≈ $6) to measure the opening mix and that probes still name the figure; kept apart from item 1.
  - **Item 4c — ruled by Lorenzo (18 Oct: "go"; the rate a config value, skipped fillers logged per turn), built (§8.2). The proposal as it stood:** every completed turn got one — 21 of 21 in 9ccf0836 (20 thinking sounds, one "Take your time."), 10 of 10 in 314295e0. The gap is real: the model's first audio came a median 2.76s after end of turn (under 1.5s on 1 of 21), so fewer fillers means more dead air. Proposed rate ≈ 3 in 4, with no latency prediction (his 17 Oct ruling): always after a 40+ word answer and on a data ask; otherwise not on two turns running. On 9ccf0836 that drops 5 of 21 (t4, t8, t13, t16, t29 — short replies right after a filler) → 16 of 21. Free to build; his ear decides.
  - **Ruled by Lorenzo (17 Oct), built:** both first filler sets dropped (the old acks confirm; the carrier cut clipped "so"); thinking sounds in beats; the connector; the say rule on every voice turn; long answers get a filler (§8.2, §16). Demo for his ear: `Case Interview Runs/voice-demos/2026-10-17-fillers-v2/` (every filler; turns 4, 7, 9 re-voiced on their measured timings, with "So," variants of 4 and 7; `timeline.txt`). **Ruled by Lorenzo (17 Oct) on the three points found after v2, built:** (1) the data-ask pool → the one-beat thinking sounds plus "Let me see…"; (2) two beats after answers of 40+ words, one otherwise, no latency prediction; (3) no action — without TTS word timestamps (Cartesia always sends them) the heard cursor would run ahead by the connector's length (~0.3s); accepted.
