If you have the COGS…" — the pressure-test judge ruled the probe unanswered ("say nothing is missing without naming any gap"), though it answers both MECE and priority; code narrowed the probe ("Let me make it concrete: … which one would you check first, and why?") and deferred COGS as premature. Not the stall ladder: the stall check classed the message as analysis, no rung. COGS went out at t8 after he repeated "costs first". (2) **t11 → t12:** "do you know what [the bean share] is now?" got a bare refusal ("I don't have coffee beans' share of COGS today.", no ledger id) — true, but the case holds the related bean price change (`bean_price_change`, +40%) and code didn't offer it. (3) **t9 → t10:** "breakdown of, like, item by item" released one of the three COGS items (`bean_share_of_cogs`): `request_scope` dropped `non_bean_input_change` from the qualified ask and `bean_price_change` wasn't mapped — at odds with the rule in force "a COGS split goes out whole".
  - **Session 9ccf0836 (17 Oct) — a false correction from converting the candidate's figure to another quantity; ruled by Lorenzo (17 Oct) — "not X" only when the candidate used code's quantity or unit, otherwise one conversion line and the step closes; a figure wrong on their own quantity keeps the correction, worded on it — built (§5.4 "Corrections on the candidate's own quantity", §16). The proposal as it stood:** **What happened** (voice turns; DB turns in brackets): seq 11 [T20] the checker's probe on "ten percent of the increase is from the coffee beans" (key `bean_points_added`) — his figure is right on a COGS base (25% × 40% = 10%); seq 13 [T22] the checker's probe, said by code, on "each one is around nine point three" (`non_bean_points_added`; 25% × 37.5% ≈ 9.4 each, right on the same base); seq 15 [T24] **the checker's correction (attempt 2, said by the model verbatim): "Bean inflation's contribution to the COGS increase, in points of revenue, is about 4.2 points, not 10."** — after he had said "ten points of the thirty seven point five percent increasing COGS" (seq 13); the checker read the claim as points of revenue (its `unit`), code's step agreed (4.2), and the gate passed it (the checker is the flagger). Then the model's own probes, licensed by the open flag: seq 16 [T26] "Take beans at 25 percent of COGS … Does ten points carry through to the revenue base?", seq 20 [T28] "Which share of revenue was COGS two years ago…" (after his 5.8 — wrong in the revenue terms he'd been pushed into), seq 22 [T30] "Ten points of COGS convert to revenue using one share of revenue. Which one applies…". **Not the stall ladder:** every stall check that turn range says rung none; T30's doubled "Take your time. Take your time." is the stuck-pool filler plus the model opening its question with the same words (the say rule only reads `say`). 4 more model probes were written for turns his resumed speech cancelled (seq 17–19, 23). **The same class elsewhere:** batch 30 Noor t16 — code's pricing check, "From today's 6% margin, recovering 16 points of margin takes about a 20.5% price rise, not 38%." — her 38% was the rise for COGS to return to 42% of revenue, stated at t13 (58/42 = 1.38), right; she conceded. **Batch 30's hand grading rated this line true; by this class it is a false correction, so batch 30 had one** (the grading file and §16's batch-30 entry say 0 — to correct on Lorenzo's word). Batch 29 Marisol t15 — code's misquote check read "rose 11.8" (points of revenue, right) as a misquote of the 37.5% price change; the checker gate (16 Oct) already drops it. **club-beta behaves the same:** no file on the correction path (math checker, checker line, pricing, misquote, unit check, fact check, settle, stall, prompts) differs between `club-beta` and `voice-feel`; given the same checker verdict the same lines go out. **Proposal:** (1) one guard before any correction — the checker's (`codeCorrection`), code's pricing line, the misquote line: read the candidate's stated quantity from the claim and their next two messages ("of COGS", "points of COGS", "of the increase", "COGS back to 42%"), and compute their figure on that quantity from the ledger (bean share × change; 0.75 × 37.5; 58/42 − 1); if code's figure measures a different quantity and theirs is right on their own (within the 3% / 0.5 tolerance), never say "not X": say once, code-written, "In revenue terms that's about 4.2 points." (Noor: "Measured on margin, the rise is about 20.5%."); (2) close that step: the checker key, the step probes and the figure (`closedFigures`, so the model's own doubts are vetoed as unsupported) — reopened only when the candidate states a wrong figure in code's unit (his 5.8 points of revenue would still get one probe); (3) a figure wrong on their own quantity keeps today's correction, worded on their quantity. Reuses the unit check's own-base machinery (`statedBaseOf`, "right on their own base", 15 Oct). **Risks:** a misread stated quantity silences a true correction (mitigated: their figure must be right on it); a conversion line can still sound like a correction; step quantities need an alternative-base value per case (prof-001's three steps; the new cases' steps don't have them yet). **Replay plan (free):** an adapter that rebuilds 9ccf0836 as a replayable turn file from the DB and the worker log (model outputs, checker verdicts) — the voice agent saves none; then before (club-beta) / after on 9ccf0836, batch 29 Marisol (b29 verdicts on file) and batch 30 Noor (b30 verdicts converted), plus the full 240-run corpus for new false or dropped true corrections.
  - **Also found (17 Oct), built 17 Oct (Lorenzo's brief, §8.2, §16):** the say rule didn't read the question field, so a question that opens with the filler's words ("Take your time.") doubled it (9ccf0836 T30).
  - **(c) Proposed by Claude, not built: the model's first line reacts to the candidate's point, then one single-part question with varied wording.** Prompt (`system.ts` "say"/"question", the acknowledged turn note): "say" becomes one short reaction to what is specific in their point (a stance, a tension or an implication: "So you're reading this as a cost story, not a pricing one."), never a figure of any kind, never a verdict, never data talk, at most ~12 words; "question" asks one thing (no second clause joined by "and"/"or"), and doesn't open the way any of the last three questions did ("With …, what does it / do they tell you…" opened 4 of 9 in session 314295e0; 6 of 9 asked two things, 8 counting "…, and why?"). Voice-only first (the acknowledged-turn note, and the voice flag now built), so the frozen text product is unchanged. The connector already opens the reply with "So, …"; the reaction must read after it. **Risks to the fact gates:** a reaction restating a wrong claim affirms it in words with no figure for F1 to catch (the batch-28 Sofia "5.8 points" pattern, without the number); reactions drift toward verdicts ("Interesting that…") and leads (hints), which the grading veto and Rule 1 only partly catch; the say-after-ack echo rule would drop reactions that read as echoes, so it and the reaction form must be tuned together; varied question wording can slip past F4's undelivered-claim patterns ("Given the numbers you now have…" with nothing delivered) and the pressure-test detection, both built on today's wordings; single-part must exempt code's own questions (the late recommendation ask carries the risk in one question by Lorenzo's 15 Oct rule) and the checker's probes. **Plan:** (1) free — two-part-question, repeated-opener and figure-in-say counters as report-only checks, baselined on batches 27–30 and this session; (2) a paid replay of batch 30's model turns (~200 turns, new prompt vs the saved baseline, same model and settings; ≈ $4.5 at batch 28's $0.022 a turn, cap $6) counting F1/echo vetoes, grading and undelivered-claim vetoes, pressure-test detection, echo-rule drops and the new counters; (3) Lorenzo's voice session(s) and the timing replay; (4) one prof-001 wave (10 personas, checker on, ≈ $7, cap $10), hand-graded against the 16 Oct bar — go only with no new false statement and faults no worse than batch 30. Each paid step on his go.

- Which 2–3 archetypes to author first?
- Audio storage / retention policy specifics (default: don't store).
- Exact per-user case cap during validation.
- drizzle vs. raw Supabase migrations (agent may choose; flag it).
- C5 report discard (behavior doc Rule 17-C5, v4.3): **decided 2026-09-29 — not built for the pilot**; a candidate who continues after a distress disclosure is scored normally with the C5 exchange excluded. Revisit if pilot users ask ("hide from my history" first; true deletion needs a retention decision).
- Live recompute recall: the v4.3 span matcher produced zero false flags across the 13 persona runs, but the corpus held no genuine dollar-impact or per-store errors, so its hit rate on real errors is measured only by unit tests. Confirm in the next persona cycle (Sofia, Jordan, Tyler) before relying on it. **Batches 12–13 (Tyler, Sofia, Jordan run):** recompute never fired — Tyler's misquotes of released data, Jordan's 38-vs-16 point error and Maya's "16% of $480M is $48M" are not case math steps, so none was flagged, and the Rule 14 two-attempt cap never applied (Maya probed six turns, Jordan four). Coverage gap, open.

- **New cases — known gaps from the 19 Oct audit (shared code; proposed, not built — any change there needs a prof-001 recheck first; (c) built on branch `engine-asks`, 19 Oct; not merged or deployed).** (a) **A defensible alternative isn't bound to a quantity.** `altAnswers` is a list of numbers, so prof-002 `breakeven_fare` accepts $150 (right only as revenue per passenger — the fare is $130) and pricing-001 `value_with_starter` accepts $1.28M (right only as the CFO's no-upgrades view) whatever the candidate names them as; the checker's prompt prints them as "also defensible". Ruled (Lorenzo, 19 Oct): a figure is right only for the quantity the candidate names it as; not strict, since a false correction is worse than a missed flag — both stay accepted until the schema can say it. Proposal: an alternative with its own cues (`altAnswers: [{ value: 150, cues: ["per passenger", "revenue per passenger", "including extras"] }]`), valid only in a clause naming one (`findStepSpans`), printed to the checker as "also right as <its quantity>", and the gate's right figures (`math-checker-live.ts`) taking it only there. (b) **Spans are unsigned.** `QUANTITY` in `lib/scoring/math-spans.ts` reads no minus sign, so a step's answer is a magnitude (Lakeport FY2025 is 600, "a loss"); prof-002 `rampup_operating_profit` is now named a $0.2M loss (tolerance 0.05) in its quantity, which the checker reads, but the deterministic verification still credits "a $0.2M profit". Proposal: `signed: true` steps with a signed answer, the span's sign from "−", "minus", "negative", "loss", "in the red" versus "profit", "positive" in its clause. (c) **The quote grammar splits thousands.** `FIG` in `lib/orchestrator/misquote.ts` (and `quoteFits` in `fact-check.ts`) reads "$1,200" as $1, so a correct quote of a figure with a thousands comma is a misquote flag: "Pro CAC is $1,200", "The Lakeport cost per flight is $9,000", "Nimbrel's net price is about $9,000", "Existing Starter customers are about 9,000" (live path, `findCandidateMisquotes`). Not spoken in practice: the checker gate releases a misquote correction only on a checker flag matching the value ($1 or $9) with a right figure that agrees — but it is noise in F2 and leaves the cut figure as an open echo. prof-001's figures have no thousands, so it never showed. Proposal: `\d{1,3}(?:,\d{3})+` before `\d+` in `FIG` and `quoteFits`, commas dropped when parsed; recheck on the misquote labelled sets and the corpus replay. **Built** *(on branch `engine-asks`, 19 Oct; not merged or deployed; Lorenzo's ruling R5, 19 Oct: folded into (p)):* the number grammar reads `\d{1,3}(?:,\d{3})+` as one figure in `FIG`, `quoteFits`, timeframe keys and the cell lookup; "Pro CAC is $1,200" and "The Lakeport cost per flight is $9,000." flag nothing; labelled-set precision unchanged (0.86 / 1.00). (d) **pricing-001 "Pro sign-ups" names two items** (sign-ups by plan: 500 → 350; the price test: 400 at $60). Kept (Lorenzo, 19 Oct): the shared name lets a figure valid for either item pass, so no true statement is flagged; splitting it flagged "Pro sign-ups were 400 at $60." and "Pro sign-ups at $60 were 400.", and period-only names caught nothing. Cost: a wrong figure that is the other item's figure isn't caught, and one wrong for both gets two correction lines (both true). (e) **fact-check D6 on the new cases is noise** (`lib/eval/math-check.ts` calls `checkRecomputeForTurn` without `checkCandidateMath`'s held-figure, own-arithmetic and 100× filters — batch 22: the new routes' 56 passengers read as Lakeport's). Ruled (Lorenzo, 19 Oct): grade D6 by hand; fact-check unchanged.

(i), (j), (s), (t) stay open. **Round 2 (Lorenzo's rulings on the free proof, 19 Oct — rulings 1–8, his; built on branch `engine-asks`, HEAD 7cfd08d, not merged or deployed; free proof final2 → r2-final done, `Case Interview Runs/replays/2026-10-19/engine-asks/r2-final.md`: prof-001 4 checker decisions in 2 runs and 77 slice-lines changed, all intended, no regression):** 1 — a later claim at a corrected root's wrong value hears the correction once more, then the report (§5.4); Shared engine: (h) **a stage deadline yields to every turn that carries a math line** (`plan-turn.ts`: `byTime = !mathLineNow && …`), not once — Aldermoor Marisol had a checker line on every turn from 6:32 to 22:07 and closed at time-up owing the brainstorm, the required survey exhibit given only with the time warning; against the 15 Oct rule (a due stage beats a checker probe, the probe held once) — proposed: one yield per stage, recorded in the session flags. **Built** *(on branch `engine-asks`, 19 Oct; not merged or deployed; Lorenzo's ruling R7, 19 Oct):* a deadline yields once to a probe (`flags.stageYielded`), then the stage goes and a probe due then is held for the next model turn; a correction rides on any overdue stage turn, said first, the stage's ask in the same turn (§5.2). Replay: Aldermoor Marisol gets the survey exhibit at 12:50 (was 20:34) and the brainstorm at 20:34 (was never); prof-001 unchanged. When two deadlines pass together a probe is held on two stage turns in a row, and can be lost at a time-up close — accepted as proposed (Lorenzo's round-2 ruling 8, his open point 5). Built: requirement and target cues (need, have to, must, required, to get, to restore, to recover) join the quote grammar's hypotheticals; in the pronoun chain ("it goes from…") a hypothetical counts only up to the chain's end, "it" is the level item named before it in the sentence else the last sentence's (not an item named after the figure), and a share applied to a base ("It's 25% of 42") is no level. Replay (190 runs): off 3 / on 4 lines, all intended — Tyler b24 t5 and Tyler b20 505297a7 t4 corrected ("COGS is 58% of revenue today…"), Marcus t9's false correction gone, and Tyler t17's own question now keeps its lead-in (its misquote is closed). - **15 Oct 2026 — the COGS split is read from the candidate's asking sentence, and the cap never splits a group (Lorenzo's batch-25 ruling 1; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: batch 25 Marcus f8ed869d t7 ("a split of that 58 by category, coffee versus dairy versus food" — labelled "the breakdown of COGS by category", a finer cut, so one item) and Fiona 52743bea t5 ("a split of COGS by component, say beans, dairy, food, packaging" — labelled item by item, two items) got part of the split because the expansion read only the model's label; Jasmine f4fcab5b t5 and Owen d3db78c0 t5 *were* expanded, but the menu prices took a release slot and the cap of three deferred the third item (Owen's deferral line also named a second, overlapping ask: "…the price change of the other inputs and input price changes for beans and other inputs shortly"). Built: `askingSentences` (request-signal.ts: addressed asks, guard B's cues, "I'd want…"); in `turnData` a declared ask mapped into a group one of those sentences names (`groupItemsAsked` on the sentence) gets the group's items still to come (not ones already given: "the rest of the COGS breakdown" isn't answered with the share again); an expansion adds only items no other of the turn's asks holds (the model's order kept); `decideData` takes the turn's asked groups and lets a group that has started complete past the cap (nothing else passes it); prof-001's COGS group gains "inside that cogs", "coffee versus/vs dairy", "beans versus/vs dairy". Tried and dropped: counting a group as one ask against the cap — the replay showed 167 lines in ~80 runs, mostly a fourth item (the menu prices beside the cost-lines group) going out a turn early; expanding in place — it moved the group ahead of the menu-price ask, which was then deferred (Raj 76e4ff6 t9, Owen 57c3889 t5); adding already-given items from a sentence — five re-asks restated the share (0bfe5f38 t9, Sofia 3baae0b t11, …); a dedupe of overlapping group asks — unreachable once expansion skips items another ask holds (0 replay lines), removed. Replay vs 2a47982 (200 runs = 190 + batch 25, batch 25's 105 checker verdicts converted to `replays/2026-10-15/b25-fixes/b25-verdicts.jsonl`): checker off 53 / on 53 lines in 28 runs, all read — every first change a COGS-input item now released with its group instead of "I'll come back to…" (the four batch-25 runs, Marcus/Fiona/Jordan/Owen of earlier batches, 20 more), the next turn's "Coming back to…" release gone or now "As I mentioned:" on the model's re-ask; Eliza 9056707 t5 defers the average ticket instead of two input changes; Jasmine dcf421b t21 gets the owed brainstorm instead of an analysis re-ask (her input data out at t5); four turns only reorder the three items to the model's order. - **15 Oct 2026 — a fixed claim isn't probed or corrected again: equivalent forms, redos, more concessions (Lorenzo's batch-25 ruling 2; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: batch 25 Marcus f8ed869d t9 redid his "15-20%" as "1+x = 1.237 — about a 24% price increase" (1.237 wasn't read as 23.7%; 24, the checker's right figure, is also the prompt's margin, so it was ignored) and code still corrected it at t10; Owen d3db78c0 t15 ("I think I was loose… roughly a 24% price increase. So my earlier range was wrong") redid his price for the 24% margin while the flag's figure was 20.5 (recovering 16 points), and the close hold at t16 re-asked the retracted range; Marisol 0f7551c2 t15 restated her inflation and gap on today's revenue ("18.4", her 16 points × 1.15) and the t16 hint re-asked "22.1 against 9.7". Built (`isStale`, `planCheckerFlags`): the factor form "1+x = N"; for a needed-price claim, a price stated as the answer (not a scenario sentence) matching a right figure to the nearest point even when a case figure, or the price for the prompt's prior margin (a redo for another target); the right figure × or ÷ the prompt's revenue growth when its sentence names a base ("scaled", "today's revenue", "index", "times 1.15"…); concessions "I was loose/sloppy", "my earlier range/number/estimate… was wrong/off/understated", "made the same mistake again"; and in a verdict, a flagged figure that is the sum or difference of an answered figure and another figure in the same or previous sentence (not itself flagged) is answered with it. Why the last: with the 9.7 answered, the checker's next pick at t15 was Marisol's "6.3 points" (16.0 − 9.7) — the same redo retracted it. - **15 Oct 2026 — stage deadlines from the phase budgets, prof-001's exhibit required, the time warning at the candidate's pace (Lorenzo's batch-26 ruling 1; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: batch 26 Blake 4755c03a's exhibit was owed on every turn (t1–t25) and never shown — the stage hold administers an owed stage only when the case would close or code would ask for the recommendation, and neither came: coverage never completed, the candidate asked for one line at a time — and time-up (t25, 20:01) went to the grace ask and the close with no brainstorm and no risk question. **Why no time warning:** the warning fires on the first message inside its 90-second window; Blake's exchanges took ~92 s (human pace), so t23 came at 18:28.6, 1.4 s before the window, and t25 after time-up. Built: prof-001's `exhibit-a` is `required`; `timeWarningDue` (pacing.ts) also fires when the remaining time is at most the time since the candidate's previous message (Settle saves `flags.lastMessageElapsedMs`; sessions without it keep the window alone); `stageDeadlinesMs`: the cumulative ends of the ANALYSIS, EXHIBIT and BRAINSTORM budgets (prof-001 11:20, 13:20, 16:00) are deadlines — at the first a required lead exhibit (no `after`) and the core analysis (`analysisCore`), at the second an exhibit ordered after another or after the brainstorm and the brainstorm, at the third the recommendation ask (`rec_ask`); through the existing kinds and order, one a turn, each once. - **15 Oct 2026 — a checker line counts as delivered only if it is heard (Lorenzo's batch-26 ruling 2; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: batch 26 Jasmine 074b0534 t31 — the checker's probe on "about 5 points" (a 10% rise is worth ≈ 8.5) was selected, booked (attempt, handled, step count) and logged "checker flag delivered: probe", but the model asked for the final recommendation instead; nothing raised it again and the understated figure went into her recommendation. Jordan 6bd9edf3 t11's correction was logged "delivered" and not said either (harmless — it repeated t8's), and his t9 probe the same. Built: Plan hands Settle the line it booked on a model turn and how to undo the booking (`CheckerLinePlan`); after the pressure-test steps and stage holds, Settle checks the delivered text with `lineHeard` (case, curly quotes, dashes, spacing and sentence punctuation aside; decimals kept); a model question that left the line out gives way to code's line, and a correction goes before the model's question; if a pressure-test step or a stage code administers took the question, the booking is undone and the decision logged `undelivered` (report: "Not raised in the interview (another line took the question)"), so the flag is selected again later. Chosen of the ruling's two options: code says it (the vetted line), and "stays pending" only when another of code's lines owns the question. Tried and dropped on the replay: checking and swapping before the stage holds — it pre-empted a hold that hands over an exhibit (Larkspur Jordan 9ab76a39 t15: Exhibit 3 not shown, and his saved t17, quoting it, drew a false misquote correction on the replay).   - *§15, Background math checker evaluation:* - **Background math checker (13 Oct, branch `math-checker`) — evaluation awaiting Lorenzo's OK:** built offline (`lib/orchestrator/math-checker.ts`), not live. Two arms (`MATH_CHECKER_CONFIGS`: Sonnet 5.5 / Opus 5.5, adaptive thinking, effort medium, fallbacks on); labelled set `evals/math-checker-labels.json` (349 messages: 73 recall, 276 precision); `scripts/eval-math-checker.ts --dry` estimates $10.15 (Sonnet) + $23.79 (Opus). Pick order: false flags (near zero) → recall → recommendation-turn latency → cost; Sonnet if both meet the bar with similar recall. **Decided (Lorenzo, 13 Oct): Sonnet 5.5.** ~~A correction is spoken only when code computes the same quantity itself and agrees~~ — superseded the same day: **code writes the correction** from its own figure when it computes the quantity the checker names (a math step that declares its inputs, all revealed; or a pricing answer from `pricingModel` once every cost line is out), naming the quantity and its input ("A 10% price rise lifts margin about 8.5 points, not 5.") — only when the checker's figure agrees with code's (3% or 0.5; else probe only, logged) and the price change or target code computes from is the candidate's: in the claim's span, or in its sentence when exactly one figure there in the input's unit (% for a price or a target margin, points for a target gain) is that input (Lorenzo, 13 Oct); the checker's arithmetic is never spoken, and its output no longer carries a correction sentence or expression; the line passes provenance (their figure must be one they said) and truth like any other. When code computes the quantity, its figure (not the checker's) decides whether the flag stands. When code can't, Rule 14's second attempt is a **pointer probe** the checker drafts (a question naming a revealed figure the claim doesn't account for; provenance + truth; must name a figure from the prompt, a released line or a shown exhibit); still unresolved → the session record for the report (step 3). A flag whose own correct value is within tolerance of the candidate's figure is dropped; word quantities hold within 10 points of share. The checker is given code's pricing answers for the price changes a message names. **Live wiring (decided; built 13 Oct, §5.4; replayed over saved runs, not yet run live):** never waits, not even on the recommendation turn — the verdict lands on a later turn as a RECOMPUTE-style flag under Rule 14 (a model probe on the claim counts as the probe); the close holds one turn for an unresolved conclusion-changing flag; when time is up it goes to the report. Deterministic checks first (a disagreeing stamp is dropped; a disagreeing flag is logged, the flag kept). Evaluation so far: 143 messages + a 37-message rerun after the rulings (§16). **Decided (Lorenzo, 13 Oct):** sentence binding (exactly one matching figure in the input's unit), the 30 s timeout (every timeout logged), the two-message expiry, no checker probe beside a code flag or misquote correction. Landing on human-paced runs' recorded clocks: of 19 replies to a recommendation ask, 14 land before the close is planned, 5 can't (the close answers that message). **Step 4** (10 prof-001 runs, Sofia and Jordan scored; after both branches merge): estimate ≈ $6.4, proposed cap $10 — awaiting his go-ahead; graded by Lorenzo or a separate grader.

- **18 Oct 2026 — voice deploy A1a: the TTS ledger in the database (Lorenzo's brief A; branch `voice-deploy`; free).** Why: a cloud container's disk is wiped on restart, and the old ledger (`.voice-cache/tts-chars-YYYY-MM.json`, read-modify-write) lost increments between concurrent jobs. Built: table `voice_tts_usage` (§4; migration `0009_voice_deploy`, **not run — awaiting Lorenzo's go**), `SharedTtsBudget` (`lib/voice/tts-budget.ts`) over an atomic store (`lib/voice/tts-ledger-store.ts`: one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET used = used + n RETURNING used`). `take()` stays synchronous — it checks the last total plus this job's increments in flight and sends the increment, one at a time — so a segment waits on no round trip; another job's spend is seen on the next increment, so concurrent jobs can overshoot the cap by at most one segment each. A failed write is logged and still counted for the job. The agent and the three paid voice scripts use it; `scripts/voice-tts-seed.ts` carries October's on-disk count into the row once.

- **18 Oct 2026 — smoke test of the hosted worker (session 9813d6ce): the last word was dropped from the heard count, not cut from the audio (Lorenzo's brief, item 1; `phrase-variants`; free).** **Found:** 10 of 23 spoken replies booked `partial` with no barge-in (the local session 9ccf0836: 4 of 21); every one of the 7 with model text in the worker log lacks exactly its final word ("…which would it be and" — "why?"; "costs?", "next?", "wrong?", "beans?", "dollars?", "on?"). The hosted worker keeps no recording, so Cartesia's word timestamps and the frame times aren't in the logs; settled from the code and a measurement instead. An uncut segment gets `endAt` only after every frame has gone to the sink, and nothing clears the sink without a cut, so the audio was complete. But the idle wait's timer (`setTimeout(fn, end − Date.now())`) can run ~1ms before a fractional `end` (15% of 400 fractional targets on this laptop, 18 Oct; the local session's 19% matches), and then `classify` took the segment as still playing and kept only the words ending 150ms before — the last word. That count is what the heard checks book (`voice_heard` "only what the candidate got is booked": the saved question lost its last word; `held: question` on t1). **Fixed:** `realClock.at` re-arms until `Date.now()` reaches its time (tests: an early host timer doesn't run it; a re-armed timer still cancels; 20 real-clock playouts with fractional lengths all book `played` — the old clock fails 3 of 3 runs). Lorenzo's other smoke-test answers: thinking sounds were off by his toggle (works); the 9:43–12:43 silence was him thinking.

The asks rotate by seed whatever was said. **Chosen: reword, not gate** — gating would need a "pricing was discussed" read that can misfire, and the line's job (a margin-focused "what else") doesn't need the premise. Now "What else could the client do to protect margin?" — still read as the brainstorm by the generic grammar ("what else could …"), so recognition and the stage evidence are unchanged. Rule added to `docs/case-authoring.md` (a brainstorm ask must not presume a topic); a test pins that no prof-001 ask names a lever and each reads as the brainstorm.
- **18 Oct 2026 — the voice export marks skipped fillers; the saved line still holds them (Lorenzo's brief, item 4; `phrase-variants`; free).** Session 9813d6ce ran with thinking sounds off: 22 of 23 fillers were skipped (`ackSkipped.why: fillers_off`), yet the transcript read "Hmm… okay… Is that MECE…". **Built:** `transcriptMd` takes the agent's records, matches each to its turn by the candidate message in order (cancelled records have none), and writes a skipped filler as `~~Hmm… okay…~~ *(filler skipped: fillers_off)*`; a played one is unchanged (`scripts/voice-export.ts` passes the records; checked on 9813d6ce's saved files: 22 marked, t8's played "Take your time." not). **Found, proposed, awaiting Lorenzo's ruling:** the cause is upstream — `playAck` sets `t.ack` before skipping, and `execute` passes it to `runTurn` as `acknowledged` whether or not it plays, so the saved interviewer line opens with an unspoken filler (`heardText`; the db, the scorer's transcript and the model's history all hold it), the turn note tells the model "you have already said 'Hmm… okay…'", and the say rule drops a repeat of it. Proposal: pass `acknowledged` only for a filler that will play — at once for `fillers_off` and `rate` skips (decided at end of turn, before the turn runs); the 500ms delayed skips (`speech_resumed`, `reply_ready`, `cut`) are decided after the turn has started, so they need the runner to read the ack when it saves (a getter) rather than when it plans.

Context: session 9813d6ce had a 3-minute silence (9:43–12:43; he was thinking) with nothing said. **Not built, for his ruling:** the check-in isn't in the export's transcript (it isn't a DB turn) or the model's history; the first silence counts from the end of the case prompt, so a candidate reading it for 90s+ hears the line (9813d6ce's first answer came 1:49 after the prompt). Tests: 7 controller cases (fires once; candidate words reset it; a voiced frame defers it; not during a 100s model wait; cut by talking over it; the record line; off without the setting).

  - Re-review: since-FY on lines whose years come from their periods; "the same … as" read as change without a period; "usually" and market's total / overall / size / adults hid true gaps; `anyYear` waived years against "today" lines → fix round 3. Then "average" alone met "benchmark" on a company's own average, the benchmark refusal kept one noun, "a FY2024" → fix round 4.

  **Proposed by Claude (the controller), awaiting Lorenzo's ruling — round 5c** (each line a `Ruling:` of the ledger):
  - Models: Opus for 61 and 62, Sonnet for 63; reviewers Opus for 61 / 62, Sonnet for 63; the proof analysis Opus.
  - Tasks 61 and 62 in parallel (disjoint files), 63 after 61.
  - The waves' $40 cap as four batches × `--cap=10` (persona-batch's per-batch ledger), not the plan's one shared budget file.
  - A conversion said on the once path counts as the root's one derived line.
  - Without "?", a self-answered sentence (", I would say / assume / think …", "yes / no because") or a cleft is a statement; "Can we assume …" stays a question.
  - The cleft guard excludes cognition and report verbs (know, think, tell, say, expect, believe, assume, guess, figure, see, mean).
  - A missing second topic doesn't make the items answering the other topics partial (they keep Haiku's label and every gate; only the missing topic is refused beside them); period and scope stay per item.
  - A scope is met when the item's label or line carries it (competitor ⇐ competing / rival / other players; market ⇐ market / category / demand / patients / eligible; industry ⇐ typical / category / sector; benchmark ⇐ typical / average / comparable / past launches / historical); #36 stays flagged.
  - A period inside one conjunct applies only to the items answering that conjunct.
  - Drop "usually" and market's total / overall / size / adults; keep "often" and "competing" for industry (both the implementer's additions).
  - `anyYear` (the implementer's addition) only on lines with no today / current / now marker.
  - "average" meets "benchmark" only with industry / market / typical / comparable — a company's own average is no benchmark.
  - The pre-authorized conditions are met on his definitions (#66 true but misleadingly worded; #52 refuses a figure no item holds); the waves run on the proven code 29469ee, and the #66 wording fix waits until after them.
  - Also flagged: the `asksAddressed` self-answer guard was the implementer's addition.

Quillpath Jasmine: firm. Hollis Bay Marisol's two ladders: borderline."
    - "Unasked or wrong-mapping releases. Serious when they hand over the solution the candidate is meant to produce, or data gated past where the interview is. - **26 Oct 2026 — Phase 1's pre-wave fixes, from Lorenzo's twelve rulings on the gate verdict (branch `phase1-prewave` off `phase1` b2496fb0; $0 — every piece of evidence a free replay or a local read; detail in `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-22-phase1/prewave-report.md`).** Why: the gate verdict (26 Oct) left twelve items open; his rulings answered them by number. Built: **ruling 1** — the eight still-failing `false_refusal` records were all Phase-1-owned; six pass now, one is a replay artifact, one is explained and left (b37h Tyler t14's "repeat rates", where the only fix is a cover that would answer a quantity the case does not hold). Its parts: the bank's `answered` assertion now counts a deferral **of the item**, however code worded it (§4.2 "assert outcomes, not wording" — code words a deferral from the item's label, so an `about` regex written from the candidate's ask failed on a deferral plainly of the right item); and a new switch **`coarseNoRefusal`** stops an ask whose coarse item goes out on the same turn also being refused (b34a Jasmine t4 released `current_treatment` and said "I don't have current US treatment breakdown by drug class" in one breath), narrowed on the bank's own evidence to **split-of-item** asks after the unnarrowed rule cost one correct record and three fault records whose refusal is right. **Rulings 3 and 4**, behind a new switch **`correctionRules`**: each item is corrected once on every path (the reply to a recommendation ask no longer bypasses `MISQUOTE_CORRECTIONS_PER_ITEM`, and the cap is one — b32 Tyler was corrected at t12 and got the identical line at t28), and **no correction on the closing turn** — the close no longer lifts the per-item cap, and what it held is logged as `misquote.closeHeld` (b38l Tyler t32 prepended a ~70-word read-out to the goodbye to fix $3.6M → $3.8M). **Ruling 11**, inside `misquoteFlat`: a misquote-typed error claim whose own figure sits inside the checker's own tolerance band of its right figure licenses the flat path even when the right figure is not code's, so the spoken line names the **misquoted input** from the ledger and never a result that is within tolerance; `statedWithinBand` is extracted from `withinTolerance`, one formula for both. **Ruling 5**: `WRAP_UP_PROBES` stays 2, and a new `wrap_up_cap` check records what the cap kept back (log only, no switch). **Ruling 6**: pricing-001's `churn_breakeven_at_60` gains `dependsOn: ["starter_ltv","pro_ltv"]` in the overlay — as `dependsOn`, because `mathSteps[].inputs` are ledger ids and the step's inputs already contain `pro_ltv`'s. **Ruling 9**: a new case-format field, `quoteAnchors[].scope` — a bare quantity name counted only where one of the scope words is named earlier in the same sentence (`cases/phase1/prof-002.json` gives `lakeport_fare` `{"names":["fare","fares"],"scope":["lakeport"]}`), with two rules: a scoped name never names the item on its own (`buildFactBase` skips it), and a modifier right before the bare name makes another quantity (break-even, target, implied, budgeted, hold). Documented in `docs/case-authoring.md`. **Ruling 2**: the four bank records on the three bare-`"Go on."` replay turns are relabelled non-replayable with wave notes (b32 Marcus t32 carries none). **Ruling 8**: the ~300 unread detector flags stay out of the bank; the confirmation is now a test, because a bare rebuild of `scripts/build-math-labels.ts` still writes 680 labels against the 372 committed. **Rulings 7, 10 and 12**: no behaviour change, as ruled — ruling 7's finding is that the worksheet binds b35q 5c3d2c21's "$600, not $400" to `step:starter_ltv`, correctly, and does not inherit the flag row's `starter_churn@7.5` key. Evidence (all free): the fault bank both arms — fault records 50 → 52 with the switches off and **73 → 79** with `*=phase1`, `false_refusal` **7/15 → 13/14**, and **0 correct records failing in either arm** (was 1); the 31-slice replay — **0 changed lines and 0 changed checker decisions with the switches off**, 12 changed lines with `*=phase1`, every one read, F1/F2-rec/F3/F4 = 0 on every slice in both arms. The one cost, by instruction: ruling 4 drops the fault record `b38l-tyler-21m-3-6m` and leaves his $3.6M for the written report (b38l F2-next 7 → 8). Measurement note carried from the combined replay: `fact-check.ts` must be run with `ENGINE_SWITCHES` **unset**, or the overlay's misquote anchors make the detector itself see more misquotes and a clean arm reads as a regression. Not done: the wave (his explicit go), and Phase 1's own body sections in this PRD — the contract, the engine switches and the case overlays are still only in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-22-phase1-contract.md` and the stream reports, and are owed when Phase 1 lands.

- **26 Oct 2026 — Phase 1's post-wave fixes, from Lorenzo's rulings on the batch-39 grade (branch `phase1` off 2a8dca76; $0 — every piece of evidence a free replay or a local read; detail in `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-22-phase1/postwave-fixes-report.md`).** Why: the hand grade of the Phase 1 wave (`Case Interview Runs/test runs/batch-39-*`, 31 runs, `ENGINE_SWITCHES='*=phase1'`) missed two of the six success bars — 2 serious faults and 12 firm stale probes — and his rulings named what to fix in each. Built: **the conduct misfire** (aldermoor p07 `35740806`) — `nothing (matters|works)` out of the `C5_DESPAIR` regex floor (his t17 was case arithmetic ending "both miss, and nothing works"; the floor fired `offer_pause` "despair", skipped the model layer and spoke the 988 script), a gate so that **after an `offer_pause` the exit path never closes the case** (an explicit accept is taken by `isPauseAccepted`, where a decline wins ties, so anything that still reads as a stop in a declining message is ambiguous: it asks the clarifying question once, at most — logged `exit_after_pause`), and `finish` as a stop verb only with an object that can only mean ending here and now ("I'd like to finish" closed his case in the EXHIBIT phase with no exhibit-2, brainstorm, recommendation or risk stage). **The stale probes** — his ruling (a) reverses the b38 Tyler t24 call, so the carried-figure leg of `staleReason` applies the same policy as the claim's own figure (a figure of the CASE still needs to be a misread they fixed; a figure of their OWN needs only the withdrawal); the figures the candidate-facing line WOULD SAY count beside the ones the claim's words carry (aldermoor p57 t8's ">2x" claim never names the $640M he withdrew — only the hint line does), which also gives ruling (c) its asymmetry, since a correction's own line names the wrong figure outright; and `lineageMoved` drops a probe or correction whose step lineage (through `dependsOn`) holds a newer standing value, unless the claim's own quantity still stands at its own figure. A latent bug the reversal exposed: the `scenario` guard's trailing `\b` applied to every branch, so "at $60" never matched. `evals/severity.md` records his grading rule — a single stale probe on a silent fix is borderline; repeated, after an explicit withdrawal, or a stale correction is firm. **The misquotes** — a new switch `misquoteAttribution` (a figure the candidate ATTRIBUTES to the interviewer is a quote by their own account, so `operandLater`, which exists for their own derived figures, no longer excuses it: aldermoor p64 t6's "Prevalence you gave me is 1.5%, so 260 million × 1.5% = …") and launch-001's `variable_costs` misquote anchor naming every component the line states (hollisbay p64 t6's "packaging $0.35" matched no anchor, because the overlay named only the ingredients). **Housekeeping** — the candidate simulator's refusal fallback in `scripts/live-run.ts` no longer pins `claude-opus-4-8` with the June beta; it takes Anthropic's recommended fallback, like every other call. That hand-pinned fallback is the whole of the wave's $0.84 of Opus 4.8 (22 calls, most of them in the 64 Tyler runs, whose brief asks the simulator to misstate figures on purpose). **Built and then kept OUT of the preset, on its own evidence: `misquoteSameTurn`** (a live misquote finding corrects on its own turn rather than a turn later behind the checker gate). It is the only way Rule 6 option 4's "one flat sentence" is spoken on a misquote the candidate self-corrects next message — code's correction is owed a turn after the message, and by then the model's Socratic question has made them fix it, so the line is stale and nothing flat ever goes. But the corpus sweep is decisive against it: corrections across the 31 slices go 52 → 95, of which 45 are sentences those runs never said, and in the base arm the gate had rated 3 of those findings CORRECT, 5 disagreeing and 19 not rated — two of the three `checker_correct` ones become spoken corrections and both are FALSE (b34a `a1133734` t31 on his own correct "$320M less $80M … is $240M"; b38l `701443f7` t17 on his own correct "$11,000/60 = $183, so a $163 fare"). It is `NON_PRESET`, nameable for evidence. **Reported, not changed: errors left at close.** The open checker flag DOES reach the judge prompt — `score-session.ts` passes `formatCheckerSection`, which emits every `flag: true` claim with the checker's right figure and what became of it, `unresolved_at_close` included — and larkspur p57 `69441a66`'s claim 29:2 is there in full (`errorType: conceptual`, `correctValue: 5.2`, the derivation naming the $1.8M double count, decision `unresolved_at_close — "time up"`). The deterministic section does not cover it (`codeQuantity.kind: none`). What is missing is any obligation to NAME it: the directive is "treat a WRONG claim as the candidate's error unless they revised it", there is no rule that an uncorrected error standing in the final recommendation must appear in the report, and the claim's own `changesConclusion` is recorded as **false** — the model's call, on a claim whose error is the recommendation's bottom line. Two proposals, awaiting his ruling. Evidence (all free): the 31-slice replay — **0 changed lines and 0 changed checker decisions with the switches off**, 14 changed lines with `*=phase1`, every one read; correction sentences **52 → 51** (0 added; the one removed is the graded stale correction `b38l-marisol-t24-stale-correction`), refusal sentences **101 → 101** (0 added, 0 removed), `fact-check` with `ENGINE_SWITCHES` unset identical on every slice (F1 0 / F2-next 101 / F2-rec 11 / F3 0 / F4 0); the fault bank — **correct records 95/95 in both arms**, fault records 74 → 77 of 153 with `*=phase1` and 48 of 154 with them off, no record escaping into a skip class; batch 39 replayed — stale lines the engine drops **7 → 17**, and of the 12 firm stale lines 1 dropped outright, 4 reclassified borderline by the new rule, **7 still firm (bar 3 still not met)**. Open for Lorenzo: four C5 lexicon alternatives that fire on ordinary case speech about the client ("Brew & Bean has had a rough few months", "What's the point of segmenting further", "I don't see the point in discounting below cost", "…why bother?") with proposed narrowings; the three remaining stale-probe widenings (a concession dropping a sibling claim dropping the message's rest; a case figure withdrawn with its replacement in the previous sentence; folding code's verified-right step bindings for the current message into the worksheet off prof-001); the misquote gate's timing; and item 4's report obligation. Note: `evals/fault-bank/specs/b39a.json` and `b39h.json` will conflict with `phase2-design`'s own b39 specs — a trivial array concatenation, no id collision. Not done: the wave (his explicit go), and Phase 1's own body sections in this PRD, still owed when Phase 1 lands.
- **26 Oct 2026 (evening) — Lorenzo's rulings on the post-wave report, applied (branch `phase1`, 3 commits to `330a0e7c`; $0 — every number a free replay or a local read; detail in `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-22-phase1/postwave-fixes-report.md` §9–§14).** Why: the entry above left him four things to rule on, and he ruled on all four. **C5 — all four floor misfires narrowed, approved (`conduct.ts` v4.13; `docs/interviewer-behavior.md` records which layer now judges what).** A gerund carve-out on "the point of/in \<verb\>ing" in both `C5_DESPAIR` and `C5_NO_POINT`, keeping the despair gerunds ("of trying", "of going on", "of carrying on", "of living", "of being here") and the `-ing`-shaped pronouns ("of anything", "of everything", "of nothing", "of something", "of this thing") on the floor; `(bad|rough|dark|awful) few (weeks|months)` and the BARE sentence-final "why bother?" moved to the model layer, whose own prompt names both shapes ("a rough few weeks" under `distress`, the analytic "what's the point of the loyalty programme" under `none`); every self-harm pattern untouched. The gate he set is a test set, since no batch-39 message is one of these shapes: 12 ordinary case sentences clean (the four client-subject ones among them), "What's the point, I'm going to fail anyway" still firing on the floor with nine other despair-shaped forms, all four first-person hardship disclosures and all eight self-harm forms still firing (the self-harm ones as `isRiskToSelf`), and the model-layer half driven through the real runner — a despair-shaped version of each narrowed phrase still gets the pause offer on a `distress` verdict, and the client-subject version reaches `classifyDistress` instead of being short-circuited by the floor. **`misquoteSameTurn` — approved on attributed quotes only, and it is now IN the `phase1` preset.** `sameTurnCorrections` (misquote.ts) is the whole restriction: the attribution phrase ("you said / gave me X", "the exhibit says X" and equivalents, a list deliberately wider than and separate from the detector's own `ATTRIBUTED`, which must keep its narrow form for `operandLater`) has to sit at or before the end of the quoted span in the same sentence, and a figure the candidate withdraws in the same message says nothing. Across the 31 slices the restricted form speaks 6 corrections against the unrestricted form's 45 — three the same sentence two turns earlier (b35q `ee99bd82`, b37h `442e37ff`, b38l `cee7d399`, all t8 → t6), three newly spoken (b32 `f59f5ea8` t8, b36a `34eb553a` t12, b38l `cee7d399` t14) — and all six quote the interviewer or the exhibit in the candidate's own words and are true misquotes; both of the afternoon's provably false corrections (b34a `a1133734` t31, b38l `701443f7` t17) are gone, and so is the unattributed Hollis Bay "packaging $0.35" one. On batch 39 it delivers the two turns the afternoon could not: **aldermoor p64 `4df38465` t6 and t10 now get Rule 6 option 4's one flat sentence**, on the misquote's own turn. **Item 4 — the recommendation turn sets `changesConclusion`, in code.** `formatCheckerSection` escalates a flagged claim whose turn states a clear recommendation (`clearRecommendation`, the predicate 4.9 uses) and whose flag nothing in the interview closed, in wording of its own ("changes the conclusion — the figure is in the recommendation itself") so code's call is never confused with the checker model's. The rule is literal: the claim's turn must BE a recommendation message. Verified against larkspur p57 `69441a66`'s stored events, where it fires on t29 alone and carries claim 29:2 at last; across the 86 stored runs of batches 32–39 it fires on 8. **The three stale widenings — measured on the free replay and reverted; all three rejected, his ruling asked for numbers only.** W1 (a concession drops the rest of its message's claims) removes 18 lines / adds 8 on b28–b38 and fixes the hollisbay p57 t14 target, but loses **2 correct-behaviour records** (`b37h-marisol-t14-true-refusal`, `b37h-marisol-t16-correction`) and misses 5 real errors. W2 ("not 12%": replacement from the preceding sentence, no case-figure requirement on a first-person withdrawal) removes 1 probe and misses 1 real error, and on its own target turn the queue takes claim 7:4 ($3.9M on Port Avery), which rests on the same withdrawn 12% — so the replay shows no net gain. W3 (fold code's verified step bindings off prof-001) is the only one that drops stale **corrections** — 2, both in the new personas, neither of the usual 25's two — and pays with the graded `b38l-marisol-t24-stale-correction` returning (pass → FAIL) and 4 real errors missed, one of them the documented `stepFlagsLive` harm itself (b32 `6a721cdf` 7:2, where "50% diagnosed = 2 million" folds over his 200,000 and kills the true probe of exactly that error). Narrowings proposed for each, none built. Evidence for what shipped (all free, 31 slices + the 5 batch-39 slices + the fault bank in both arms): **0 new false corrections** (39 → 48 correction sentences, all 12 additions read), **0 new false refusals** (107 → 107; 111 → 111 off), **0 changed checker decisions and 0 missed real errors** (150 → 150 checker lines on b28–b38, 58 → 58 on batch 39), **95/95 correct records in both arms** (fault 78/154 on, 48/155 off), **all 31 slices identical with the switches off**, `fact-check` with `ENGINE_SWITCHES` unset **F1 0 / F2-next 101 → 97 / F2-rec 11 / F3 0 / F4 0** (the four falls are misquotes now corrected on their own turn instead of standing into the next), and the **batch-39 stale count unchanged** (16 checker + 1 misquote drops, 15 `stale_drop` acts, bar 3 still not met). Open for Lorenzo: his ruling on the three widenings' numbers, and his go for item 4's 5-run re-score ($2.40–$2.60 against a $3 cap, exact commands in §13.3) plus the drafted report mandate — which I recommend narrowing before it ships, since taken literally it makes 19 needsWork items mandatory on quillpath p07 `e08c362a`. Not done: the wave (his explicit go), and Phase 1's own body sections in this PRD.

- **27 Oct 2026 — speech normalization for the arithmetic checker, built behind `speechNormalization` and NOT in the `phase1` preset (branch `phase1`, 3 commits off `7f5c4e9d`; $0 — every number a free replay or a local read; detail in `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-22-phase1/speech-normalization-report.md`).** Why: Stream 3 task 6 (26 Oct) found the arithmetic checker blind on real STT output, and Lorenzo briefed the fix with the warning that it inverts Phase 1's risk profile — every guard so far has been about not speaking wrongly, this one asks the engine to start *raising* claims on speech it ignores. **Measured first** (`scripts/voice-math-measure.ts`): over the **131 recorded voice candidate turns** in `Case Interview Runs/voice-live/` (15 sessions, all prof-001; turns read from each session's per-turn `.jsonl`, carry chains collapsed, which reproduces `session_turns` exactly where a DB dump exists) the candidate-math path parses **0 claims — 0.0 per 100 turns** against **32.3 claims / 32.1 verified correct / 0.2 flagged per 100** on the same case's 4,977 typed turns. The voice corpus is small and its rates noisy; the typed comparison and the replay below carry the weight. All 32 unparsed voice turns are listed in the report, grouped by STT pattern. **Built:** `lib/orchestrator/speech-normalize.ts`, a text pre-pass (not a second grammar) threaded through `checkCandidateMath` as a second option and read from `switchOn` at the live turn and in scoring, with four transforms, each bounded to a shape writing does not take — a disfluency run and its commas (after a figure it must carry a filler nobody writes, and the comma is kept, so "= 10, um, plus 0.75 × 37.5 = 28.1" stays two claims); a decimal spoken with no whole part ("point seven five" is 0.75, only where no figure sits directly before, and only on a maximal digit-word run); "something like" as "about", after an equals word and before a clause-closing figure; and a comma between a result-introducer and a clause-closing figure. **Result:** voice **0 → 3 claims, all three verified correct, 0 flags added** (16/42 = 38%, 375/4 ≈ 93, 58/4 = 14.5 — each read by hand), and the typed corpus **byte-identical: 0 of 6,304 candidate messages** change a claim, a flag or a verified figure. **Speech-like replay** (`scripts/speech-replay.ts`): the whole typed corpus rewritten as STT would hand it over (number words, spoken currency and scale, symbols spoken, ranges spoken, brackets gone) and replayed in three readings, so the spoken form's effect and the switch's are never confused. The spoken form alone nearly triples the flag rate (1.9 → 4.9 per 100) and multiplies wrong claims seventeenfold (0.2 → 3.5), because a share said as "point two five" loses its whole part; **with the switch on both return to the written level (flags 1.7 vs 1.9, wrong 0.3 vs 0.2): 221 false flags removed, 0 raised.** Verified-correct is **26.3 vs the written 39.9** — reported as **not** within a few points, and the shortfall is the spoken form's: currency and scale words ending the expression, "of" as multiplication, and `normalizeNumberWords` reading a lone spoken "one" as a pronoun. **Deliberately left, each with its cost in the report:** "of" as multiplication (5 true spoken claims; it fires on written text), "and you get" (43 real written uses), fraction words, a result that lands in the next sentence (3 true claims; the highest-risk join available), scale and currency words after a figure (they change a figure's magnitude 100–1,000×), an article between an operator and its operand, and every figure the candidate abandons mid-thought. **Gate:** fault bank fault **78/154** on and **48/154** off, correct **95/95** both arms, **0 records moved** against the previous round's own arms; the 31-slice replay at HEAD **0 changed decisions, 0 changed lines, 0 one-sided runs** against `nw3-gate`; `fact-check` with `env -u ENGINE_SWITCHES` **F1 0 · F2-next 97 · F2-rec 11 · F3 0 · F4 0**; suite 5,862 pass, types and lint clean. **For Lorenzo:** the switch stays out of every preset until he rules (his instruction). The report recommends pinning it on for one live voice session rather than a preset entry, because three claims on 131 turns is a small return and the commonest spoken result-introducer — a filler before a bare "is" — is still unread; adding `is`/`are`/`equals` to the reader's comma-crossing set, only where a hard filler was removed, is the one bounded change that would widen it, and was left unbuilt because the bare-"is" shape does not occur in the recordings. Nothing pushed, merged or deployed; nothing written inside the corpus.

- **27 Oct 2026 — the voice recall denominator, the three speech-normalization follow-ups, and the checker's equations (branch `phase1`, 5 commits off `c71da5a9`; $1.0138 of a $2.00 cap; detail in `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-22-phase1/voice-math-round2-report.md`).** Why: the entry above left Lorenzo three follow-ups and no measure of what the checker was missing on speech — the 27 Oct report counted only what it found (0, then 3 claims), never what there was to find. **The denominator, hand-counted over all 131 recorded voice candidate turns: 19 self-contained stated-arithmetic claims (14.5 per 100 turns, against 32.3 on the same case's 4,977 typed turns — speech states less than half as much checkable arithmetic), plus 8 context-bound and 2 borderline; 1 of the 19 is a real error.** Recall is therefore 0 → 3 (16%) → 6 (32%) across this round. **Every checker line in the recorded voice corpus, graded: 3 probes, all in session `9813d6ce` — 1 right (his "COGS increased by thirty percent overall", really +38% relative or 16 points), 2 challenges of correct work (0.75 × 37.5% = 28.1% and 25% × 40% = 10%, raised on the same turn a turn apart), 0 corrections, 0 stale lines; plus 1 false code flag (`unit:COGS@25`, raised on a spoken self-correction "25 percent of revenue, uh, 25 percent of cogs, sorry") suppressed at the gate before it was spoken.** Five of the fifteen sessions (69 turns, including the two math-heaviest) have no interviewer record of any kind, so nothing there can be graded — a gap worth closing before the next voice session. Changed: **follow-up 1** — `speechNormalization` now lets the reader cross a comma to `is` / `are` / `equals`, but only where a hard filler was removed and only in front of a figure that closes its clause (impossible in written text); **follow-up 3** — a new switch `ofAsMultiplication` reads `of` as × between a share (a percent or a fraction word) and a figure that follows immediately, with no article and no noun between, which is the one position where `of` cannot be the preposition the old rule protects; **item E** — a new switch `checkerEquations` widens the Sonnet math checker's existing per-message output with the equations it heard (operands with scale and unit, operator, stated result, the candidate's own words, and how sure it is that it *heard* the sentence right) and an answer sheet of the quantities whose value changed, each naming the figure it replaces. **Code verifies the equations itself** (`lib/orchestrator/checker-equations.ts`, `verdictFor`'s own bands), a low-confidence entry is `unclear` whatever the arithmetic says, an entry whose figures or words are not the candidate's is `unclear` with the reason, and the answer sheet drops a probe or correction on a figure it supersedes — in `vetCheckerClaims` for the same message and in plan-turn's `staleDrop` hook for a later one. **Follow-up 2 was measured only, on his ruling** (`scripts/scale-words-measure.ts`; nothing in `lib/` changed): absorbing scale and currency words recovers **0 on the recordings** (their currency sentences state no result), +602 claims and verified figures 62.0 → 76.4 per 100 on spoken text, and raises 7 flags of which **all 7 are false and 0 are real errors** — so "never a flag" costs nothing on this corpus; both readings of that rule are priced in the report (capped at `unclear` recovers nothing and collapses into `spokenMathParser`; never-a-flag-with-`correct`-allowed recovers 589 verified claims). Evidence: follow-up 1 moves the `fillers` replay arm from 2.2 to 16.6 claims and 36.1 to 51.2 verified figures per 100 and changes nothing on the recordings or in the realistic arm, with typed identity still **0 of 6,304** messages (now a run, `speech-replay --arm=identity`); follow-up 3's typed arm is **102 turns changed, 107 claims gained, every one `correct`, 0 claims lost, 0 new flags, 0 flags removed, 0 verified figures lost, all 107 read** — the condition he set for dropping typed identity, met exactly — and it adds 3 hand-verified voice claims while leaving "25 percent of COGS" in the very same sentence untouched; **b39's three remaining stale lines: 1 dropped (quillpath p07 `c82e7bd7` t26), 2 not (p24 `99dd37d1` t24, whose sheet is empty because he never restates the ratio; larkspur p57 `69441a66` t10, where the superseded figure is an input, not the claim's own — following it is lineage, which narrow W3 was rejected for)**; the standing gate unmoved (fault **78/154** on `*=phase1` and **48/155** off, correct **95/95** in both, 316 → 316 records with 0 leaving the denominator in both arms, the 31-slice replay byte-identical over 230 runs and 6,020 turns, `fact-check` **F1 0 · F2-next 97 · F2-rec 11 · F3 0 · F4 0**), 5,892 tests, types and lint clean at each commit. **Item E fails its latency gate and is not shipped: the checker's p50 goes 11,114 → 13,345 ms and its p90 15,663 → 19,899 ms — +4,236 ms against Lorenzo's 100 ms bar — and the second task in the same call also moved the model's own verdicts (firm recall 12/16 → 11/16, `fc290dc0` t20's real error going `error/medium` to `unverifiable/low`, with no code guard involved).** The asynchronous variant proposed in its place: a small parallel Haiku extractor over the message alone, read at delivery rather than at the turn, which takes it off the critical path entirely and restores the checker's prompt byte-for-byte (≈$0.07 to measure). Decisions: **all three switches are out of every preset** (`speechNormalization` by his standing instruction; `ofAsMultiplication` and `checkerEquations` added to `NON_PRESET_SWITCHES`); `ofAsMultiplication` is a switch of its own rather than part of `speechNormalization`, because it is not a speech transform and does change written output; `supersededBy` matches on the figure and never on the label, so a probe is never silenced on a quantity code only guessed at. **Proposed, awaiting his ruling:** whether `ofAsMultiplication` enters the preset (its condition is met); which reading of the scale-word rule he meant (they differ by 589 verified claims); whether to build item E's asynchronous variant. **One lapse recorded:** the shared budget ledger was seeded as `{"spentUsd":0}` where `FileLedger` expects one JSON line per call, so the cap summed to `NaN` and was not enforcing for the first three calls ($0.1340); noticed on the first run's `already spent $NaN`, repaired before anything else, and every later call checked against a real total. Nothing pushed, merged or deployed; no DB write; nothing under `Case Interview Runs/` staged.

- **27 Oct 2026 — `ofAsMultiplication` shipped into the preset, the scale-word reading shipped behind its own switch after a hand-read sample, and item E rebuilt as a parallel call that costs 0 ms (branch `phase1`, 3 commits off `63e0377a`; $0.0448 of a $0.10 cap; detail in `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-22-phase1/voice-math-round3-report.md`).** Why: the entry above left Lorenzo three questions and he ruled on all three. **(1) `ofAsMultiplication` is in `PHASE1_SWITCHES`** on its own evidence (107 claims gained over 6,304 typed messages, every one `correct`, 0 lost, 0 new flags; the recordings 3 → 6), and the standing gate **did not move by one number** — fault **78/154** on and **48/155** off, correct **95/95** both arms, `run_missing` 0, no record into a skip class, the 31-slice replay **0 changed decisions / 0 changed lines / 0 one-sided** over 230 runs and 6,020 turns, `fact-check` with `env -u ENGINE_SWITCHES` **F1 0 · F2-next 97 · F2-rec 11 · F3 0 · F4 0**. The switch's whole typed effect is new `correct` claims and new verified figures, and across those 31 slices none met a checker claim or a step flag, so nothing a candidate can see changed; the gain is live-side. A contract test now asserts the preset carries it and carries neither `speechNormalization` nor `checkerEquations`. **(2) The scale-word reading ships as `scaleWordReading`, out of every preset, and only after the sample he required**: 50 of the 589 newly verified claims, drawn across all 5 cases, 36 personas and all 7 reading classes (the corpus holds no `billion` reading), each recomputed by hand — **50/50 verify right work, 0 verify wrong work**; behind it, a global check of all 589 shows **585 differ from the single reading only by an exact power of ten** (the scale factor itself) and the 4 that do not were read too, all four right. `lib/orchestrator/scale-words.ts` **is** the pre-pass the 27 Oct measurement simulated, unchanged (`scale-words-measure.ts` imports it), so what ships is what was measured and sampled — verified claim by claim, 589 = 589. His rule is enforced by construction: a second pass deduped against the first (0 claims lost, 0 verdicts moved), `incorrect` → `unclear` on any absorbed claim (so `flagsFromArithmetic` can never raise one), an absorbed claim barred from silencing a step flag (a false `correct` shielding a real error is the same harm backwards), and the claim's span kept as the candidate's own sentence so the absorbed text never reaches a prompt as a quote. Measured: spoken **602 claims gained (589 `correct`, 13 `unclear`, 0 `incorrect`)**, written **146, every one `correct`**, with **0 lost, 0 new flags, 0 flags removed, 0 verified figures lost** in both, and 0 change on the 131 recorded voice turns. Known gap, pinned as a test: a figure carrying both a scale and a currency word ("8 thousand dollars"). **(3) Item E's asynchronous variant is built and passes** (`checkerEquationsAsync`, `lib/orchestrator/equation-extractor.ts`): a background classifier (Haiku 5.5) over the candidate's own words with no case in the prompt, fired in parallel from `startMathCheck`, failing open. **The checker's whole request is byte-identical to today's with the switch on — a test asserts the serialized request, schema and settings included — so the synchronous form's recall regression (12/16 → 11/16) cannot occur in this form, with no paid arm needed to show it.** The latency question is **not** added turn time (nothing awaits the job; verdicts are read from stored rows a turn later) but when the verdict becomes available: over the same 16 labelled messages, arrival p50/p90/max is **11,114 / 15,663 / 16,932 ms today, 13,345 / 19,899 / 22,976 synchronous, and 11,114 / 15,663 / 16,932 asynchronous — 0 ms added at p50, p90 and max against a 100 ms bar**, because the extractor (p50 2,563 / p90 3,992 ms) is faster than the checker on all 16. Against the **115 real candidate-turn gaps** of the recorded voice sessions, verdicts landing before the next turn are **89.5% today, 85.5% synchronous, 89.5% asynchronous** — the widening would have pushed 4.0 points of verdicts past the turn they could act on. Two findings the run forced, both fixed: **`supersedes` names a figure from an EARLIER message**, so a message-only extractor cannot fill it (a Haiku and a Sonnet extractor both left it empty for the quantity b39's stale line hung on) — the extractor now gets the candidate's own last 8 messages, still no case data, and with them it drops `c82e7bd7` t26 on the right entry and leaves the other two, **§E.2's 1-of-3 exactly**; and **`supersededBy` was matching figures without their units**, dropping a probe on the candidate's "1.4x" against an entry superseding "$1.4m net" — the match now carries each figure's kind ($ / % / x) and explicit scale and refuses a disagreeing pair, since a false drop is a missed real error. Also, in the safe direction only, `verifyEquation` gained the two readings the deterministic reader already allows (a percent operand as a growth factor → `correct`; a relative change transcribed as a difference → `unclear`): **5 of the extractor run's equations were rated `incorrect` and 3 of the 5 were false**, every one of the three one of those shapes, and the two real errors stay `incorrect` — all five pinned as tests, and (a) is still wired to nothing live, which this is the reason for. Gate at HEAD and at each commit: fault **78/154** on / **48/155** off, correct **95/95** both, 31 slices byte-identical (three consecutive compares, 230 runs, 6,020 turns), `fact-check` **F1 0 · F2-next 97 · F2-rec 11 · F3 0 · F4 0**, 5,928 tests, types and lint clean. The budget ledger was created empty and summed before the first call and before every later paid command (a real number each time), so round 2's `NaN` lapse is not repeated. **Proposed, awaiting his ruling:** whether `scaleWordReading` enters the preset (he approved the reading, not the preset); whether the asynchronous variant enters a preset or runs behind `ENGINE_SWITCHES` for one wave first (its answer sheet is a Haiku transcription evaluated on 4 messages); and whether the equations half (a) is wired to anything. Nothing pushed, merged or deployed; no DB write; nothing under `Case Interview Runs/` staged.

- **27 Oct 2026 — the safe direction of the checker's "equations heard", built behind `equationClears` and then measured and REJECTED (branch `phase1`, 2 commits off `67d7979f`; $0.1608 of a $0.25 cap; detail in `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-22-phase1/equations-safe-direction-report.md`).** Why: the entry above left half (a) of item E wired to nothing, and Lorenzo ruled the one direction he thought safe — "a correct equation may clear a false flag or block a doubt probe, but only if code confirms every operand and the result appear in the candidate's own words (number words or digits)". Built exactly that, behind its own switch and out of every preset: the model's entry is a pointer to a span of his message and never an authority on it, so `confirmedInOwnWords` re-reads his own text at the edge — the quoted span must be a verbatim substring, and every operand and the stated result must appear as a figure of the message (through `normalizeNumberWords`, so "twenty eight point one" is 28.1) at a 1e-6 tolerance, as said or at its scale. `clearedByEquation` returns a clear or null and has no path that raises a flag, writes a correction or marks a figure correct; it refuses any verdict that is not `correct`, refuses an entry code RESCUED off `incorrect` (the 1.10 the rescue reads for his "10%" is not a figure he said) and refuses an unconfirmed entry, and it clears in two shapes only, both in the doubted clause's own breath — the equation's result IS the doubted figure, or its result is code's own right figure while the doubted figure is one of its operands. Two hooks, both drops: `vetCheckerClaims` blocks a doubt probe on the message's own verdict, and `gateCodeFindings` drops a code line when it would be released a turn later (new outcome `equation_cleared`), for which the equations now ride on the verdict row; every gate clear is logged (`equation_clear`) with the equation, his words and code's verdict, and the claim side in the claim's `vetNotes`. **The measurement says it shields real errors, so it does not ship and must not be turned on for a wave.** On the speech-like corpus (the typed corpus as STT hands it over; 100 messages carrying 108 code flags) it makes **21 clears, of which 19 are real errors** — 10 `unit:COGS` flags ("25% of COGS up 40% is 10% of COGS, so 10 points of revenue": right arithmetic, and 10% of COGS is 4.2 points) and 9 `pricing:gain` flags ("COGS 58% → 53%, five points of margin back": right subtraction, and the margin gain is 8.5 points because labor and overhead compress too) — and only **2 false doubts cleared**, one on sound evidence (a step flag that bound his two-years-ago revenue per store, 417.4/200 = 2.087, to today's step) and one on a digit coincidence (180 thousand passengers cleared by his $180 fare — `clearedByEquation` matches a figure's value and not its kind, the same failure `supersededBy` was tightened against earlier the same day). On the corpus's own recorded flagged checker claims (508 such messages; 120 sampled systematically, 211 flagged claims re-vetted with the same context on both sides) it **blocks 88 of 211 — 42% of every doubt the engine was about to speak** — by the checker's own error type conceptual 29, misquote 28, wrong_base 16, wrong_period 9, arithmetic 4, unit 2; the **10 read verbatim are all real errors**, including Marisol's "$10,000 list, discounts 20% of list, so the realized figure is $2,000" (the net price is $8,000) and the two figures built on it. **The reason is structural and is the finding worth keeping: a doubt is almost never about whether the arithmetic holds but about WHICH QUANTITY the figure is — the base, the unit, the year, a misquoted input — and in exactly those errors the candidate's arithmetic is internally correct.** "Right arithmetic on the wrong base" is the commonest real error this engine catches (round 2 §A.1 found it in the recordings themselves), so a correct equation in his own words is that error's signature rather than evidence against the doubt. Narrowing a clear to doubts the checker itself types `arithmetic` leaves 4 of the 88 and all four are real errors too; narrowing the code side to `arithmetic`-kind flags leaves the one coincidence. **On the voice corpus it changes nothing**, the three graded probes of round 2 §A.2 included: the two that doubted correct work sit on a message that states results and no working (the extractor's entries have zero operands, so they are `unclear` and unconfirmed), probe #2's operands are in his answer a turn LATER and probe #3's 40% is the exhibit's figure, not his words; and the false code flag `unit:COGS@25` is not cleared either (its 10.5 is an operand of a correct equation in the next clause, not its result) — it stays suppressed at the gate as before. Gate, all with the switch out of every preset: fault **78/154** on `*=phase1` and **48/155** off, correct **95/95** both arms, `run_missing` 0 and `316 → 316 records · 0 moved · 0 left the denominator` against round 3's own labels in both arms; the 31-slice replay **0 changed checker decisions / 0 changed interviewer lines / 0 one-sided** over 230 runs and 6,020 turns; `fact-check` with `env -u ENGINE_SWITCHES` **F1 0 · F2-next 104 · F2-rec 11 · F3 0 · F4 0**, identical on both sides; 5,949 tests, types and lint clean. **The baseline for that replay is a run of the same proof at `67d7979f`, not round 3's label, and that is itself a finding:** the two commits that landed inline on this branch just before this work (`34818edc`, `67d7979f`) move **1,308 interviewer lines and 153 checker decisions** across those 31 slices with no switches set, and F2-next 103 → 104; most of what I sampled is acknowledgment-phrase rotation but at least one is a code-written data line disappearing (b28 54-owen t10). They are left alone, and the numbers are flagged for whoever owns them; the fault bank is unmoved by them. Decisions: **`equationClears` is in `NON_PRESET_SWITCHES` with its measurement recorded beside it, and the code is kept — off everywhere — exactly as `checkerEquations` was kept after failing its latency gate.** The budget ledger was created empty and summed before the first call (`lines 0, sum 0, isFinite true`) and re-summed before the second paid arm. **Proposed, awaiting his ruling:** confirm the switch stays off everywhere or say to revert it; and two separable fixes this surfaced — the unit check has no `statedValid` counterpart (which is why the `unit:COGS@25` false flag exists at all), and any figure match of this kind wants the kind-and-scale guard `supersededBy` got. Nothing pushed, merged or deployed; no DB write; nothing under `Case Interview Runs/` staged.
